# Emulator ledger rules at PV11: inventory, gaps and phases – design

Status: implemented, phases 0 to 6, merged to master on 2026-11-04 (14 commits, 3ff06695e to
967100881). Not ported: `nonMyopic` pool ranking (D103). Date: 2026-11-02.

## 1. Purpose and scope

The Scalus Emulator behaves like a small Cardano node for blocks, the mempool and forks (spec `2026-10-21-emulator-chain-design.md`). Its ledger runs only the transaction rules, and its only epoch rule moves donations into the treasury. So a dApp test can pass in the Emulator and fail on a real node, and some realistic scenarios cannot be tested at all. Examples:

- A PlutusV3 tx that spends a UTxO and also reads it as a reference input passes in the Emulator. At PV11 the node rejects it (section 5.4, `[TX-1]`).
- A vote on a gov action that does not exist passes in the Emulator. The node rejects it with `GovActionsDoNotExist` (section 5.8).
- A stake credential delegated to a pool never earns a reward. Tests use `addRewards` instead (section 6).
- A retired pool is never removed, and its deposit is never refunded (section 6).

This spec does four things:

1. It lists every Cardano ledger rule that applies at PV11 (Conway), with its source (section 4).
2. It compares each rule and each predicate failure with Scalus (sections 5 and 6).
3. It ranks the gaps by their impact on dApp tests (section 7).
4. It gives the data model (section 8), the normative rules (sections 9 to 15) and the phases (section 16). The phases are the work plan. There is no separate plan document.

In scope: the tx rules (LEDGER and below), the block body rules (BBODY), and the epoch rules (TICK, NEWEPOCH, EPOCH, SNAP, POOLREAP, RUPD, RATIFY, ENACT, HARDFORK).

Out of scope:

- Consensus: OCERT, OVERLAY, PRTCL, TICKN, UPDN, TICKF, nonces and the leader schedule (section 17).
- PV12 (Dijkstra) rules and Plutus V4.
- An N2C socket server and a random block schedule (chain spec section 18).

## 2. Definitions

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| ledger | The Haskell `cardano-ledger` at tag `cardano-ledger-conway-1.22.1.0`, the pin of mainnet node 11.0.1. |
| PV11 | Protocol version with major 11. It is the highest Conway version (`libs/cardano-ledger-core/internal/Cardano/Ledger/Internal/Definition/Era.hs:132`). |
| Emulator | `EmulatorBase` and its JVM and JS subclasses, and `ImmutableEmulator`. |
| tx rules | The Scalus validators and mutators in `R/` that `EmulatorBase.processTransaction` runs. |
| epoch | `slotConfig.epochOf(slot)`. |
| boundary n | The first tick into epoch n. "At boundary n" means "during the transition from epoch n − 1 to epoch n". |
| epoch transition | One run of NEWEPOCH for one boundary. |
| epoch state | The new per-snapshot state of section 8.2: account state, snapshots, blocks made, pending reward update, pending ratify result, previous pparams. |
| account state | Treasury and reserves, the Haskell `ChainAccountState`. |
| snapshot (stake) | One of mark, set, go: the stake per credential, the delegations and the pool params at one boundary. Not the `LedgerSnapshot` of the chain spec, which this spec calls "ledger snapshot". |
| gov state | Proposals, committee, constitution and the four enacted roots (section 8.1). |
| performance model | `PoolPerformance.Ideal` or `PoolPerformance.Measured` (section 14). |
| matters for tests | A gap that lets a dApp test pass in the Emulator and fail on a node, or that makes a realistic scenario impossible to test. |
| faithful, low value | A gap that changes only the name or the number of reported failures, or a rare edge case. |
| consensus-only | A rule that checks consensus data that the Emulator does not produce. Skip. |

Path prefixes. Ledger, relative to `../cardano-ledger`:

- `C/` = `eras/conway/impl/src/Cardano/Ledger/Conway/`
- `B/` = `eras/babbage/impl/src/Cardano/Ledger/Babbage/`
- `A/` = `eras/alonzo/impl/src/Cardano/Ledger/Alonzo/`
- `S/` = `eras/shelley/impl/src/Cardano/Ledger/Shelley/`
- `K/` = `libs/cardano-ledger-core/src/Cardano/Ledger/`

Scalus, relative to the repo root:

- `R/` = `scalus-cardano-ledger/shared/src/main/scala/scalus/cardano/ledger/rules/`
- `L/` = `scalus-cardano-ledger/shared/src/main/scala/scalus/cardano/ledger/`
- `N/` = `scalus-cardano-ledger/shared/src/main/scala/scalus/cardano/node/`
- `SC/` = `scalus-core/shared/src/main/scala/scalus/cardano/ledger/`

## 3. Method

- Every ledger claim was read from source at the pin. The ledger checkout is at `226b002d5`, tag `cardano-ledger-conway-1.22.1.0`.
- Every Scalus claim was read at `416d76361` (master at the time of writing).
- The full research notes, with more line references, are outside the repo in the shared notes folder of this session: `ledger-utxo-inventory.md`, `ledger-certs-gov-inventory.md`, `ledger-epoch-block-inventory.md`, `scalus-tx-rules.md`, `scalus-state-emulator.md`.

Five PV gates differ between PV10 and PV11 (`C/Era.hs:174-202`, `S/Era.hs:157-160`):

| Gate | Condition | Effect at PV11 |
|---|---|---|
| `hardforkConwayBootstrapPhase` | major = 9 | false: no bootstrap relaxations |
| `hardforkConwayDisallowUnelectedCommitteeFromVoting` | major > 10 | GOV raises `UnelectedCommitteeVoters` |
| `hardforkConwayDELEGIncorrectDepositsAndRefunds` | major > 10 | DELEG raises `DepositIncorrectDELEG`, `RefundIncorrectDELEG` |
| `hardforkConwayMoveWithdrawalsAndDRepChecksToLedgerRule` | major > 10 | withdrawal checks, drain and DRep expiry updates run in LEDGER, before CERTS |
| `hardforkConwayDisallowDuplicatedVRFKeys` | major > 10 | POOL raises `VRFKeyHashAlreadyRegistered` |

Other PV11 behaviour: PlutusV3 TxInfo rejects common inputs and reference inputs (`C/TxInfo.hs:488-493`); `ppuWellFormed` rejects `nOpt = 0` (`C/PParams.hs:951-952`); BBODY counts reference scripts of earlier txs in the block (`C/Rules/Bbody.hs:369-382`); the HARDFORK rule into PV11 fills `psVRFKeyHashes` (`C/Rules/HardFork.hs:77-78`).

| LEDGERS | `S/Rules/Ledgers.hs:193-202` | – | ledger state | `LedgerFailure` wrapper | I: `N/EmulatorState.scala:289-305` folds txs | – |

| DELEG | `C/Rules/Deleg.hs:180-304` | accounts, pools, DReps | accounts, reverse delegations | 8, section 5.5 | P: `DRepState.delegates` never written (`R/CertsMutator.scala:210-214`) | T |

| GOV | `C/Rules/Gov.hs:456-643` | proposals, cert state, committee, constitution | proposals, votes | 20, section 5.8 | M: only the deposit (`R/ProposalDepositValidator.scala:11`) | T |
| TICK | `S/Rules/Tick.hs:260-279` | all | all | none (`Void`) | P: only donation move (`N/EmulatorBase.scala:608-621`) | T |
| NEWEPOCH | `C/Rules/NewEpoch.hs:165-192` | epoch state | epoch state | none | M | T |
| EPOCH | `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:276-379` | all | all | none | P: only step E9 donations | T |
| SNAP | `S/Rules/Snap.hs:76-103` | UTxO, accounts, pools | snapshots, fee snapshot | none | M (`L/StakeDistribution.scala` is not SNAP, section 6) | T |
| POOLREAP | `S/Rules/PoolReap.hs:132-229` | pools, accounts | pools, accounts, treasury, deposited | none | M (`R/CertsMutator.scala:144-146`) | T |

| reward application | `S/LedgerState/IncrementalStake.hs:95-169` | accounts | accounts, treasury, reserves, fees | none | M | T |

| TICKF | `C/Rules/Tickf.hs:60-92` | – | forecast | none | N/A | X |

MIR, UPEC, NEWPP, PPUP and DELEGS are `VoidEraRule` in Conway (`C/Era.hs:58-86`). So no MIR exists, and only the reward update changes the reserves.

**Why** the epoch rules have no failures: TICK, NEWEPOCH, EPOCH, SNAP, POOLREAP, RUPD, RATIFY, ENACT and HARDFORK all have `Void` predicate failures (`S/Rules/Tick.hs:106`, `C/Rules/NewEpoch.hs:120`, `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:182`, `S/Rules/Snap.hs:63`, `S/Rules/PoolReap.hs:110`, `S/Rules/Rupd.hs:90`, `C/Rules/Ratify.hs:101`, `C/Rules/Enact.hs:75`, `C/Rules/HardFork.hs:63`). Their gaps are wrong state, not missing rejections.

## 5. Gap table: tx and block predicate failures

Status: I = implemented, P = partial, M = missing, N/A = not raised at PV11 or not applicable. Phase refers to section 16. The tables and the prose of sections 5 to 7 record the gap at design time; the *Implementation status* note of each rule records what a phase landed.

General difference: the ledger collects every failure (`libs/small-steps/src/Control/State/Transition/Extended.hs:703-721`). Scalus stops at the first failure and bundles several constructors into one exception (`R/Entities.scala:130-139`; `L/Entities.scala:8-515`). Pass or fail is the same. This is `[FL-1]`, phase 6, section 9.3.

### 5.2 UTXOW (`C/Rules/Utxow.hs:77-131`)

All 17 raised constructors are implemented. Evidence: `R/VerifiedSignaturesInWitnessesValidator.scala:10`, `R/MissingKeyHashesValidator.scala:12`, `R/MissingOrExtraScriptHashesValidator.scala:12`, `R/NativeScriptsValidator.scala:12`, `R/ExactSetOfRedeemersValidator.scala:12`, `R/MissingRequiredDatumsValidator.scala:12`, `R/ProtocolParamsViewHashesMatchValidator.scala:15`, `R/ScriptsWellFormedValidator.scala:13`, `R/MetadataValidator.scala:12`. *Implementation status (phase 6, design):* correction to the `OutputTooBigUTxO` (value size) row, which said P. The ledger serializes the value with the major PV (`serialize (pvMajor protVer) v`, `A/Rules/Utxo.hs:444`), but the `MaryValue` encoder has no version branch: a bare coin when there is no multi-asset, else `[coin, multiAsset]` (`eras/mary/impl/src/Cardano/Ledger/Mary/Value.hs:334-342`); only the decoder branches, at PV 9 and 12 (`:314-318`). The Scalus encoder writes the same two shapes (`SC/Value.scala:110-119`). So the size is the same at every PV, and no gap exists. The TODO at `R/OutputsHaveTooBigValueStorageSizeValidator.scala:40` becomes a comment that cites the two encoders; there is no test, since no behaviour changes (D101). Superseded in part by the next note (D115): a map of 256 or more entries differs by its head bytes, so "no gap" holds only for the PV.

*Implementation status (phase 6, value-size-and-header):* correction to the note above. The PV does not change the size, but the two encoders differ in one way. From version 2 on, the ledger's Map encoder writes a map of more than 23 entries with an indefinite length: a 1-byte head and a 1-byte break (`libs/cardano-ledger-binary/src/Cardano/Ledger/Binary/Encoding/Encoder.hs:395-425`). `MultiAsset` derives this encoder for both of its maps (`Mary/Value.hs:154-155`). The Scalus encoder writes a definite head: 2 bytes for 24 to 255 entries, 3 bytes for 256 to 65 535, 5 bytes above. So for each map of 256 or more entries, the Scalus size was 1 byte (3 bytes above 65 535) larger than the ledger's. `OutputsHaveTooBigValueStorageSizeValidator.ledgerSerializedSize` now subtracts these bytes, and the exception reports the ledger size (D115). The TODO is now the doc comment of `ledgerSerializedSize`, and the D101 shapes are tested. *Example, normative* (`OutputsHaveTooBigValueStorageSizeValidatorTest`): one policy of 256 assets with 2-byte names and quantity 1, with coin 2 000 000, is 1 + 5 + 1 + 30 + 2 + 256 × 4 = 1 063 bytes in the ledger and 1 064 in Scalus CBOR. At `maxValueSize = 1 063` the output passes; at 1 062 it fails with size 1 063. A value of 255 assets has the same size in both encoders.

### 5.4 UTXOS (`C/Rules/Utxos.hs:72-83`)

| Failure | Ledger | Scalus | Status | Phase |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ValidationTagMismatch` (FailedUnexpectedly) | `B/Rules/Utxos.hs:184-191` | `R/PlutusScriptsTransactionMutator.scala:54-64` | I | – |
| `ValidationTagMismatch` (PassedUnexpectedly) | `B/Rules/Utxos.hs:255-260` | `R/PlutusScriptsTransactionMutator.scala`, `PassedUnexpectedlyException` | I | 6 (`[FL-9]`) |
| `CollectErrors` `NoRedeemer`, `NoCostModel` | `A/Plutus/Evaluate.hs:185-188` | `L/PlutusScriptEvaluator.scala` `collectErrors`, `CollectErrorsException` | I | 6 (`[FL-10]`) |
| `CollectErrors` V1/V2 context errors (13 constructors, `C/TxInfo.hs:142-180`) | `B/TxInfo.hs:118-322`, `C/TxInfo.hs:317-734` | `SC/LedgerToPlutusTranslation.scala`; the 25 IT vectors "Conway features fail in Plutus V1/V2" pass | I | – |
| `CollectErrors` `ReferenceInputsNotDisjointFromInputs` | `C/TxInfo.hs:488-493`, PlutusV3 at PV ≥ 11 | `SC/LedgerToPlutusTranslation.scala` `guardReferenceInputsDisjointForPlutusV3` | I | 0 (`[TX-1]`), 6 (`[FL-10]`) |

*Implementation status (phase 6, distinct-plutus-errors):* each context error of the table is a `CollectErrorsException` with one `BadTranslation`, and its message starts with the ledger `ContextError` constructor and a colon, for example `CertificateNotSupported: RegDRepCert not supported in Plutus V1/V2 contexts. Use Plutus V3.` (D114). The prefixed constructors are `ByronTxOutInContext`, `RedeemerPointerPointsToNothing`, `InlineDatumsNotSupported`, `CertificateNotSupported`, `PlutusPurposeNotSupported`, `VotingProceduresFieldNotSupported`, `ProposalProceduresFieldNotSupported`, `TreasuryDonationFieldNotSupported`, `CurrentTreasuryFieldNotSupported` and `ReferenceInputsNotDisjointFromInputs`. The PlutusV1 check fails on the first output that `transTxOutV1` rejects: inputs, then reference inputs, then outputs (`C/TxInfo.hs:405-412`), and per output the inline datum before the address (`:315-321`). Earlier it gave one message for both constructors. The PlutusV3 TxInfo translates the validity interval, the inputs and the reference inputs before the `ReferenceInputsNotDisjointFromInputs` check (`C/TxInfo.hs:480-493`), so a Byron input that is also a reference input fails as `ByronTxOutInContext`. Four constructors are not raised: `ReferenceInputsNotSupported` and `ReferenceScriptsNotSupported` are checks of the Babbage PlutusV1 TxInfo only (`B/TxInfo.hs:117-118, 322`), which the Conway instance does not run (`C/TxInfo.hs:399-434`); `TimeTranslationPastHorizon` cannot happen, because `SlotConfig` has no horizon; `TranslationLogicMissingInput` is the `[FL-11]` (a) difference (D109). `PlutusPurposeNotSupported` is shadowed in practice: a Voting or Proposing purpose needs a non-empty voting or proposal field, and the field guard of the TxInfo fails first. The `NonFatal` case of the evaluation in `R/PlutusScriptsTransactionMutator.scala` stays an `IllegalArgumentException`; after `[FL-5]` only the missing datum of a PlutusV1 or PlutusV2 spend reaches it, which `UnspendableUTxONoDatumHash` rejects in phase 1 (`A/UTxO.hs:184-188`, `A/Rules/Utxow.hs:237-245`). Tests: `PlutusScriptsTransactionMutatorTest`, one per constructor that Conway raises from a V1/V2 TxInfo, two for the PlutusV1 order, and `ReferenceInputsNotDisjointFromInputs`; `ByronAddressInScriptContextTest`; `LedgerFailuresImpTest` for `RedeemerPointerPointsToNothing`; the zero-script `PassedUnexpectedly` case is `LedgerFailuresImpTest` "Valid transaction marked as invalid".

### 5.5 DELEG (`C/Rules/Deleg.hs:106-114`)

All 7 constructors raised at PV11 are implemented, bundled into one exception (`R/StakeCertificatesValidator.scala:152`). `IncorrectDepositDELEG` is not raised at PV11 (`C/Era.hs:184-185`). Gap: the vectors are not run (`[CV-1]`). State gap: `DRepState.delegates` is never written (`R/CertsMutator.scala:210-214`), phase 1. The pool delegator set (`spsDelegators`, `K/State/CertState.hs:281-298`) has no Scalus field and no phase: no rule reads it, the vector decoder discards it, and D5 computes the stake from the accounts (D22).

### 5.8 GOV (`C/Rules/Gov.hs:180-221`)

At design time, Scalus has no GOV rule (section 11 records what phase 2 landed). It checks only the deposit (`R/ProposalDepositValidator.scala:11`) and the voter witnesses (`L/utils/AllNeededKeyHashes.scala:77`). It stores no proposal and no vote.

| Failure | Ledger | Scalus | Status | Phase |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TooManyExUnits` | `A/Rules/Bbody.hs:245-249` | forge limit, `N/emulator/BlockForge.scala:77-82` | I | – |
| `WrongBlockBodySizeBBODY`, `InvalidBodyHashBBODY` | `A/Rules/Bbody.hs:201-223` | forge builds the header from the body (chain spec `[BH-3]`, `[BH-4]`) | I by construction | – |
| `BodyRefScriptsSizeTooBig` | `C/Rules/Bbody.hs:347-355, 369-382` | none | **M** | 0 (`[BB-1]`) |
| `HeaderProtVerTooHigh` | `C/Rules/Bbody.hs:320-346` | forge copies the PV from pparams (chain spec `[BH-7]`) | N/A | – |
| `BlockSizeTooLargeCHAIN` | `Chain.hs:73-75` | forge limit | I | – |
| `HeaderSizeTooLargeCHAIN` | `Chain.hs:70-72` | mock header of real field sizes, about 900 bytes | X (D102, section 17) | – |
| `ObsoleteNodeCHAIN` | `Chain.hs:69` | – | X | – |

*Implementation status (phase 6, value-size-and-header):* `BlockSizeTooLargeCHAIN` needs no new code. The forge measures the encoded body with `BlockForge.bodySize`, which equals the `blockBodySize` field that the CHAIN check reads (`Chain.hs:73-75`). The boundary is tested. `BlockForgeTest` "the body size limit holds for the encoded body of the whole prefix" takes 2 txs at the size of 2 and 1 tx at one byte less. `EmulatorForkTest` "produceBlock(on, ...) rejects txs over the block limits" rejects 2 txs when the limit is the size of 1. One known difference stays: when `maxBlockBodySize` is below the size of one tx, the automatic forge still takes the first mempool tx (chain spec `[FG-4]`, M2 status), and a node never forges it. Only a ParameterChange to such a value makes this reachable. For `HeaderSizeTooLargeCHAIN` (D102), `BlockForgeTest` "a forged header stays within the mainnet maxBlockHeaderSize at its largest fields" forges a header at the largest height and slot and checks it against the mainnet limit of 1 100 bytes.

## 6. Gap table: state and data

| Data a rule needs | Ledger | Scalus today | Needed by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Reserves | `K/State/ChainAccountState` `casReserves` | none | RUPD, reward application |
| Treasury as state | `casTreasury` | `UtxoEnv.treasury` only (`R/Entities.scala:44-49`) | EPOCH, ENACT, POOLREAP |
| Stake snapshots mark, set, go, fee | `K/State/SnapShots.hs:187-465` | none; `L/StakeDistribution.scala:30` counts UTxO stake only, ignores reward balances and registration | SNAP, RUPD, RATIFY |
| Blocks made per pool | `nesBprev`, `nesBcur` | none; forged `issuerVkey` is fixed bytes (`N/emulator/BlockForge.scala:43`) | RUPD |
| Reward update | `nesRu` | none; `L/RewardsCalculation.scala` is a pro-rata split in `Double`, used only by its test | reward application |
| Previous pparams | `cgsPrevPParams` | none | RUPD |
| Exact rationals for rho, tau, a0 | `UnitInterval`, `NonNegativeInterval` | `Double` (`SC/ProtocolParams.scala:32,33,40`) | RUPD |
| Proposals with votes | `C/Governance/Proposals.hs` | `GovState = Array[Element]`, always empty (`L/Entities.scala:576`) | GOV, RATIFY, EPOCH |
| Committee, constitution, enacted roots | `C/Governance/Internal.hs:131-142` | none | GOV, GOVCERT, RATIFY, ENACT |
| Committee state | `K/State/CertState.hs:302-330` | commented out (`L/Entities.scala:630`) | GOVCERT, GOV, RATIFY |
| Dormant epochs | `vsNumDormantEpochs` | commented out (`L/Entities.scala:631`) | GOVCERT, LEDGER, EPOCH |
| VRF key map | `psVRFKeyHashes` | none | POOL, POOLREAP |
| `ProtocolParamUpdate` applied to pparams | `C/PParams.hs` `applyPPUpdates` | no `applyTo` (`SC/ProtocolParamUpdate.scala:13`) | ENACT |
| Mutable pparams | gov state | fixed at construction in `Context.env.params` | ENACT |
| Seeded DRep expiry | – | `expiry = 0L` (`N/EmulatorBase.scala:779`) | DRep expiry |

## 7. Impact on dApp tests

### 7.1 Matters for tests, ranked

| Rank | Gap | Effect on a test | Phase |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | No GOV rule, no gov state | Any vote and any proposal pass. Proposal deposits vanish. A governance dApp cannot test a proposal lifecycle at all. | 2, 5 |
| 2 | No rewards, no snapshots, no reserves | A staking dApp never sees a real reward. `addRewards` is the only way to fund an account. Liquid-staking and reward-sharing logic cannot be tested end to end. | 3, 4 |
| 3 | No POOLREAP, no future pool params | A retired pool stays registered. Its deposit never returns. A pool update never takes effect. Delegations to a retired pool stay. | 3 |
| 4 | PlutusV3 input and reference-input overlap at PV11 | A tx passes in the Emulator and fails on mainnet with `CollectErrors`. This affects a common pattern: spend a script UTxO that carries its own reference script. | 0 |
| 5 | No RATIFY, no ENACT | Treasury withdrawals, parameter changes, committee updates and hard forks never happen. Tests of a guardrail script or a treasury dApp stop at the proposal. | 5 |
| 6 | DRep expiry and dormancy wrong | A seeded DRep has expiry 0. Votes do not extend expiry. Ratify results would be wrong once phase 5 lands. | 1 |
| 7 | Missing tx checks: pool metadata hash size, duplicate VRF key, committee certs, block ref-script total | Txs pass that a node rejects. | 0, 1, 2 |

### 7.2 Faithful, low value

- Collect all failures and report Haskell constructor names (`[FL-1]` to `[FL-11]`, section 9.3).
- PV-dependent value size (`R/OutputsHaveTooBigValueStorageSizeValidator.scala:40`). *Decided (phase 6 design):* no PV switch, the size does not depend on the PV (section 5.3 note, D101). *Done (phase 6):* a map of 256 or more entries is measured with the ledger's indefinite head (D115).
- Distinct errors for `PassedUnexpectedly` and `CollectErrors` (`[FL-9]`, `[FL-10]`).
- `nonMyopic` pool ranking (`S/PoolRank.hs:126-175`). It changes no balance. *Decided (phase 6 design):* not kept (D103).
  *Implementation status (phase 6):* the implement stage read the source again and kept D103; no code changed. The ledger stores each sample as `LogWeight = Float` (`S/PoolRank.hs:88`). `toLogWeight` and `likelihood` compute a `Double` `log` and round it to `Float` (`:93`, `:137-171`). `leaderProbability` uses a `Double` `**` (`:126-132`). `applyDecay` multiplies by `decayFactor = 0.9 :: Float` (`:175-178`, `S/LedgerState/PulsingReward.hs:308-309`), `updateNonMyopic` applies it (`S/LedgerState/PulsingReward.hs:311-330`), and `<>` adds and normalises in `Float` (`S/PoolRank.hs:110-124`). A port must therefore give float32 results and a `log` and `pow` that match the GHC `libm` results bit for bit, on the JVM and in JS. `Math.log` and `Math.pow` do not guarantee this. A later port is checked on the NEWEPOCH dumps (element 4 of `EpochState`), as D103 says.
- Header size check. *Decided (phase 6 design):* consensus-only, section 7.3 (D102).
- The pparams "point of no return" and pulsing schedules (section 12, decision D3).
- HARDFORK into PV10 (`updateDRepDelegations`). The Emulator starts at PV11.

### 7.3 Consensus-only, skip

OCERT, OVERLAY, PRTCL, TICKN, UPDN, TICKF, `ObsoleteNodeCHAIN`, `HeaderSizeTooLargeCHAIN` (D102), nonces, leader schedule, KES. Section 17 lists them.

## 8. Data model

### 8.1 Tx-visible state (in `rules.State` and `CertState`)

These fields are read or written by tx rules, so they live in the ledger state that the tx rules thread.

- `VotingState` MUST gain `committeeState: Map[Credential, CommitteeAuthorization]`. `[DM-1]`
- `CommitteeAuthorization` MUST be one of `HotCredential(hot: Credential)` or `Resigned(anchor: Option[Anchor])`. `[DM-2]`
- `VotingState` MUST gain `numDormantEpochs: EpochNo`. `[DM-3]`
- `PoolsState` MUST gain `vrfKeyHashes: Map[VrfKeyHash, Long]`, the count of pools per VRF key. `[DM-4]`
- `rules.State.govState` MUST hold a `ConwayGovState` with the fields below. `[DM-5]`

| Field | Type | Ledger |
|---|---|---|
| `proposals` | insertion-ordered `GovActionState`s, keyed by `GovActionId` | `C/Governance/Proposals.hs:238-243`, `pProps` |
| `roots` | `GovRoots(pparamUpdate, hardFork, committee, constitution: Option[GovActionId])` | `pRoots` of `Proposals` (`C/Governance/Proposals.hs:240`); GOV reads them through `toPrevGovActionIds` (`C/Rules/Gov.hs:466`). They equal `ensPrevGovActionIds` of the enact state at each boundary |
| `committee` | `Option[Committee(members: Map[Credential, EpochNo], threshold: UnitInterval)]` | `C/Governance/Procedures.hs:544-549` |
| `constitution` | `Constitution` (exists, `SC/Types.scala:801`) | – |

- `GovActionState` MUST hold: `id`, `committeeVotes`, `drepVotes`, `spoVotes`, `procedure: ProposalProcedure`, `proposedIn`, `expiresAfter`. `[DM-6]`
- The CHANGELOG MUST list the type change of `State.govState` and `UTxOState.govState` in bold as a migration hazard. `[DM-5a]`
- The current pparams MUST stay in `Context.env.params`. `[DM-7]`

*Implementation status (phase 2, design):* `[DM-5]` the class is `GovState` in `scalus.cardano.ledger`, the Scalus name of the ledger's `ConwayGovState`, as `VotingState` is the name of `VState` (D27). It replaces the alias `type GovState = Array[Element]` under the same name, so `State.govState` and `UTxOState.govState` keep their names and only change type; `GovState.empty` has no proposal, empty roots, no committee and the constitution of `[ES-14]`. Fields, in this order: `proposals: Vector[GovActionState]`, `roots: GovRoots`, `committee: Option[Committee]`, `constitution: Constitution`. There is no per-purpose graph (D28): the ledger serializes a `Proposals` as the roots and the ordered map only (`C/Governance/Proposals.hs:385-388`) and rebuilds the graph from them (`mkProposals`, `:338-356`), so a parent check needs only the roots and the list. `GovState` has `proposal(id: GovActionId): Option[GovActionState]`, a linear scan, and the GOV operations of `[GV-6]`, `[GV-14]` and `[GV-15]` as methods; phase 3 and 5 add the removal and enactment operations to it. `GovRoots` is a case class of four `Option[GovActionId]` with `GovRoots.empty`; `GovActionPurpose` is an enum `PParamUpdate, HardFork, Committee, Constitution` with `GovActionPurpose.of(action: GovAction): Option[GovActionPurpose]` (`withGovActionParent`, `C/Governance/Procedures.hs:782-790`: NoConfidence and UpdateCommittee share `Committee`; TreasuryWithdrawals and InfoAction have none). `Committee` is `case class Committee(members: Map[Credential, EpochNo], threshold: UnitInterval)`. `[DM-6]` `GovActionState` is a case class with the seven fields in the ledger's order (`C/Governance/Procedures.hs:213-221`): `committeeVotes: Map[Credential, Vote]` keyed by the hot credential, `drepVotes: Map[Credential, Vote]`, `spoVotes: Map[PoolKeyHash, Vote]`, and `def action: GovAction = procedure.govAction`. `[DM-1]`, `[DM-2]` `committeeState` is the third field of `VotingState`, after `numDormantEpochs` (D29), and `CommitteeAuthorization` is an enum `HotCredential(hot: Credential)`, `Resigned(anchor: Option[Anchor])` (`K/State/CertState.hs:302-307`). All new types live in one file, `L/GovState.scala`, have no default parameters and have `empty` values where the ledger has a `Default`. None of them gets a production CBOR codec: nothing in production serializes a `State` (D36). MiMa: `scalus.cardano.ledger.rules` and `scalus.cardano.ledger` take per-symbol filters, with a comment, for `State` and `UTxOState` (`apply`, `copy`, the constructor, the product accessor of the field, the three default-argument methods of `State.govState`), for `GovState.empty` (result type) and for the 2-argument `VotingState.copy`; a deprecated `Array[Element]` constructor of `State` is not kept, since no body of it could mean anything.

*Implementation status (phase 2, code, gov-state-model):* `[DM-1]`, `[DM-2]`, `[DM-5]`, `[DM-6]` are in `L/GovState.scala` (`CommitteeAuthorization`, `Committee`, `GovActionState`, `GovRoots`, `GovState`) and `VotingState.committeeState`. `GovActionPurpose` and the GOV operations of `[GV-6]`, `[GV-14]`, `[GV-15]` come with GOV; no rule writes the new fields yet. `VotingState` has no deprecated 2-argument forms (D39): the 2-argument form never shipped, and MiMa reports no `VotingState` problem beyond the 1-argument `copy` filter of D17. MiMa reports 14 problems for the type change, all on `UTxOState`, `rules.State` and the companion object of the old alias (`Entities$package$GovState$`); `State.apply$default$5` is not among them. They are per-symbol filters with one comment in `build.sbt`. Tests: `ImmutableEmulatorTest` "keep every field of the ledger state" (one proposal with a vote, a committee and a hot key, phase 2 acceptance 5); the `assertSameSnapshot` helpers of `EmulatorForkTest`, `EmulatorChainTest` and `EmulatorMempoolTest` compare the snapshots with `==`, gov state included.

*Implementation status (phase 2, code, gov-conformance):* `[CV-2]`, `[CV-3]`, `[CV-7]`, `[CV-8]` are in the conformance test. `CardanoLedgerConformanceTest` runs the 132 GOV, EPOCH, RATIFY, ENACT and LEDGER dirs (a test pins the count), and all 361 conformance tests pass. `LedgerStateComparison` compares `govState.proposals` (in order; the detail names the ids, or each differing proposal), `govState.roots`, `govState.committee`, `govState.constitution` and `committeeState`; the `govState` exclusion and the two `dreps.expiry` exclusions of phase 1 are gone, and only `instantStake` stays excluded. A mutation that drops DRep key votes in `GovState.withVote` (not committed) fails 67 vectors with `govState.proposals`. `[CV-8]` is `CardanoLedgerVectors.caseEpoch`, refined by D43: a case runs at the largest of 899, the `[CV-8]` epoch of each case of its dir up to it, and the `proposedIn` of each proposal in its old state. The cases of a dir are now read in numeric order (`10` came before `2`). The verdict is strict: a pass needs `Right`, a fail needs `Left`, and a thrown exception matches neither; no case of the run dirs relied on a throw. A ledger state that does not decode gives `UndecodableState`, not a fail verdict, and its decode is inside the `Try`, so one such case no longer stops its dir. `expectedFailures` holds three entries, each with its cause: `ENACT.Treasury withdrawals.Withdrawals exceeding maxBound Word64 submitted in a single proposal` cases 10 to 13 and `GOV.Voting.committee member can not vote on UpdateCommittee action` cases 2 and 3 (states with a `Word64` above `Long.MaxValue`, Q9), and `GOV.Voting.expired gov-actions` case 5, a vote three epochs after its proposal (`GovSpec.hs:788-798`) that leaves no trace in the dump; the ledger disables that test in its own conformance run (`disableInConformanceIt`, `S/ImpTest.hs:758-766`), and `GovMutatorTest` covers `VotingOnExpiredGovAction`. Correction to **Why** `[CV-2]`: consecutive cases do not always chain. Of 2 241 consecutive pairs in the run dirs, 151 differ (the new state, or the old one of a failed case, is not the next old state): `passEpoch` and Imp state edits between txs. Each case still carries its own old state, so no case needs a transition the harness does not run.

**Why** `[DM-7]`: every tx rule reads `env.params` today. ENACT changes pparams only at a boundary, and each boundary already builds a new env (`N/EmulatorBase.scala:608-621`). Moving pparams into the gov state would touch every validator.

### 8.2 Epoch state (in `LedgerSnapshot`, not seen by tx rules)

- `LedgerSnapshot` MUST gain `epoch: EpochState`. `[DM-8]`
- `EpochState` MUST hold the fields below. `[DM-9]`

| Field | Type | Ledger |
|---|---|---|
| `lastEpoch` | `EpochNo` | `nesEL` |
| `accountState` | `AccountState(treasury: Coin, reserves: Coin)` | `ChainAccountState` |
| `snapshots` | `SnapShots(mark, set, go: StakeSnapshot, fee: Coin)` | `K/State/SnapShots.hs` |
| `blocksMadePrev`, `blocksMadeCur` | `Map[PoolKeyHash, Long]` | `nesBprev`, `nesBcur` |
| `pendingRewards` | `Option[RewardUpdate]` | `nesRu` |
| `pendingRatify` | `RatifyState` (D85: not an `Option`) | `cgsDRepPulsingState` |
| `prevParams` | `ProtocolParams` | `cgsPrevPParams` |

- `StakeSnapshot` MUST hold: stake per credential, pool delegation per credential, and the pool params per pool at the boundary. `[DM-10]`
- `RewardUpdate` MUST hold: `deltaT`, `deltaR`, `deltaF`, and the rewards per credential. `[DM-11]`
- `RatifyState` MUST hold: the enact state after the run, the enacted ids in order, the expired ids, and `delayed`. `[DM-12]`
- `UtxoEnv.treasury` MUST equal `epoch.accountState.treasury` in every ledger snapshot. `[DM-13]`

**Why** `[DM-13]`: `[SC-6]` of the provider spec keeps the treasury in the env for LEDGER. The account state becomes the source, and the env copies it at each boundary.

- New epoch types and functions MUST live in a new package `scalus.cardano.ledger.epoch`. `[DM-14]`
- `RewardsCalculation` and `StakeDistribution` MUST be deprecated, with a pointer to the new package. `[DM-15]`

**Why** `[DM-14]`, `[DM-15]`: `scalus.cardano.ledger.rules` is a MIXED MiMa package, so every new symbol there needs care. The old objects use `Double` and a wrong formula (section 6). Fixing them in place would change results under the same name.

*Implementation status (phase 3, design):* `[DM-8]` `LedgerSnapshot` gains `epoch: EpochState` as its fourth field; `after` copies it, `atSlot` writes it, and the block and mempool snapshots carry it, so a rollback restores the account state with the ledger. `[DM-9]` in phase 3 `EpochState` has one field, `accountState: AccountState`, and `AccountState` is `case class AccountState(treasury: Coin, reserves: Coin)`; phases 4 and 5 append their fields. `lastEpoch` is not stored (D46): every snapshot's ledger is at the epoch of its `context.env.slot`, since a snapshot is made by `atSlot(slot)` and then by txs at that slot, so `lastEpoch` is `slotConfig.epochOf(context.env.slot)`, and the boundary loop of `[EB-1]` runs for `n` from `epochOf(env.slot) + 1` to `epochOf(slot)`. *Superseded (agent, phase 3 review):* D66 stores it as `EpochState.lastEpoch`, since in the `Instant` preset a block snapshot can have an env slot in an epoch before the last boundary its ledger ran; the loop runs for `n` from `lastEpoch + 1` to `epochOf(slot)`, the genesis takes `epochOf(context.env.slot)`, and `EpochTransition.transition` writes `lastEpoch := n` (`[EB-21]`, `C/Rules/NewEpoch.hs:186`). `[DM-14]` the package is split (D47): `scalus.cardano.ledger.epoch` holds the value types a caller reads, in phase 3 `AccountState` with `AccountState.mainnet` (the two constants of `[ES-7]` and `[ES-7a]`); `scalus.cardano.ledger.epoch.internal` holds `EpochState` and `EpochTransition`, the step functions of section 12.2, under one wildcard MiMa filter with a comment, as `scalus.cardano.node.stream.internal.*` has. `EpochTransition.transition(ledger: State, epoch: EpochState, boundary: EpochNo): (State, EpochState)` is pure and takes no pparams in phase 3; phase 5 adds them. `[DM-13]` holds by construction: the genesis account state copies `context.env.treasury` and takes the reserves of `EmulatorInitialState` (D54), and `LedgerSnapshot.atSlot` writes `env.treasury` from the account state after the last transition (D49). `LedgerSnapshot` is `private[node]` but public bytecode; the implementer takes the per-symbol filters for its constructor, `apply` and `copy` from `mimaReportBinaryIssues`, if it reports them. `[DM-15]` stays in phase 4 with the rewards code.

*Implementation status (phase 3, code, epoch-state-and-tick):* `[DM-8]`, `[DM-9]`, `[DM-13]`, `[DM-14]` are in the code as the design note says: `AccountState` in `L/epoch/AccountState.scala`, `EpochState` and `EpochTransition` in `L/epoch/internal/`, `LedgerSnapshot.epoch` the fourth field. The genesis account state is built in one place, the `private[node]` `EmulatorState.initial` overload that takes the reserves; the other overloads pass `AccountState.mainnet.reserves`. `LedgerSnapshot.atSlot` writes `env.treasury` from the account state after the loop, also for a move that crosses no boundary, so `[DM-13]` holds for every snapshot built from a genesis. `EmulatorBase.atSlot` is removed (D49). MiMa (baseline 1.4.0) reports neither it nor `LedgerSnapshot`, both qualified-private, so neither needs a filter; `build.sbt` has the one wildcard filter `scalus.cardano.ledger.epoch.internal.*` of D47. `EpochTransition.transition` runs `[EB-17]` only; the other phase 3 steps join it with their items, each as a private step function called in the order of section 12.2.

Rules added in the phase 4 design round (agent, 2026-11-03), read from `K/State/SnapShots.hs:150-465`, `K/State/Stake.hs:157-246`, `S/RewardUpdate.hs:108-142` and `C/Rules/NewEpoch.hs:165-192` at the pin:

- In phase 4 `EpochState` MUST gain, after `lastEpoch` and in this order: `snapshots: SnapShots`, `blocksMadePrev: Map[PoolKeyHash, Long]`, `blocksMadeCur: Map[PoolKeyHash, Long]`, `pendingRewards: Option[RewardUpdate]`. `prevParams` and `pendingRatify` join in phase 5 (D68). `[DM-9a]`
- `StakeSnapshot` MUST hold `activeStake: Map[Credential, Coin]`, the stake of each credential of `[SN-2]` to `[SN-4]`; `delegations: Map[Credential, PoolKeyHash]`, with the same keys; and `pools: Map[PoolKeyHash, PoolSnapshot]`, one entry per pool of `[SN-7]`. `[DM-10a]`
- `PoolSnapshot` MUST hold the fields of the ledger's `StakePoolSnapShot` except the derived `spssStakeRatio`, in the ledger's order: `stake: Coin`, `selfDelegatedOwners: Set[AddrKeyHash]`, `selfDelegatedOwnersStake: Coin`, `vrfKeyHash: VrfKeyHash`, `pledge: Coin`, `cost: Coin`, `margin: UnitInterval`, `numDelegators: Int`, `rewardAccount: Credential` (`K/State/SnapShots.hs:160-185`). `StakeSnapshot.totalActiveStake` MUST be the sum of `activeStake`, or 1 when that sum is 0 (`[SN-6]`, `sumAllActiveStake`, `K/State/Stake.hs:157-160`). `[DM-10b]`
- `RewardUpdate` MUST be `RewardUpdate(deltaT: Coin, deltaR: Coin, rewards: Map[Credential, Coin], deltaF: Coin)`: `deltaR` and `deltaF` carry a sign, `rewards` holds the sum per credential of `[RW-7]`, and a leader reward of 0 is an entry (`[RW-8a]`). The ledger's `nonMyopic` is not kept (section 7.2). `[DM-11a]`
- `SnapShots`, `StakeSnapshot`, `PoolSnapshot`, `RewardUpdate` and `PoolPerformance` MUST live in `scalus.cardano.ledger.epoch`, with `SnapShots.empty` and `StakeSnapshot.empty`; the SNAP function, the reward calculation and the exact rational type MUST live in `scalus.cardano.ledger.epoch.internal` (D47, D69). `[DM-14a]`

**Why** `[DM-10a]`, `[DM-10b]`: the dumps store a snapshot as `[activeStake, stakePoolsSnapShot]`, `activeStake` a map from credential to `[stake, pool]` and each pool a 10-element record (`K/State/SnapShots.hs:239-253, 290-296`; checked on `test_5057`, 2026-11-03). A Scalus snapshot of the same fields decodes from `st` with no loss and compares with `st_out` by `==` (`[CV-14]`). The pool params of `[DM-10]` cannot be decoded from a dump: it has no relays, no metadata and only the owners that delegate to the pool. `selfDelegatedOwners` and `numDelegators` count accounts with zero stake too (`spsDelegators`, `:203-216`), so they are fields, not functions of `activeStake`.

*Implementation status (phase 4, design):* correction to `[DM-10]`: "the pool params per pool" is `PoolSnapshot` of `[DM-10b]` (D69). `[DM-9]`: `prevParams` is not stored in phase 4 (D68): until phase 5 nothing changes `context.env.params` (`[DM-7]`; no Emulator API edits the pparams), so the reward calculation reads `context.env.params`. Phase 5 MUST add `prevParams: ProtocolParams` to `EpochState` with `[EB-13]`, its only writer, and MUST pass it, not `context.env.params`, to `[EB-22]`: `[EB-13]` runs before `[EB-22]` in the same transition, so after an enactment the two differ. `[DM-11]` is `[DM-11a]`. `[DM-15]` lands in phase 4 (D80): `RewardsCalculation` and `StakeDistribution` get `@deprecated("use scalus.cardano.ledger.epoch", "1.4.1")`; `EmulatorBase.stakeDistribution` reads the instant stake of `EpochTransition.instantStake` instead of `StakeDistribution.aggregateUtxoStake` (`N/EmulatorBase.scala:245`), so no main code uses a deprecated object; `RewardsCalculationTest` and `StakeDistributionTest` take `@nowarn("msg=deprecated")` with a comment. The new types have no `with<Field>` methods and no default parameters (D55); MiMa sees additions only, since `EpochState` is under the wildcard filter of `epoch.internal.*`.

*Implementation status (phase 4, reward-application):* `[DM-15]` is in the code as D80 says. `RewardsCalculation` and `StakeDistribution` carry `@deprecated("use scalus.cardano.ledger.epoch: …", "1.4.1")`, each naming the Emulator read that replaces it (`pendingRewards`, `snapshots`); `RewardsCalculation` still calls `StakeDistribution`, and Scala 3 reports no deprecation inside a deprecated object. `EmulatorBase.stakeDistribution` sums the UTxO stake with `EpochTransition.instantStake`, now public in `epoch.internal` (wildcard MiMa filter, D47). `stakeDistribution` is not made the same as SNAP: it stays the live view, with a row for every account and every credential with base-address coin, registered or not, and its `rewards` column; the snapshot drops those rows (`[SN-3]`, `[SN-4]`). Only its coin is the SNAP coin; `StakeDistribution.extractStakeCredential` and `instantStake` count the same addresses (base addresses only), so no row changes. `RewardsCalculationTest` and `StakeDistributionTest` carry `@nowarn("msg=deprecated")` with a comment.

Rules added in the phase 5 design round (agent, 2026-11-03), read from `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:276-379`, `C/Governance.hs:301-325, 460-516`, `C/Governance/Internal.hs:131-141, 257-275`, `C/Rules/Ratify.hs:308-360` and `C/Translation.hs:69-83` at the pin:

- In phase 5 `EpochState` MUST gain, after `pendingRewards` and in this order: `prevParams: ProtocolParams` and `pendingRatify: RatifyState`. `pendingRatify` is not an `Option` (D85). `[DM-9b]`
- `RatifyState` MUST be `RatifyState(enactState: EnactState, enacted: Vector[GovActionId], expired: Set[GovActionId], delayed: Boolean)`: `enacted` in the order RATIFY enacted them. `EnactState` MUST be `EnactState(committee: Option[Committee], constitution: Constitution, params: ProtocolParams, withdrawals: Map[Credential, Coin], roots: GovRoots)`. It has no treasury and no previous pparams (D86). Both MUST live in `scalus.cardano.ledger.epoch`, with no default parameters and no `with<Field>` methods (D55); RATIFY and ENACT MUST live in `scalus.cardano.ledger.epoch.internal` (D47). `[DM-12a]`
- At genesis, `prevParams` MUST be the genesis pparams, and `pendingRatify` MUST be `RatifyState(enactState of the genesis, ∅, ∅, false)`: the enact state holds the committee, the constitution and the roots of the genesis gov state, the genesis pparams and no withdrawals. Both genesis builders MUST do this, also `Emulator.withState` (`C/Translation.hs:69-83`). `[DM-9c]`

**Why** `[DM-9c]`: the ledger enters Conway with `DRComplete def ratifyState` and `rsEnactState = mkEnactState govState`, so the first boundary enacts nothing, expires nothing and keeps the committee and the constitution (`C/Translation.hs:72-77`). A default enact state would remove a seeded committee at the first boundary, since `[EB-13]` sets the committee from it. A consequence: a `withState` genesis with proposals expires none of them at the first boundary, as the ledger does.

*Implementation status (phase 5, design):* correction to the table of `[DM-9]`: `pendingRatify` has type `RatifyState`, not `Option[RatifyState]` (`[DM-9b]`, D85). `[DM-12]` is `[DM-12a]`. The ledger's `ensTreasury` is a variable of the RATIFY run, not state: RATIFY sets it to 0 when the run ends (`C/Rules/Ratify.hs:360`), and it is 0 in all 1066 states of the NEWEPOCH dumps (checked 2026-11-03). `ensPrevPParams` has no reader. The JS and Java reads are `[ES-9b]`.

*Implementation status (phase 5, code, enact-and-pparams):* `[DM-9b]`, `[DM-9c]` and `[DM-12a]` are in the code. `EnactState` and `RatifyState` are in `L/epoch/RatifyState.scala`; `EpochState` has `prevParams` and `pendingRatify` as fields 7 and 8. Both genesis builders reach one site, the `private[node]` `EmulatorState.initial` overload with the reserves (`Emulator.withState` calls it too, `N/EmulatorBase.scala:958`), which takes the enact state from `Ratify.enactStateOf(ledger.govState, context.env.params)`, the port of `mkEnactState` that `[EB-20a]` reuses. Until `[EB-20]` lands, no boundary replaces `pendingRatify`, so it stays the genesis value, which enacts nothing (`[EB-24]`) (superseded: `[EB-20]` landed, see the ratify note under `[EB-20]`). Tests: `EmulatorEpochParamsTest` "the genesis ratify result holds the enact state of the genesis and enacts nothing" (a seeded committee and roots).

### 8.3 Rationals

- The reward calculation MUST use exact rationals for `rho`, `tau` and `a0`. `[DM-16]`
- `[DM-17]` withdrawn by the owner decision on Q3 (2026-11-02). It derived rationals from `Double`.
- `ProtocolParams.monetaryExpansion` and `ProtocolParams.treasuryCut` MUST have type `UnitInterval` (`SC/UnitInterval.scala:18`). `[DM-18]`
- `ProtocolParams.poolPledgeInfluence` MUST have type `NonNegativeInterval` (`SC/NonNegativeInterval.scala:20`). `[DM-19]`
- The JSON readers of `ProtocolParams` MUST parse these three values from their decimal text, not through `Double`. `[DM-20]`

*Example, illustrative,* for `[DM-20]`: Blockfrost `"rho": 0.003` gives `UnitInterval(3, 1000)`. A `Double` path gives `0.003000000000000000062...`, which is not `3/1000`.

**Why** `[DM-18]`, `[DM-19]`: the ledger types are `UnitInterval` and `NonNegativeInterval`. The change breaks the binary API of `scalus-core`; the CHANGELOG MUST list it in bold as a migration hazard. `[DM-21]`

Rules added in the phase 4 design round (agent, 2026-11-03):

- A JSON reader of `[DM-20]` MUST take the decimal of `rho`, `tau` and `a0` as follows: a JSON string is the decimal text; a JSON number is the shortest decimal that reads back as the same `Double` (`BigDecimal.decimal`). The rational MUST be that decimal in lowest terms, and the read MUST fail with the field name when a term does not fit a `Long`. `[DM-20a]`
- `fromCardanoCliJson` MUST read the three fields by `[DM-20a]` too; the other `UnitInterval` and `NonNegativeInterval` fields of both readers (voting thresholds, prices) MUST keep their codecs. `[DM-20b]`
- The Blockfrost and CLI writers MUST write the three fields as JSON numbers, `toDouble`. `[DM-21a]`

*Example, normative test vector,* for `[DM-20a]`: `CardanoInfo.mainnet`, `preprod` and `preview` carry `monetaryExpansion = UnitInterval(3, 1000)`, `treasuryCut = UnitInterval(1, 5)`, `poolPledgeInfluence = NonNegativeInterval(3, 10)`, the tag-30 values of the Conway pparams in the NEWEPOCH dumps, and `fromBlockfrostJson(toBlockfrostJson(p))` gives the same three values for each. Both assertions are in a shared test, so they run on the JVM, JS and Native: the first checks the compile-time read of the resources (`blockfrost-params-epoch-645.json`, `-preprod-310.json`, `-preview-1370.json`) and `ToExpr`, the second the runtime reader, since the writer emits `0.003` as a JSON number. Scala.js and Native have no class-path resources at test time, so a direct read of the resource files stays in the JVM `ProtocolParamsTest`.

**Why** `[DM-20a]`: upickle parses a JSON number into `ujson.Num`, a `Double`, before either reader sees it, so the number text does not reach `blockfrostParamsReadWriter`. The shortest round-trip decimal equals the text for every number of at most 15 significant digits, which covers every Cardano source; `Double.toString` is shortest on JVM 19 and later, on JS (`Number.prototype.toString`), and on Native (Ryu, `scala.scalanative.runtime.ieee754tostring.ryu`, javalib 0.5.12); before JDK 19 it is not shortest for some values (`8.41E21` prints `8.409999999999999E21`), none of them a Cardano pparam. This is not the `Double` field that D9 rejected: the value is exact from the reader on (D78). **Why** `[DM-20b]`: the CLI reader is `macroRW`, which takes the `UnitInterval` codec of the companion (`fromDouble`, precision 10^6) for every `UnitInterval` field; the least code reads the record with `macroRW` and then overwrites the three fields from the same `ujson.Value`.

*Implementation status (phase 4, design):* the readers and writers that change are: `ProtocolParams.blockfrostParamsReadWriter` (`SC/ProtocolParams.scala:249-265, 353-383`), `cardanoCliParamsReadWriter` (`:396-415`), `ProtocolParamsToExpr` (the existing `ToExpr[UnitInterval]` and `ToExpr[NonNegativeInterval]`), so `CardanoInfo.mainnet`, `preprod` and `preview` inline exact values; `ConwayProtocolParams.toProtocolParams` (`scalus-cardano-ledger/jvm/src/main/scala/scalus/cardano/ledger/ConwayProtocolParams.scala:105-113`) passes `rho`, `a0`, `tau` through without `toDouble`; `JsProtocolParams.zero` takes `UnitInterval.zero` and `NonNegativeInterval.zero`. No CBOR, `ToData` or `FromData` codec of `ProtocolParams` exists, and `ProtocolParamUpdate` already has the ledger types. The JS surface does not change: `JsProtocolParams` exposes none of the three fields, so `scalus.d.ts` stays as it is and `checkDtsUpToDate` confirms it. Java callers see `monetaryExpansion()` and `treasuryCut()` return `UnitInterval` and `poolPledgeInfluence()` return `NonNegativeInterval`. MiMa (`scalusJVM`): per-symbol filters with one comment for the `ProtocolParams` constructor, `apply`, `copy`, the three accessors, their product accessors and `copy$default$` methods, as `mimaReportBinaryIssues` lists them; no `Double` forwarders (D79). The bold CHANGELOG line of `[DM-21]` names the three fields, their new types and the Java return types. Fixtures: `scalus-core/shared/src/main/resources/protocol-params.json` (CLI shape, `0.003`, `0.3`, `0.2`) reads unchanged.

*Implementation status (phase 4, rational-pparams):* done as designed. `decimalFraction` in `ProtocolParams` (`SC/ProtocolParams.scala`) implements `[DM-20a]` for both readers. Refinements: the read also fails, with the field name, when `rho` or `tau` is outside `[0, 1]` or `a0` is negative, instead of the bare `require` of the `UnitInterval` constructor. `cardanoCliParamsReadWriter` becomes a `ujson.Value` bimap around the `macroRW` codec; the read overwrites the three fields (`[DM-20b]`) and the write is `macroRW`'s. The Blockfrost and CLI JSON that `toBlockfrostJson` and the CLI writer emit for `CardanoInfo.mainnet`, `preprod` and `preview` is byte-identical before and after the change (SHA-256 of all six outputs compared). The shared `ProtocolParamsJsonTest` passes on the JVM, JS and Native. The resource test in the JVM `ProtocolParamsTest` reads only the three resources that `CardanoInfo` inlines: the older `blockfrost-params-epoch-471/507/544/642.json` predate Conway and lack `committee_max_term_length`, so `fromBlockfrostJson` rejects them, as it did before. MiMa: 12 per-symbol filters in `build.sbt` (`scalus` project); `scalusCardanoLedgerJVM` needs none. `checkDtsUpToDate` passes unchanged.

*Implementation status (phase 4, review round 1):* `ConwayProtocolParams.toProtocolParams` puts `a0`, `rho` and `tau` in lowest terms too, as the ledger holds them (`boundRational` uses `%`, `K/BaseTypes.hs:340`). So a CBOR value `UnitInterval(2, 10)` and the JSON value `0.2` give the same `UnitInterval(1, 5)`, and `UnitInterval` keeps plain case-class equality. The JSON round trip of `[DM-21a]` is exact only for a decimal fraction: a non-decimal value such as `1/3` writes as a `Double` and reads back as `3333333333333333/10^16`. No Cardano network carries such a value. Test: `ConwayProtocolParamsTest` (JVM).

## 9. Tx parity rules (phase 0)

- The PlutusV3 TxInfo builder MUST fail with a collect error when `pvMajor ≥ 11` and inputs and reference inputs share an element. `[TX-1]`
- The failure of `[TX-1]` MUST name every common input. `[TX-1a]`
- The POOL check MUST reject a pool registration whose metadata hash is longer than 32 bytes. `[TX-2]`
- `[TX-3]` withdrawn. Conway decoding rejects an empty redeemer map (`A/TxWits.hs:571-572`), so the case cannot occur.
- `OutputBootAddrAttrsSizeValidator` MUST check the collateral return output too. `[TX-4]`

*Example, illustrative,* for `[TX-1]`, PlutusV3 spend of `u` with `u` also in reference inputs:

| PV | Ledger | Scalus today | Scalus after |
|---|---|---|---|
| 10 | `BabbageNonDisjointRefInputs` | reject | reject |
| 11 | `CollectErrors [BadTranslation (ReferenceInputsNotDisjointFromInputs [u])]` | accept | reject |
| 11, PlutusV2 script | accept | accept | accept |

**Why** `[TX-1]`: commit `123babebd` allowed the overlap outside PV 9 and 10, which matches the UTXO rule. The PlutusV3 context check (`C/TxInfo.hs:488-493`) was not ported. The TxBuilder now builds such txs (`SpendAndReferenceSameUtxoTest`), so a dApp test passes locally and fails on mainnet.

*Implementation status (phase 0):* `[TX-1]` `getTxInfoV3` throws an `IllegalArgumentException` that names the common inputs, sorted; the Plutus mutator turns it into a phase-1 rejection whatever `isValid` says, so no collateral is taken. The evaluator builds the `TxInfo` of every Plutus version the needed scripts use before it runs any script, as `collectPlutusScriptsWithContext` does (`eras/alonzo/impl/src/Cardano/Ledger/Alonzo/Plutus/Evaluate.hs:157-214`), so an earlier failing PlutusV1 or PlutusV2 script of a tx with `isValid = false` cannot turn the collect error into a collateral collection. The TxBuilder keeps allowing the overlap in its steps, since a PlutusV2 or native-script tx is valid with it at PV 11. For a PlutusV3 tx, `complete` evaluates the scripts and fails with a `BalancingException` that names the input. `[TX-2]` is a new `PoolMetadataHashTooBigException`, not a field of `StakePoolException`, to keep MiMa compatibility; the bundled `StakePoolException` is reported first when both apply.

*Implementation status (phase 6, design):* `[FL-10]` replaces the `IllegalArgumentException` of `[TX-1]` with `CollectErrorsException(txId, Seq(BadTranslation(...)))`, still a phase-1 rejection. `SubmitError.rule` of such a tx changes from `IllegalArgument` to `CollectErrors`; the CHANGELOG states it, and the phase 6 test of `[TX-1]` asserts `CollectErrors`, red on its parent.

*Implementation status (phase 0):* `[TX-4]` `ByronAddress.attributesSize` computes `bootstrapAddressAttrsSize`: the length of the HD payload that attribute 1 serializes as a CBOR bytestring, without its head (`eras/byron/ledger/impl/src/Cardano/Chain/Common/AddrAttributes.hs:109`, `:178-179`), plus the content bytes of every attribute whose key is not 1 or 2 (`Attributes.hs:140-142`); a repeated key keeps its last value (D13). The network magic does not count. An address that a node cannot decode has no node size; for it Scalus counts all bytes of a malformed attribute 1 and ignores a malformed attribute 2 (D14). `ByronAddress.derivationPath` still returns the raw attribute-1 value (D15).

### 9.1 Block body

- The forge MUST stop a block before the total reference-script size of its txs exceeds 1 MiB. `[BB-1]`
- `produceBlock(on, slot, txs)` MUST return `ForgeError.BlockTooBig` when the total of `[BB-1]` exceeds 1 MiB. `[BB-2]`
- At PV ≥ 11 the total MUST resolve each tx's inputs against the UTxO at the block start plus the outputs of earlier txs in the block. `[BB-3]`
- For an earlier tx with `isValid = false`, `[BB-3]` MUST add only its collateral return. `[BB-4]`

**Why** `[BB-3]`, `[BB-4]`: `C/Rules/Bbody.hs:369-382`. Spent inputs are not removed from the lookup. The per-tx size function is `C/UTxO.hs:171-175`.

*Implementation status (phase 0):* `BlockForge.refScriptsSize` ports `totalRefScriptSizeInBlock`, PV switch included: up to PV 10 every tx resolves on the UTxO at the block start. The forge limit and `[FG-18]` of `produceBlock(on, …)` check it with the body size and the execution units. `submit` holds every tx, `isValid` or not, to the per-tx reference-script limit of 200 KiB, as the node's mempool measure does (`txMeasureRefScripts`, ouroboros-consensus `Shelley/Ledger/Mempool.hs`): LEDGER skips that check when `isValid` is false, and the forge always takes the first mempool tx, so such a tx over 1 MiB would otherwise be forged into a block that breaks `[BB-1]`. The mempool capacity (chain spec `[MP-4]`) still measures only the body size and the execution units; the node's block capacity also has a reference-script dimension. The acceptance test of phase 0 item 3 uses six txs of 200 KiB, not three of 400 KiB: `ConwayTxRefScriptsSizeTooBig` caps a valid tx at 200 KiB, so three valid txs total at most 600 KiB. Five fit a block (1 024 000 bytes), the sixth forges in the next block.

### 9.2 Conformance harness

- `CardanoLedgerConformanceTest` MUST run the DELEG (24), CERTS (2) and GOVCERT (10) vector dirs. `[CV-1]`
- `CardanoLedgerConformanceTest` MUST run the GOV (56), EPOCH (13), RATIFY (46), ENACT (16) and LEDGER (1) vector dirs once phase 2 lands. `[CV-2]`
- Each phase MUST remove the `LedgerStateComparison` exclusions it makes true (`scalus-cardano-ledger/jvm/src/test/scala/scalus/testing/conformance/LedgerStateComparison.scala:39-50`). `[CV-3]`

*Implementation status (phase 0):* `[CV-1]` 33 of the 36 dirs pass. Four cases of three GOVCERT dirs need committee state (`ConwayCommitteeHasPreviouslyResigned`, `ConwayCommitteeIsUnknown`, section 5.7): "fails for.registering a resigned CC member hotkey" cases 3 and 6, "fails for.resigning a nonexistent CC member hotkey" case 1, "succeeds for.resigning a non-CC key" case 0. The test lists them as expected failures and fails when one of them starts to match, so phase 2 must remove them. The harness also failed open: the vectors are cached in the temp directory, and macOS had deleted the cached files, so every vector loaded no case and passed. The cache now records its file count, and a vector with no case fails.

**Why** `[CV-2]`: every vector is one LEDGER transition: the tx, the old ledger state and the new ledger state. Consecutive vectors chain with no gap (checked: `new(0) == old(1)` in "EPOCH.Proposals.Expired proposal deposit refunded"). So the EPOCH, RATIFY and ENACT dirs test the tx rules and the stored proposals of those scenarios. They do not test the boundary. Boundary acceptance comes from ported Imp scenarios (section 16).

| Exclusion | Removed in phase |
|---|---|
| `deposited` | 1 |
| `dreps.delegates` | 1 |
| `dreps.expiry` | 1 |
| `govState` | 2 |
| `instantStake` | never (decisions D5, D81) |

- The vector decoder MUST pass the decoded `numDormantEpochs` into `VotingState` (`LedgerState.scala:342-351` drops it today), and the decoded VRF map into `PoolsState` (the old 4-map layout has none and decodes to empty). `[CV-4]`
- The comparison MUST compare `pools.vrfKeyHashes`. `[CV-5]`

**Why** `[CV-4]`: without the counter the dormant bump of `[DR-2]` never fires in a vector, and the `dreps.expiry` exclusion cannot come off. **Why** `[CV-5]`: the vectors are PV10, where neither side writes the map (`[BK-4]`), so the comparison checks the PV gate for free (D23).

- The vector decoder MUST read the gov state of a vector as the seven-field `ConwayGovState` record (`C/Governance.hs:361-371`): the proposals as `[roots, [GovActionState…]]` (`C/Governance/Proposals.hs:385-388`), the committee and the constitution; it MUST ignore the current and previous pparams (hashes in the vectors), the future pparams and the DRep pulser. It MUST read the committee state of `VState` into `committeeState`. `[CV-6]`
- The comparison MUST compare `govState.proposals` in order, each with its id, votes, procedure and epochs, and `govState.roots`, `govState.committee`, `govState.constitution` and `committeeState`. `[CV-7]`

**Why** `[CV-6]`: checked on the vectors (2026-11-03, `GOV.Proposals.Consistency.Proposals are stored in the expected order/9`, `GOVCERT.fails for.resigning a nonexistent CC member hotkey/1`). A `StrictMaybe` in the state is `[]` or `[x]`: the four roots, the committee, the anchor of a `Resigned` committee entry (`K/State/CertState.hs:318-319`). Inside a stored `GovAction` the previous action id is `null`, as in a tx (`encodeNullStrictMaybe`), and so is an absent constitution guardrails hash (`C/Governance/Procedures.hs:936-941`), so the existing `GovAction`, `ProposalProcedure`, `GovActionId` and `Constitution` codecs read them. A `GovActionState` is a seven-element array in field order; votes are maps from `[tag, hash]` credentials or pool key hashes to `0 | 1 | 2` = No, Yes, Abstain, the `Vote` ordinals. A `Committee` is `[members, threshold]` with a tag-30 rational. A `CommitteeAuthorization` is `[0, credential]` or `[1, [] | [anchor]]`.

- The harness MUST derive the epoch of each case from its `newLedgerState`, in this order: the `proposedIn` of a proposal whose id carries the case tx's id; else, for a DRep that the tx registers, updates or votes with, its new expiry minus `dRepActivity` plus the `numDormantEpochs` of the old state; else 899. The slot is the first slot of that epoch. `[CV-8]`

**Why** `[CV-8]`: the dump carries no epoch, and the Imp tests pass epochs. Checked on the vectors (2026-11-03): 132 GOV, EPOCH, RATIFY, ENACT and LEDGER dirs hold proposals with `proposedIn` from 900 to 919 (`RATIFY.When CC expired.*` at 919, `GOV.Proposals.Consistency.Subtrees are pruned … over multiple rounds` at 906 and 913), and DRep expiries of 1000 and 1001 from votes at 901. At a fixed 899 the `govState.proposals` and `dreps.expiry` comparisons of `[CV-7]` fail in those cases, and `ExpirationEpochTooSmall` and `VotingOnExpiredGovAction` can flip a verdict. The new state is the only record of the epoch: `[GV-5]` writes it into every new proposal, and `[DR-1]`, `[DR-4]` into every DRep the tx touches; the DRep formula inverts `[DR-1]` with the counter the tx saw, which is the old counter when the tx has no proposal (a tx with a proposal takes the first branch). A case that touches neither has no observable epoch, and 899 is as good as any.

*Implementation status (phase 2, design):* the decoders are test-only, in `LedgerState.scala` of the conformance test; `LedgerState` and `UTxOState` derive decoders only, since nothing encodes a `GovState` (D36). The comparison names its fields `govState.proposals`, `govState.roots`, `govState.committee`, `govState.constitution`, `committeeState`; the `govState` exclusion goes. `[CV-8]` also retires the two `dreps.expiry` exclusions of phase 1, whose vote txs give its second branch (D38). The GOV, EPOCH, RATIFY, ENACT and LEDGER dirs of `[CV-2]` may still need an `expectedFailures` entry scoped by vector and case for a verdict the harness cannot replay (the treasury is 0 in every case), and a `LedgerStateComparison.Exclusion` scoped by vector name for a state field it cannot replay; each entry names its cause (D37).

*Implementation status (phase 2, code, gov-state-model):* `[CV-6]` is in `LedgerState.scala`: decoders for `GovState`, `GovRoots`, `GovActionState`, `Committee` and `CommitteeAuthorization`, and `VState` reads the committee state; the encoders of the test state classes are gone (D36). The gov state decoder reads the record length and skips the fields after the constitution, so a dump with full pparams in place of hashes still decodes. `GovStateDecoderTest` decodes every old and new state of every vector dir, except two whose stored proposal holds a `Word64` above `Long.MaxValue`: `ENACT.Treasury withdrawals.Withdrawals exceeding maxBound Word64 submitted in a single proposal` (withdrawal amounts, `Coin` is a `Long`) and `GOV.Voting.committee member can not vote on UpdateCommittee action` (a threshold denominator of 10^19, `UnitInterval` is two `Long`s). A harness run of these dirs cannot decode those cases (the state, or the tx that makes the proposal) and needs a D37 `expectedFailures` entry; before this change the decoder skipped the gov state and decoded them. The comparison still compares `govState` as one field under the `govState` exclusion; `[CV-7]` splits it.

NEWEPOCH runner, rules added in the phase 3 design round (agent, 2026-11-03). The vectors of `[CV-1]` and `[CV-2]` are LEDGER transitions and never cross a boundary (**Why** `[CV-2]`). The Dugite corpus (Q5) holds 533 NEWEPOCH transitions at PV11, each a `NewEpochState` before and after one boundary, and they are the vectors of the epoch rules:

- `scalus-cardano-ledger/jvm/src/test/resources/newepoch-vectors.tar.gz` MUST hold the 533 Dugite NEWEPOCH cases (`newepoch/NEWEPOCH/test_*/conformance_dump_{sig,st,st_out}.cbor`; `ctx` and `env` are `null`), with a README that records the source: release `conformance-corpus-v20261101-084724` of `michaeljfazio/dugite`, cardano-ledger `a88b60bd`, conway 1.23.0.0, PV 11.0 in every case. A test MUST pin the count at 533. `[CV-9]`
- The runner MUST decode `st` and `st_out` as the ledger's `NewEpochState`: `[lastEpoch, blocksPrev, blocksCur, epochState, rewardUpdate, poolDistr, null]` with `epochState = [[treasury, reserves], [certState, utxoState], snapshots, nonMyopic]` (`S/LedgerState/Types.hs:134-142, 401-411`, `K/State/ChainAccount.hs:46-48`); `[certState, utxoState]` is the existing test `LedgerState` decoder. `sig` is the boundary epoch and equals `lastEpoch + 1` in every case. `[CV-10]`
- The runner MUST read, from the input `st` of each case, the `RatifyState` of the DRep pulser (`enacted` ids and `expired` ids, record fields 2 and 3, `C/Governance/Internal.hs:672-680`, the set under tag 258) and `deltaT` of the reward update (`[1, [deltaT, deltaR, rs, deltaF, nonMyopic]]`). Both are always present: the dump encodes a pulsing pulser as the finished `DRComplete` (`C/Governance/DRepPulser.hs:463-467`), and every input reward update is `Complete`. `[CV-11]`
- The runner MUST run `EpochTransition.transition` on the decoded ledger state and account state for the boundary `sig`, without an Emulator, and compare with `st_out`. When `enacted` is empty it MUST compare `pools.*`, `accounts` with its four subfields, `dreps.numDormantEpochs`, `committeeState`, `govState.proposals`, `govState.roots`, `govState.committee`, `govState.constitution`, `deposited`, `donation`, the treasury as `actual.treasury == expected.treasury − deltaT`, and the ids `[EB-12a]` removed as equal to `expired`. When `enacted` is not empty it MUST compare only `pools.*` and `donation`, since every other field depends on phase 5. `[CV-12]`
- Phase 4 MUST add `reserves`, `fees`, the snapshots, the blocks made and the reward update to the comparison and drop the `deltaT` adjustment; phase 5 MUST drop the `enacted` branch. Reserves, fees, snapshots, blocks made, the reward update, the pool distribution and the pparams are not compared in phase 3. `[CV-13]`

**Why** `[CV-11]`: `Decoder[GovState]` skips the tail of the gov state record, and nothing in production holds a ratify result (D36), so the runner parses each state a second time as a borer `Dom.Element` and walks to the pulser and the reward update; a case is 10 KB. **Why** `[CV-12]` as an inclusion list: the least code is `LedgerStateComparison.compare` on the typed decode of `st_out`'s `[certState, utxoState]`, with a phase 3 exclusion list (`fees`, `instantStake`; in an `enacted` case every field but `pools.*` and `donation`) and the two extra checks, the adjusted treasury and the expired set; `utxo` and `dreps.*` then come free, and both must be unchanged at a boundary. Do not write a second comparator. **Why** the rest of `[CV-12]`: in the Imp tests no block is made, so `eta = 0`, `rs` is empty in all 533 cases, and the reward update moves only fees: `deltaT` into the treasury and `deltaR` into the reserves. So the treasury of `st_out` is `treasury + deltaT + donation + unclaimed deposits`: checked on all 427 cases whose `enacted` is empty (2026-11-03), which makes the adjusted treasury compare the check of `[EB-14]` and `[EB-17]`; 7 cases carry a donation, 1 an unclaimed proposal deposit. The 106 cases with an enacted action change the roots, the committee, the constitution, the accounts or the proposals by phase 5 rules. Coverage caveat: no case retires a pool at its boundary (2 cases carry a later `retiring`), so the corpus does not exercise `[EB-8]` and `[EB-9]`; the ported Imp tests of phase 3 acceptance 3 and 6 are the only check of them. 339 cases carry future pool params (`[EB-7]`), 210 bump the dormant counter (`[EB-15]`), 21 expire a proposal (`[EB-12a]`), 6 change `committeeState` (`[EB-16]`).

*Implementation status (phase 3, tests, newepoch-runner):* `[CV-9]` to `[CV-12]` are `NewEpochVectors` (decoder and loader) and `NewEpochConformanceTest` in `scalus-cardano-ledger/jvm/src/test/scala/scalus/testing/conformance/`. The archive `newepoch-vectors.tar.gz` holds the `sig`, `st` and `st_out` files of the 533 cases, 0.72 MB (not 0.9 MB: `ctx` and `env` are left out, each is CBOR `null`); `newepoch-vectors.README.md` beside it records the release, the ledger commit, the license (Apache-2.0), the sha256 of `ledger-rules.tar.gz` and the tar command, and each file matches its sha256 in the release's `corpus-manifest.json`. A missing archive fails the tests, it does not skip them (D65). The decoder reads `st` and `st_out` once with a borer `Reader` for `[CV-10]` (the treasury, the reserves and the existing `LedgerState` decoder) and parses `st` a second time as a `Dom.Element` for `[CV-11]`; it requires a `Complete` reward update with an empty `rs` in every case. The comparison is `LedgerStateComparison.compare` with two exclusion lists of the runner, not the shared `LedgerStateComparison.exclusions` of the tx harness: `fees` and `instantStake` (removed by phase 4), and in an `enacted` case also every other field but `pools.*` and `donation` (removed by phase 5), each entry named by field. The expired check of `[CV-12]` compares the removed ids with `expired` and its descendants, since `rsExpired` holds only the expired ids and EPOCH removes their descendants too (D64). Result: all 531 decoded cases pass, with no `expectedFailures` entry; `test_5226` and `test_5227` do not decode, since their gov state holds a TreasuryWithdrawals amount above `Long.MaxValue` (D44), and take a D37 entry in the runner's `undecodable` map, which the `[CV-9]` test pins. Both enact nothing, so `[CV-11]` pins 106 cases with an enactment and 425 decoded cases without. No case is a Scalus bug and no case shows a 1.23.0.0 difference from 1.22.1.0. One-line mutations of `EpochTransition` (not committed) show each step is checked: POOLREAP off fails 6 cases (`pools.*`, `accounts.balance`, `accounts.stakePoolDelegation`, `deposited`); `[EB-12a]` at `expiresAfter < n` fails 23 (`govState.proposals`, the expired set, `deposited`, `accounts.balance`, one `treasury`); `[EB-15]` off fails 127 (`dreps.numDormantEpochs`); `[EB-16]` off fails 1 (`committeeState`); `[EB-17]` without the donation fails 7 (`donation`, `treasury`); `[EB-18]` off fails 20 (`deposited`); `[EB-7]` without the activation fails 2 (`pools.stakePoolParams`); `[EB-14]` without the refund fails 19 (`accounts.balance`). Correction to the coverage caveat above: 4 cases retire a pool at their boundary (`test_5057`, `test_5072`, `test_6834`, `test_7749`), and 4 more carry a later `retiring`; so the corpus does exercise `[EB-8]` (the refund to a registered account) and `[EB-9]` (2 cases clear a delegation), but no case refunds a pool deposit to the treasury. Only 2 cases change `pools.stakePoolParams` by `[EB-7]`: in the others the future params equal the current ones.

NEWEPOCH runner, rules added in the phase 4 design round (agent, 2026-11-03), each checked on the 533 dumps (2026-11-03):

- The runner MUST also decode from `st` and `st_out`: `blocksPrev` and `blocksCur` (maps from pool id to count); the snapshots `[mark, set, go, fee]`, each snapshot `[activeStake, pools]` with `activeStake` a map from credential to `[stake, pool]` and each pool the 10-element `StakePoolSnapShot` (`K/State/SnapShots.hs:239-253, 290-296, 355-361`), into the types of `[DM-10a]`, `[DM-10b]`; the reward update of `st` as `[deltaT, −deltaR, rs, −deltaF, nonMyopic]`: the ledger encodes `invert deltaR` and `invert deltaF` (`S/RewardUpdate.hs:125-131`, re-inverted at `:140-142`), and `rs` maps a credential to a set of `[type, pool, amount]`, summed per credential; and the previous pparams of `st`, field 4 of the gov state record, with the `ConwayProtocolParams` decoder; if that decoder rejects the 1.23.0.0 pparams array of the dumps, the runner reads elements 8 to 12 (`nOpt`, `a0`, `rho`, `tau`, `protVer`) directly, which is all `[CV-16]` needs. `[CV-14]`
- The runner MUST start the transition from an `EpochState` with the decoded snapshots, blocks made and `pendingRewards = Some(reward update of st)`, and MUST compare with `st_out`, in both branches of `[CV-12]`: `reserves`, `fees`, `snapshots.mark`, `snapshots.set`, `snapshots.go`, `snapshots.fee`, `blocksMadePrev`, `blocksMadeCur`; in the branch with `enacted` empty also the treasury, with no `deltaT` adjustment. `[CV-15]`
- For each case the runner MUST check the reward calculation of `[RW-1]` on `st` against the reward update of `st`: `go`, `fee`, `blocksPrev` and the reserves of `st`, the previous pparams of `[CV-14]`, `Measured`. `[CV-16]`

**Why** `[CV-15]` in both branches: SNAP runs after `[EB-5]` and before POOLREAP, RATIFY and ENACT (`C/Rules/Epoch.hs:291-297`), and only the treasury and the accounts take enacted withdrawals, so the snapshots, the reserves, the fees and the blocks made do not depend on phase 5. **Why** `[CV-16]`: the reward update of `st_out` is `SNothing` in every case, since NEWEPOCH clears `nesRu` at the boundary (`C/Rules/NewEpoch.hs:190`) and RUPD starts later in the epoch, so the `[EB-22]` result of the transition has no expected value in the dump. The update in `st` was computed in the epoch before from fields that do not change inside an epoch: `go`, `fee`, `nesBprev`, the reserves and the previous pparams (`startStep`, `S/LedgerState/PulsingReward.hs:99-212`). Checked by a scratch script (not committed): in all 533 cases `deltaT = floor(tau · fee)`, `deltaR = fee − deltaT`, `deltaF = −fee` and `rs` is empty, with `tau` of the previous pparams; the reserves of `st_out` are those of `st` plus `deltaR`, its fees are those of `st` plus `deltaF`, `st_out.set = st.mark`, `st_out.go = st.set` and `st_out.fee` = the fees of `st` plus `deltaF`. The previous pparams are not the current ones: 105 inputs carry them at PV 2.0, and in 9 cases their `rho` or `tau` differs. `blocksPrev` and `blocksCur` are empty in every case, so `eta = 0` and the dumps test the fee path, the SNAP rotation and the snapshot contents (231 inputs have a non-empty `mark`, 125 a non-empty `go`), not the pool reward formulas; acceptance 1 and 2 of phase 4 test those.

*Implementation status (phase 4, design):* correction to `[CV-13]`: "the reward update" is `[CV-16]`, not a field of `st_out` (D81). The phase 3 exclusions `fees` and the `deltaT` adjustment go; `instantStake` stays excluded in the tx harness and in the runner for good (D5): Scalus keeps no instant stake, and `[CV-15]` checks the stake through `snapshots.mark`. The pool distribution (`nesPd`), `nonMyopic` and the pparams stay out of the comparison; phase 5 decides the pool distribution with `[RT-2]`. The runner passes the current pparams of `st` (gov state field 3) as the pparams of the transition, `Measured`, `activeSlotsCoeff = 1/20` and the mainnet epoch length; none of them changes a compared field. `PoolSnapshot.numDelegators` and `selfDelegatedOwners` are compared too: they come from the accounts at SNAP (`[SN-8]`), and the derived `spssStakeRatio` is compared as `stake / totalActiveStake` by cross-multiplication.

*Implementation status (phase 4, tests, newepoch-rewards-conformance):* `[CV-14]` to `[CV-16]` are in `NewEpochVectors` and `NewEpochConformanceTest`. All 531 decoded cases pass `[CV-12]` and `[CV-15]` with no `expectedFailures` entry, and all 531 pass `[CV-16]`. No case is a Scalus bug, and no case shows a 1.23.0.0 difference from 1.22.1.0. Details:

- The `fees` exclusion is gone. `instantStake` stays, with the cause "never, D5 and D81".
- The snapshot fields are compared as `snapshots.<mark|set|go>.<activeStake|delegations|pools>` and `snapshots.fee`. The blocks made are `blocksMadePrev` and `blocksMadeCur`.
- The decoder checks each `spssStakeRatio` by cross-multiplication against the decoded snapshot, so equal snapshots have equal ratios. The decoder accepts the ratio with or without tag 30.
- The pparams of `[CV-14]` are the mainnet pparams with `nOpt`, `a0`, `rho` and `tau` read from elements 8 to 11 of gov state fields 3 and 4. `protVer` is not read, since the calculation of `[RW-20]` does not depend on it. The runner does not try the `ConwayProtocolParams` decoder first: the direct read is the least code, and it is all `[CV-16]` needs.
- A coverage test pins, per input `st`: 231 non-empty mark stakes, 277 non-empty mark pool maps, 125 non-empty go stakes, 165 non-empty go pool maps, 198 non-zero fee snapshots, 198 non-zero `deltaR`, 0 reward maps and 0 block maps. A decoder that reads empty snapshots fails it.

Mutations, not committed, show each comparison is live:

| Mutation | Cases that fail | Fields |
|---|---|---|
| SNAP off | 385 | `snapshots.*`, `snapshots.fee` (369) |
| `deltaR` sign not flipped back | 198 | `reserves`; `[CV-16]` fails too |
| `deltaF` sign not flipped back | 198 | `fees`, `snapshots.fee` |
| `numDelegators + 1` | 339 | `snapshots.mark.pools` |
| wrong stake ratio check | every case | decode fails |
| `tau` of mainnet in `[CV-16]` | not counted | `[CV-16]` fails |
| current pparams in `[CV-16]` | 0 | none |

The last row is a coverage gap, not a bug: no case where the previous and current pparams differ has a `tau` difference that changes `floor(tau · fee)`. Blocks made are empty in every dump, so no mutation of `[EB-21]` or of the pool reward formulas shows here; `EpochTransitionTest` and `RewardCalculationTest` cover them.

NEWEPOCH runner, rules added in the phase 5 design round (agent, 2026-11-03), each checked on the 533 dumps (2026-11-03):

- The runner MUST decode from `st`: the current pparams (gov state field 3) as `env.params`, the previous pparams (field 4) as `EpochState.prevParams`, both with the `ConwayProtocolParams` decoder (all 1066 pparams of the dumps have 31 elements, its arity); and the `RatifyState` of the pulser (field 6, `[snapshot, ratifyState]`) as `EpochState.pendingRatify`: `ratifyState = [enactState, enacted, expired, delayed]`, `enactState = [committee, constitution, curPParams, prevPParams, treasury, withdrawals, roots]` (`C/Governance/Internal.hs:131-141, 257-275`), the ids of the `enacted` gov action states in order, `expired` the set under tag 258. The phase 4 read of elements 8 to 11 into mainnet pparams goes. `[CV-17]`
- The runner MUST compare with `st_out`, in all decoded cases and with no `enacted` branch: every field of `[CV-12]` and `[CV-15]`, the treasury with no adjustment, the current pparams with field 3, `prevParams` with field 4, and `pendingRatify` with the `RatifyState` of the pulser of `st_out`: `enacted` in order, `expired`, `delayed`, and the committee, constitution, pparams, withdrawals and roots of the enact state. The enact state's treasury and previous pparams MUST NOT be compared (`[DM-12a]`). The expired check of `[CV-12]` and D64 goes, since `expired` is now an input. `[CV-18]`
- The runner MUST compare the distributions of `[RT-3a]` and `[RT-4a]`, computed on the state after the transition, with `psDRepDistr` and `psPoolDistr` of the pulser snapshot of `st_out`, zero entries included. `nesPd` MUST NOT be compared: it is the pool distribution of the mark before SNAP (`pd' = ssStakeMarkPoolDistr (esSnapshots es0)`, `C/Rules/NewEpoch.hs:182`), which only the leader schedule reads (section 17). `[CV-19]`
- The runner MUST pin these 7 cases as D37 `expectedFailures` of `pendingRatify` only, with the cause `[EN-4]` (owner, Q8): `test_5339`, `test_5440`, `test_5461`, `test_6937`, `test_7002`, `test_7480`, `test_7507`, whose `st_out` pulser enacts a HardForkInitiation to 12.0. Their successors `test_5340`, `test_5441`, `test_5462`, `test_6938`, `test_7003`, `test_7481`, `test_7508` consume that pulser and MUST pass: `[EB-13a]` sets the protocol version 12.0, and HARDFORK at 12 is identity on both sides. `[CV-20]`
