**Why** `[CV-17]` to `[CV-19]`: the pulser of `st_out` is the RATIFY run of the state after the boundary with `reCurrentEpoch = sig` (`setFreshDRepPulsingState eNo`, `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:379`), and the dump encodes it finished (`DRComplete`), so every case is a vector of `[EB-20]`. 126 input pulsers enact or expire an action; the input pulsers enact 115 actions (46 ParameterChange, 25 TreasuryWithdrawals, 19 NoConfidence, 11 NewConstitution, 7 HardForkInitiation, 7 UpdateCommittee), and so do the output pulsers; 85 input pulsers are delayed; 6 input enact states carry withdrawals. With `pendingRatify` from `st`, the phase 3 cases `test_5591` and `test_6836` match `st_out` in full. In `test_5591` the pulser enacts ParameterChange `a4e3…#0` (`utxoCostPerByte` 4057) at its last chance and expires its sibling `6eae…#0`; `[EB-12c]` removes `6eae…#0` as expired, then `85ce…#0`, the third proposal with no parent, as a sibling; the proposals are empty, the pparam root is `a4e3…#0`, the current pparams take 4057 (from 4310) and `prevParams` the old ones. In `test_6836` the pulser enacts UpdateCommittee `f3a4…#0` with `delayed = true`; the committee gains member `2360…` (term 924) and threshold 3/4, and `[EB-16]` keeps the cold credentials of the new committee.

*Implementation status (phase 5, design):* corrections to `[CV-13]`: the phase 5 part is `[CV-17]` to `[CV-20]`; "the pool distribution" is the SPO distribution of `[CV-19]`, not `nesPd`. A RATIFY difference between conway 1.22.1.0 and 1.23.0.0 shows in `[CV-18]`; the implementer checks every difference against the 1.22.1.0 source before calling it a bug, and a 1.23.0.0 rule takes a D37 entry by case name.

*Implementation status (phase 5, tests, newepoch-full-conformance):* `[CV-17]` to `[CV-20]` are in `NewEpochVectors` and `NewEpochConformanceTest`. All 531 decoded cases pass with no exclusion but `instantStake` (D5, D81); the 7 cases of `[CV-20]` are the only `expectedFailures`, and each of them differs in `pendingRatify.*` only (in `test_5339`: `enacted`, `delayed`, `params` and `roots`, the HardForkInitiation to 12.0). `test_5591`, `test_6836` and the 7 successors of `[CV-20]` pass in full. No case is a Scalus bug, and no case shows a 1.23.0.0 difference from 1.22.1.0. Details:

- The `enacted` branch of `[CV-12]`, its exclusion list and the D64 expired check are gone. `Case` keeps the reward update of `st` and one `GovStateTail` per side: the current and previous pparams (the `ConwayProtocolParams` decoder and `toProtocolParams`), the `RatifyState` of the pulser and the two distributions of its snapshot. The phase 4 read of elements 8 to 11 into mainnet pparams is gone; `[CV-16]` passes with the full previous pparams.
- `pendingRatify` is compared as `pendingRatify.<enacted|expired|delayed|committee|constitution|params|withdrawals|roots>`. A `[CV-20]` case passes the runner only when every field it differs in starts with `pendingRatify.`, and fails it when it stops differing.
- `[CV-19]` compares `Ratify.dRepDistribution` and the `stake` of `Ratify.poolDistribution` on the state and mark after the transition with `psDRepDistr` and `psPoolDistr`. The dump holds the stake of each pool only (`individualTotalPoolStake`, conway Governance/DRepPulser.hs:399), so the total of `PoolDistribution` is not compared.
- A coverage test pins, per output `st_out`: 106 pulsers enact, 22 expire, 85 are delayed, 6 enact states hold withdrawals, 21 cases change the current pparams, 326 DRep and 336 pool distributions are not empty, 9 hold a DRep entry of 0, and 49 add a proposal deposit to a pool's mark stake (`[RT-4a]`).

Mutations, not committed, show the new comparisons are live:

| Mutation | Cases that fail | Fields |
|---|---|---|
| no proposal deposits in the DRep stake (`[RT-3a]`) | 40 | `dRepDistribution` (40); `pendingRatify.*` (2) |
| no `numDelegators > 0` filter in the pool stake (`[RT-4a]`) | 11 | `poolDistribution` |
| `[EB-13a]` never takes the enact state's pparams | 21 | `params`, `pendingRatify.params` (21); `pendingRatify.enacted` (2) |
| `[EB-13a]` always takes the enact state's pparams (no D87 guard) | 0 | none |

The last row is a coverage gap, not a bug: no dump edits the pparams between the RATIFY run and the boundary, so the enact state's pparams equal the current ones wherever nothing pparam-related is enacted. `EpochTransitionTest` covers the guard (acceptance 13).

*Implementation status (phase 1, review round 1):* the comparison also covers `dreps.numDormantEpochs`. The decoder passes the counter in (`[CV-4]`), and the bump of `[DR-2]` shows in `dreps.expiry`, but the reset of `[DR-3]` shows only in the output counter: seven success vectors fire it (a valid tx with a proposal and a counter of 1 or 2), and 41 more carry a non-zero counter through unchanged. All run dirs pass with the compare.

LEDGER runner, rules added in the phase 6 design round (agent, 2026-11-03). The PV10 vectors of `[CV-1]` and `[CV-2]` carry a verdict and a new state, and no failure. The Dugite corpus (Q5) holds 7 446 LEDGER cases at PV11. Checked on the archive and its `corpus-manifest.json` (2026-11-03): each case has `ctx`, `env`, `st` and `sig`, and `st_out` only when the ledger accepts the tx; 93 cases have no `st_out`; no file names a failure. The dump hook writes the rule's environment, state and signal (`libs/cardano-ledger-conformance/src/Test/Cardano/Ledger/Conformance/ExecSpecRule/Core.hs:262-274`), and Dugite's patch adds only the new state. So these cases test the verdict and the state, not the failure constructors (D99).

- A JVM test `DugiteLedgerConformanceTest` MUST run every LEDGER case of a local `ledger-rules.tar.gz`. The path MUST come from the system property `scalus.dugite.ledgerRules`, else from the environment variable `SCALUS_DUGITE_LEDGER_RULES`. Example: `../dugite-corpus-20261101/ledger-rules.tar.gz`, release `conformance-corpus-v20261101-084724`, sha256 `018c480caad1abf311b7203998d0e905b743d17ee26213edc4575d027faab996`. When neither names an existing file, the test MUST be canceled, not failed. `[CV-21]`
- The runner MUST read the archive as one gzip tar stream and hold one case at a time in memory. It MUST NOT extract the archive, and it MUST skip each `ctx` entry without reading it into memory. `[CV-22]`
- The runner MUST pin 7 446 cases, 93 cases without `st_out`, and 6 918 distinct `(env, st, sig)` triples. `[CV-22a]`
- Per case, the runner MUST decode `env` as `[slot, [epoch], txIx, pparams, [treasury, reserves]]` (`S/Rules/Ledger.hs:116-125`), with the `ConwayProtocolParams` decoder for `pparams`; `st` and `st_out` as `[certState, utxoState]` with the test `LedgerState` decoder; and `sig` as a `Transaction`. `[CV-23]`
- The context of a case MUST take the slot, the pparams and the treasury from `env`. Its `SlotConfig` MUST be that of Imp's genesis: system start 2017-09-23T21:44:51Z, slot length 1 s, epoch length 4 320 (`eras/shelley/impl/testlib/Test/Cardano/Ledger/Shelley/ImpTest.hs:806-814`). The runner MUST check that `epochOf(slot)` equals the `epoch` of `env` in every case. `[CV-23a]`
- The runner MUST run `CardanoMutator.transit`. A case without `st_out` MUST be rejected. A case with `st_out` MUST be accepted, and the new `[certState, utxoState]` MUST equal `st_out` by `LedgerStateComparison.compare`, with the `instantStake` exclusion only (D5, D81). `[CV-24]`
- A case that differs MUST take a D37 `expectedFailures` entry by case name with its cause. The implementer MUST check the difference against the 1.22.1.0 source before calling it a Scalus bug: the corpus pin is conway 1.23.0.0. `[CV-24a]`
- `scalus-cardano-ledger/jvm/src/test/resources/ledger-vectors-pv11.tar.gz` MUST hold a sample of the corpus: the 93 rejected cases, and every accepted case whose five files total less than 30 000 bytes, deduplicated by `(env, st, sig)`, without the `ctx` files. That is 932 cases, at most 2.2 MB. A README beside it MUST record the release, the ledger commit `a88b60bd`, conway 1.23.0.0, the license, the sha256 of `ledger-rules.tar.gz`, the selection rule and the tar command. `[CV-25]`
- A CI test MUST run the sample of `[CV-25]` by `[CV-23]` to `[CV-24a]` and pin 932 cases and 93 rejected ones. A missing sample MUST fail it, as D65 says for the NEWEPOCH archive. `[CV-25a]`

**Why** `[CV-21]` is opt-in: the archive is 1.6 GB, 10.2 GB unpacked, of which the LEDGER `ctx` files are 3.3 GB; it cannot be vendored, and a disk may not hold it unpacked. **Why** `[CV-22]` can stream: the files of a case are adjacent in the tar (checked: 8 059 cases, none split). **Why** `[CV-23a]`: the dumps carry the slot and the epoch, and with epoch length 4 320 `slot / 4 320` gives the epoch of `env` in all 1 429 cases that the size rule of `[CV-25]` selects before deduplication (epochs 899 to 919, the range `[CV-8]` infers for the PV10 vectors). So this runner needs no `[CV-8]` inference and no zero treasury (D37). Every sampled `env` has PV 11.0 and `txIx = 0`, and every sampled tx has `isValid = true`. **Why** `[CV-25]`: the 93 rejected cases are 0.22 MB compressed and the small accepted cases 1.95 MB; 839 of the 1 336 small accepted cases are distinct. The sample keeps the only PV11 rejections in CI.

*Implementation status (phase 6, failure-conformance):* `[CV-21]` to `[CV-25a]` are in `DugiteLedgerVectors` and `DugiteLedgerConformanceTest`. The full archive gives 7 446 cases, 93 without `st_out` and 6 918 distinct triples (`[CV-22a]`), in 24 s; the sample gives 932, 93 and 932. Scalus matches the ledger in every case it can decode: each of the 90 decodable rejected cases is rejected, and each of the 7 349 decodable accepted cases is accepted with an equal state. No case is a Scalus bug. No case shows a 1.23.0.0 difference from 1.22.1.0 in a verdict or a state; D116 is a decoder difference that changes neither. Details:

- The 7 `expectedFailures` entries are decode limits of the harness, each with its cause:
  - `test_5222` to `test_5225`: a TreasuryWithdrawals amount above `Long.MaxValue` (Q9, D44). `test_5222` fails in `sig`, the other three in `st`. All four are accepted cases.
  - `test_5970`, `test_5974`: a rejected tx proposes a hard fork to 13.0, which Scalus does not decode (D116).
  - `test_5968`: a rejected tx proposes a hard fork to 11.2609581943; the minor does not fit `ProtocolVersion.minor: Int` (D116).
- The runner uses the default evaluator mode, `Validate`, unlike the PV10 runner of `[CV-1]`, which uses `EvaluateAndComputeCost`. The dumps come from a ledger run, which enforces the script budgets. The network is `Testnet` (`ImpTest.hs:808`).
- The sample is 637 123 bytes after deduplication, below the 2.2 MB bound of `[CV-25]`. `ledger-vectors-pv11.README.md` holds the selection script; a rebuild is byte-identical, and each of the 3 635 files matches its sha256 in `corpus-manifest.json`.
- A coverage test pins, over the sample: 60 txs with redeemers, 498 with certificates, 104 with proposals, 112 with votes, 7 with withdrawals, 2 with `currentTreasuryValue`, 7 with a donation, and 836 accepted cases whose `st_out` differs from `st`. No sampled tx mints or sets a validity interval. A second pin lists the first failure that Scalus reports for each of the 90 rejected cases, 33 distinct ledger names, `CollectErrors` 24 times.

Mutations, not committed, show which comparisons are live:

| Mutation | Sample cases that fail | Full-archive cases that fail |
|---|---|---|
| compare `st_out` with the input state, not the new one | 836 | not run |
| the Imp slot config of `[CV-8]` (epoch length 100) in the context | 215 | not run |
| a zero treasury in the environment | 0 | 0 |
| `EvaluateAndComputeCost` instead of `Validate` | 0 | 0 |

The last two rows are coverage gaps, not passes: no case tells a zero treasury from the treasury of its `env`, and no tx exceeds a script budget.

### 9.3 Failure lists (phase 6)

The ledger collects every failure of a tx (section 5), and the Imp tests assert the whole list, in order: `submitFailingTxM` compares the `NonEmpty` with `shouldBeExpr` (`eras/shelley/impl/testlib/Test/Cardano/Ledger/Shelley/ImpTest.hs:1306-1316`). Scalus stops at the first failure. These rules add the list next to the first-failure API, which does not change.

- `STS.Mutator` MUST gain `transitAll(validators, mutators, context, state, event): Either[Seq[TransactionException], State]`, and `CardanoMutator` MUST gain `transitAll(context, state, event)` over `DefaultValidators.all` and `DefaultMutators.all`. A `Left` MUST hold every failure of `[FL-2]` to `[FL-5]` and MUST NOT be empty. `[FL-1]`
- `transitAll` MUST run every validator, then every mutator, whatever an earlier one returned. A mutator that fails MUST record its failure and pass its input state to the next mutator. `[FL-2]`
- The `transit` methods MUST keep their behaviour: they stop at the first failure, in the iteration order of the given sets. `transitAll` MUST NOT change what `transit`, `submit`, mempool revalidation and the forge report. `[FL-3]`
- `transitAll` MUST drop every `BadInputsUTxOException`, `BadCollateralInputsUTxOException` and `BadReferenceInputsUTxOException`. The `BadAllInputsUTxOException` of `AllInputsMustBeInUtxoValidator` reports each missing input once, as `BadInputsUTxO` (`B/Rules/Utxo.hs:397`). `[FL-4]`
- When no other failure is left, `transitAll` MUST keep the failures that `[FL-4]` drops, so that the list of `[FL-1]` is not empty. Only a rule set without `AllInputsMustBeInUtxoValidator` reaches this case. `[FL-4a]`
- `transitAll` MUST call a `private[ledger]` collecting entry of `PlutusScriptsTransactionMutator` that knows whether the list is empty so far. The entry MUST report the collect errors of `[FL-10]` in every case, and MUST evaluate the scripts only when the list is empty, as `whenFailureFree` does (`B/Rules/Utxos.hs:184, 255`; `libs/small-steps/src/Control/State/Transition/Extended.hs:482-487, 699-702`). The evaluator MUST expose its collect step apart from the evaluation, `private[ledger]`. `[FL-5]`
- `TransactionException.ledgerNames(ex): Seq[String]` MUST give the cardano-ledger predicate-failure constructors that `ex` stands for, at the level that Imp's `injectFailure` takes, for example `FeeTooSmallUTxO`, `MissingRedeemers`, `CollectErrors`. It MUST give one name per constructor that the fields of `ex` show, sorted by `[FL-7]`. An exception with no ledger counterpart (`IllegalArgumentException`, the bare `MetadataException`) MUST give `Seq(ruleName(ex))`. `ruleName`, and so `SubmitError.rule`, MUST NOT change for an existing exception. `[FL-6]`
- The order of the list MUST be the order of the ledger's interpreter: a rule's list is the reverse of the order in which its own predicates and its sub-rules fail, and each sub-rule adds its own list, so reversed again (`Extended.hs:708, 721`). For a Conway tx with at most one certificate, the LEDGER list is: (1) the UTXOW checks in source order (`B/Rules/Utxow.hs:346-388`); (2) the UTXOS failures (`B/Rules/Utxos.hs:184-195, 255-266`); (3) the UTXO checks in reverse source order (`B/Rules/Utxo.hs:378-434`); (4) the GOV checks in source order (`C/Rules/Gov.hs:489-620`); (5) the DELEG, POOL or GOVCERT checks of the certificate in source order; (6) the LEDGER checks in reverse source order (`C/Rules/Ledger.hs:394-416`, `S/Rules/Ledger.hs:361-369`). The errors of one predicate keep their order inside it in (1), (2), (4) and (5), and are reversed in (3) and (6). `[FL-7]`
- `[FL-7]` MUST be one static list of the constructor names in that order. `ledgerNames` MUST sort by it. `transitAll` MUST sort its list stably by the position of the first name of each exception. `[FL-7a]`
- The list of `[FL-1]` MAY differ from the ledger's only in these known differences, and a test that meets one MUST name it: (a) a check that the ledger computes on the inputs it finds and that a Scalus validator skips when an input is missing (`[FL-4]`); (b) several failing certificates, which the ledger interleaves per certificate (`C/Rules/Certs.hs:245-250`) and Scalus reports as one group in source order; (c) the state after a failing certificate: the ledger runs the later certificates and GOV on the state after the certificates that passed, and Scalus, by `[FL-2]`, runs GOV on the state before all of them; (d) several failing proposals or votes, which Scalus groups by constructor (D110); (e) a withdrawal failure, after which the GOVCERT and VRF checks inside `CertsMutator` are not listed (D113); (f) a pool registration that fails several POOL checks, of which Scalus lists one (D117). `[FL-11]`
- `EmulatorBase` MUST gain `ledgerFailures(transaction: Transaction): Seq[TransactionException]`: `transitAll` of the emulator's `validators` and `mutators` on the context and the ledger state that `submit` validates against. It MUST be empty when the ledger rules accept the tx, and it MUST NOT change the state. `EmulatorJavaApi` MUST gain `getLedgerFailureNames(transaction): java.util.List[String]`, and `JEmulator` MUST gain `ledgerFailures(txCborBytes: Uint8Array): string[]`; both give the names of `[FL-6]`, flattened, in the order of `[FL-7]`. `[FL-8]`
- `PlutusScriptsTransactionMutator` MUST reject a tx with `isValid = false` whose scripts all pass with a new `TransactionException.PassedUnexpectedlyException(transactionId)` (`B/Rules/Utxos.hs:255-260`). Its `ruleName` MUST be `PassedUnexpectedly`, and its `ledgerNames` MUST be `ValidationTagMismatch`. `PlutusScriptValidationException` stays the `FailedUnexpectedly` case, so `SubmitError.ScriptFailure` does not change. `[FL-9]`
- A collect error MUST be a new `TransactionException.CollectErrorsException(transactionId, errors: Seq[CollectError])`, with `CollectError` an enum of `NoRedeemer(tag: RedeemerTag, index: Int)`, `NoCostModel(language: Language)` and `BadTranslation(message: String)`. Its `ruleName` and its `ledgerNames` MUST be `CollectErrors`. The errors MUST follow `merge` of `collectPlutusScriptsWithContext` (`A/Plutus/Evaluate.hs:157-214`): after the first error only `NoRedeemer` errors are added, each in front. The failure of `[TX-1]` MUST be a `BadTranslation`, and the Plutus mutator MUST keep making it a phase-1 rejection whatever `isValid` says. `CollectError` MUST NOT have `NoWitness`: `collectPlutusScriptsWithContext` drops a needed script that the tx does not provide (`mapMaybe`, `A/Plutus/Evaluate.hs:178-179`), so Conway never raises it. `[FL-10]`

**Why** `[FL-3]`: the first failure of `transit` is not the ledger's first one: `DefaultValidators.all` is sorted by name (`R/DefaultValidators.scala:10-43`), so `AllInputsMustBeInUtxoValidator` runs first. The Emulator depends on that. Mempool revalidation drops a tx as `InputsSpent` when the error maps to `UtxoNotAvailable` (`N/emulator/TxStatus.scala:68-71`), and `[FL-7]` puts the UTXOW checks, `ValueNotConservedUTxO` and every later UTXO check before `BadInputsUTxO`. Reordering would change the reported error of every tx with two failures (D100). **Why** `[FL-4]`: eight validators return a `Bad*InputsUTxOException` of their own when an input is missing (`R/MissingRequiredDatumsValidator.scala:9`, `R/ExactSetOfRedeemersValidator.scala:8`, `R/FeesOkValidator.scala:19`, `R/MissingKeyHashesValidator.scala:8`, `R/NativeScriptsValidator.scala:8`, `R/ProtocolParamsViewHashesMatchValidator.scala:11`, `R/ValueNotConservedUTxOValidator.scala:15`, `R/MissingOrExtraScriptHashesValidator.scala:8`), and the Plutus mutator a `BadCollateralInputsUTxOException`. Each repeats a part of `BadAllInputsUTxO`. The ledger computes those checks on the inputs it finds instead: in `Test/Cardano/Ledger/Shelley/UnitTests.hs:329-333` (shelley test-suite), a missing input gives `[ValueNotConservedUTxO, BadInputsUTxO]`, and Scalus gives `[BadInputsUTxO]` (`[FL-11]` (a), D105). **Why** `[FL-5]`: the interpreter skips the evaluation when any earlier rule of the tx failed, since a sub-rule inherits the failing state of its parent (`Extended.hs:689-695, 713-715`); `CollectErrors` is outside `whenFailureFree`, so it is reported in every case. **Why** `[FL-6]`: `SubmitError.rule` is documented as stable enough to assert on in a test (`N/BlockchainProvider.scala:339-362`).

*Example, normative test vectors,* for `[FL-7]`, from Imp lists at PV 11:

| Imp test | List |
|---|---|
| Alonzo UTXOW "MissingRedeemers" (`eras/alonzo/impl/testlib/Test/Cardano/Ledger/Alonzo/Imp/UtxowSpec/Invalid.hs:72-84`) | `MissingRedeemers`, `CollectErrors [NoRedeemer]` |
| Alonzo UTXOW "Redeemer with incorrect purpose" (`:166-186`) | `ExtraRedeemers`, `MissingRedeemers`, `CollectErrors [NoRedeemer]` |
| Babbage UTXOW "ExtraRedeemers/RedeemerPointerPointsToNothing" (`eras/babbage/impl/testlib/Test/Cardano/Ledger/Babbage/Imp/UtxowSpec/Invalid.hs:94-107`) | `ExtraRedeemers`, `CollectErrors [BadTranslation]` |
| Conway CERTS "Withdrawing from an unregistered staking address" (`eras/conway/impl/testlib/Test/Cardano/Ledger/Conway/Imp/CertsSpec.hs:33-90`, two submissions) | `ConwayWithdrawalsMissingAccounts`, `ConwayWdrlNotDelegatedToDRep` |
| Conway GOV "VotersDoNotExist" (`eras/conway/impl/testlib/Test/Cardano/Ledger/Conway/Imp/GovSpec.hs:746-760`) | `UnelectedCommitteeVoters`, `VotersDoNotExist` |

The rows show (1) before (2), (6) reversed (`validateWithdrawalsDelegated` at `C/Rules/Ledger.hs:410` runs before `testIncompleteAndMissingWithdrawals` at `:416`), and (4) in source order (`C/Rules/Gov.hs:489` before `:616`). The shelley unit test of the `[FL-4]` note shows (3) reversed: `BadInputsUTxO` is checked before `ValueNotConservedUTxO` (`B/Rules/Utxo.hs:397, 400`; Shelley `S/Rules/Utxo.hs:380, 391`) and comes after it in the list.

Names of `[FL-6]` where the Scalus exception name differs from the ledger constructor, illustrative; the implementer completes the table from the ledger source of each validator:

| Scalus exception | `ledgerNames` |
|---|---|
| `BadAllInputsUTxOException` | `BadInputsUTxO` |
| `EmptyInputsException` | `InputSetEmptyUTxO` |
| `NonDisjointInputsAndReferenceInputsException` | `BabbageNonDisjointRefInputs` |
| `FeesOkException` | the subset of `FeeTooSmallUTxO`, `ScriptsNotPaidUTxO`, `CollateralContainsNonADA`, `InsufficientCollateral`, `IncorrectTotalCollateralField`, `NoCollateralInputs` its fields show |
| `InvalidTransactionSizeException` | `MaxTxSizeUTxO` |
| `ExUnitsExceedMaxException` | `ExUnitsTooBigUTxO` |
| `OutputsHaveNotEnoughCoinsException` | `BabbageOutputTooSmallUTxO` |
| `OutputsHaveTooBigValueStorageSizeException` | `OutputTooBigUTxO` |
| `MissingKeyHashesException` | `MissingVKeyWitnessesUTXOW` |
| `InvalidSignaturesInWitnessesException` | `InvalidWitnessesUTXOW` |
| `MissingOrExtraScriptHashesException` | `MissingScriptWitnessesUTXOW`, `ExtraneousScriptWitnessesUTXOW` |
| `NativeScriptsException` | `ScriptWitnessNotValidatingUTXOW` |
| `InvalidScriptDataHashException` | `ScriptIntegrityHashMismatch` |
| `WithdrawalsNotInRewardsException` | `ConwayIncompleteWithdrawals`, `ConwayWithdrawalsMissingAccounts` |
| `WithdrawalsNotDelegatedToDRepException` | `ConwayWdrlNotDelegatedToDRep` |
| `TreasuryValueMismatchException`, `TxRefScriptsSizeTooBigException` | `ConwayTreasuryValueMismatch`, `ConwayTxRefScriptsSizeTooBig` |
| `PoolMetadataHashTooBigException` | `PoolMedataHashTooBig` (the ledger's spelling, `S/Rules/Pool.hs:249-254`) |
| `GovException` | `failure.productPrefix`, as `ruleName` (D31) |
| `PlutusScriptValidationException`, `PassedUnexpectedlyException` | `ValidationTagMismatch` |

*Implementation status (phase 6, failure-accumulation):* `[FL-1]` to `[FL-10]` are done. `STS.Mutator.transitAll` and `CardanoMutator.transitAll` are in `R/Entities.scala` and `R/CardanoMutator.scala`; they filter the per-kind certificate mutators as `transit` does (`[SC-22]`). The collecting entries are `GovMutator.transitAll` (`private[rules]`, every GOV failure in source order; a failing proposal is still added when its parent allows it, `C/Rules/Gov.hs:574-578`) and `PlutusScriptsTransactionMutator.transit(context, state, event, failureFree)`. The collect step is `PlutusScriptEvaluator.collectErrors` (D112): per needed Plutus script, the redeemer, then the cost model, then the TxInfo of its language and its purpose; any exception of the context build is a `BadTranslation` with its message. Plutus V4 is not collected. A missing datum of a PlutusV1 or PlutusV2 spend stays an `IllegalArgumentException` of the evaluation. `EmulatorBase.ledgerFailures` runs `transitAll` on the snapshot and the slot of `submit`, without the mempool check of `TxRefScriptsSizeValidator.check`. Corrections to the names table, by the ledger source: `OutputBootAddrAttrsTooBigException` is `OutputBootAddrAttrsTooBig`, a constructor that Conway UTXO keeps (`C/Rules/Utxo.hs:122, 432`), not `OutputTooBigUTxO` (the section 5.3 row and its "never raised in Conway" row were wrong on this, and are now fixed); `MissingOrExtraScriptHashesException` is `ExtraneousScriptWitnessesUTXOW` before `MissingScriptWitnessesUTXOW`, since `babbageMissingScripts` checks the extraneous scripts first (`B/Rules/Utxow.hs:219-222`); the DELEG balance failure is `StakeKeyHasNonZeroAccountBalanceDELEG` (`C/Rules/Deleg.hs:271`). Group (5) is one order that every certificate kind keeps: `DepositIncorrectDELEG`, `StakeKeyRegisteredDELEG`, `RefundIncorrectDELEG`, `StakeKeyHasNonZeroAccountBalanceDELEG`, `DelegateeStakePoolNotRegisteredDELEG`, `DelegateeDRepNotRegisteredDELEG`, `StakeKeyNotRegisteredDELEG`, then POOL, then GOVCERT. `transitAll` keeps one of two equal failures (D111). Known differences (d) to (f) of `[FL-11]` are recorded in D110, D113 and D117. The collect step of a tx with a missing input is empty (D109, `[FL-11]` (a)). Tests: `LedgerNamesTest` (shared, acceptance 5, the table of every case and the order of `[FL-7]`), `LedgerFailuresImpTest` (shared, JVM and JS: the five Imp lists, the three single-failure lists, `[FL-5]`, `[FL-3]`, the `[FL-4]` vector, `[FL-8]`), `GovMutatorTest` (`[FL-1]` for GOV, and a failing proposal that a vote and a child proposal of the same tx still name), `PlutusScriptsTransactionMutatorTest` (`[FL-9]`, `[FL-10]` with the `merge` order and the ledger order of the needed scripts, `[TX-1]` now `CollectErrors`), `JavaEmulatorInteropTest` and `JEmulatorChainTest` (acceptance 7). A mutation that evaluates the scripts after an earlier failure fails the two `[FL-5]` tests: one with a validator failure, one with a GOV failure. The `[FL-3]` and `[FL-4]` txs have no scripts, so they do not catch it. `collectErrors` folds the needed scripts in the ledger order, Spend, Reward, Cert, Mint, Voting, Proposing (`C/UTxO.hs:64-70`), and keeps the index order inside each tag. The evaluation order does not change. The two Conway `NoCostModel` rows are ported with the cost models of PlutusV1 and PlutusV2 only, so the lookup key of the language is tested: the minting row `UtxosSpec.hs:536-548` and the guardrails row `:516-534`, a `ParameterChange` whose PlutusV3 policy runs with purpose Proposing. A test without `AllInputsMustBeInUtxoValidator` pins `[FL-4a]`. Acceptance item 6, the Dugite runner, is another item of phase 6.

## 10. Bookkeeping rules (phase 1)

- The tx rules MUST add the tx deposits to `State.deposited` and subtract the tx refunds, for `isValid = true`, computed against the cert state before CERTS. `[BK-1]`
- The deposits and refunds of `[BK-1]` MUST be the values that `TxBalance` uses (`L/utils/TxBalance.scala:33-109`), proposal deposits included. `[BK-2]`
- DELEG MUST keep `DRepState.delegates` equal to the set of accounts delegated to that DRep. `[BK-3]`
- POOL MUST keep `vrfKeyHashes` as `S/Rules/Pool.hs:276-310` does, and MUST NOT write it at PV < 11. `[BK-4]`
- POOL MUST reject a new pool whose VRF key is in `vrfKeyHashes` at PV ≥ 11. `[BK-5]`
- POOL MUST reject a re-registration whose VRF key differs from its own and is in `vrfKeyHashes` at PV ≥ 11. `[BK-6]`
- The Emulator MUST fill `vrfKeyHashes` from the seeded pools at genesis. `[BK-7]`
- `[BK-5]` and `[BK-6]` MUST run per certificate, in tx order, against the state the earlier certificates of the same tx produced. `[BK-8]`

**Why** `[BK-7]`: the ledger fills the map only on the hard fork into PV11 (`C/Rules/HardFork.hs:107-125`). An Emulator that starts at PV11 never sees that hard fork.

**Why** `[BK-8]`: the ledger runs one POOL step per certificate (`C/Rules/Certs.hs:249-251`). A tx that registers two new pools with one VRF key fails on the second.

*Implementation status (phase 1):* `[BK-1]` said "the UTXO mutator". That cannot work in Scalus: the ledger computes the deposit change against the cert state before CERTS (`S/Rules/Utxo.hs:607-608`; the refund of an unregistered account or DRep, and the pool deposit by `psStakePools` membership, `C/State/CertState.hs:121-123`; LEDGER passes that state to UTXOW on purpose, `C/Rules/Ledger.hs:457-466`). Scalus runs `CertsMutator` before `PlutusScriptsTransactionMutator` (`R/DefaultValidators.scala:53-55`), and `UtxoEnv.certState` is set when the `Context` is built, never refreshed per tx, and read by no rule, so it is stale in the Emulator. So `CertsMutator` writes `deposited`: it is the one mutator that holds the pre-CERTS state (D16). Details: a tx with proposals and no certificate changes `deposited` (`conwayTotalDepositsTxBody` adds `conwayProposalsDeposits`, `C/TxBody.hs:373-391`), so the update runs before the empty-certificates early return of `applyCertificates`; the change can be negative, and `Coin(Long)` has no sign check, so `deposited + deposits − refunds` in that order is exact; a tx with `isValid = false` leaves `deposited` as it is (the Babbage invalid path writes only the UTxO and the fees, `B/Rules/Utxo.hs:497-510`), which the `!event.isValid` early return of `CertsMutator` already gives.

*Implementation status (phase 1):* `[BK-4]` semantics as read from `S/Rules/Pool.hs:266-310`: a new pool does `Map.insert vrf 1`, an overwrite, not a count; every re-registration runs the update, whatever its VRF key: no future params → insert the new VRF key with 1; future params with a different VRF key → insert the new one and delete the future one, even when that is the VRF key of the current params; the same VRF key → no change; otherwise the VRF key of the current params stays in the map until POOLREAP (`S/Rules/PoolReap.hs:225-228`, phase 3). Only the hard-fork fill counts occurrences (`insertWith (+1)`, `C/Rules/HardFork.hs:107-125`), and `[BK-7]` copies that: two seeded pools with one VRF key give the count 2. At PV < 11 POOL does not touch the map (`updateVRFKeyHash = id`), so the PV10 vectors keep the map of their old state. The VRF key type is `VrfKeyHash` (`SC/Hashes.scala:126`), the type of `Certificate.PoolRegistration.vrfKeyHash`; the count is a `Long` (D18). The failure is a new `VrfKeyHashAlreadyRegisteredException`, not a field of `StakePoolException`, as the phase 0 `PoolMetadataHashTooBigException` (D19). The check runs in the POOL step of `CertsMutator`, which gains an error channel for it (`private[rules]`, no MiMa effect), gated on `context.env.params.protocolVersion.major >= 11` as `R/InputsAndReferenceInputsDisjointValidator.scala:14-15` does.

*Implementation status (phase 1, code):* `[BK-4]` to `[BK-8]` live in `CertsMutator.pool` (`R/CertsMutator.scala`); the deprecated `StakePoolCertificatesMutator` uses the same step, so its `Error` gains `VrfKeyHashAlreadyRegisteredException`. `[BK-6]` compares with the VRF key of the current params, not of the future ones (`S/Rules/Pool.hs:284-286`), so a second re-registration in one epoch with the same fresh VRF key fails: the first one put that key in the map. Tests pin this, and the delete of a future VRF key equal to the current one, in `CertsMutatorVrfKeyHashesTest`. `[BK-7]` fills the map at genesis, counting the seeded pools only, since a genesis has no future params. *Superseded by `[BK-7a]` (D92):* the fill in `EmulatorBase.buildInitialState` is gated on major >= 11. `ruleName` names the failure `VRFKeyHashAlreadyRegistered`, the ledger's spelling.

*Implementation status (phase 1):* `[BK-3]` writes `delegates` in the DELEG step as `C/Rules/Deleg.hs:324-373` and `C/State/VState.hs:120-143` do: a new vote delegation to a credential DRep adds the account; a changed one removes it from the old DRep and adds it to the new; `UnregCert` removes it; `AlwaysAbstain` and `AlwaysNoConfidence` have no set. The PV9 reverse-delegation bug (`processDelegationInternal (pvMajor pv < 10)`, ledger issue 4772) is not emulated (D20). `UnregDRepCert` keeps clearing the accounts by their `dRepDelegation` (`R/CertsMutator.scala:215-227`, spec `[SC-21]`), not by the `delegates` set (D21). The pool delegator set (`spsDelegators`) has no Scalus field and no phase (D22).

*Implementation status (phase 1, code):* `[BK-1]` and `[BK-2]` are `TxBalance.depositChange` (deposits from `conwayTotalDepositsTxCerts` and `producedProposalDeposits`, refunds from `conwayTotalRefundsTxCerts`, the functions of `consumedExact` and `producedExact`), added in `CertsMutator.transit` against the cert state before the withdrawal drain. The Emulator genesis starts `deposited` at the deposits of its seeded registrations (D26). `[BK-3]` is `CertsMutator.deleg`, which now works on the whole `CertState`; `applyCertificate` passes the DReps it writes on to GOVCERT, so a vote delegation and the DRep's unregistration in one tx clear both. The deprecated `StakeCertificatesMutator` writes `delegates` too, but not `deposited`. Tests: `CertsMutatorBookkeepingTest`, `EmulatorGenesisDepositedTest`; the conformance vectors cover both fields (with either update removed, 22 or more vectors fail).

### 10.1 DRep expiry

- GOVCERT MUST set the expiry of a registered or updated DRep to `currentEpoch + dRepActivity − numDormantEpochs`. `[DR-1]`
- LEDGER MUST run, for a valid tx with at least one proposal, the dormant bump of `C/Rules/Certs.hs:313-333` before CERTS. `[DR-2]`
- The dormant bump MUST add `numDormantEpochs` to the expiry of every DRep whose `expiry + numDormantEpochs ≥ currentEpoch`, then set `numDormantEpochs` to 0. `[DR-3]`
- LEDGER MUST set, after `[DR-2]` and before CERTS, the expiry of each registered DRep that votes in the tx by `[DR-1]`. `[DR-4]`
- `CertsMutator` MUST run `[DR-2]` and `[DR-4]` after the withdrawal drain and before the cert loop (`R/CertsMutator.scala:20-34`). `[DR-4a]`
- The Emulator MUST give a seeded DRep the expiry `genesisEpoch + dRepActivity`. `[DR-5]`
- At PV 9 a DRep registration MUST get the expiry `currentEpoch + dRepActivity`, without the dormant subtraction; an update subtracts at every PV. `[DR-1a]`
- The Emulator MUST seed `numDormantEpochs` with 0. `[DR-6]`

**Why** `[DR-1a]`: `computeDRepExpiryVersioned` (`C/Rules/GovCert.hs:281-309`) skips the subtraction in the bootstrap phase for `ConwayRegDRep` only; `ConwayUpdateDRep` and the vote bump call `computeDRepExpiry` directly. The PV10 vectors and the PV11 Emulator always subtract.

**Why** `[DR-6]`: the ledger genesis `VState` has `EpochNo 0` (`C/State/VState.hs:75-76`). Nothing increments the counter before the EPOCH rule of phase 3, so in the Emulator the dormant bump of `[DR-2]` is a no-op until then; the vectors and the unit tests of the example below exercise it.

*Implementation status (phase 1):* `[DR-5]` is `context.slotConfig.epochOf(context.env.slot) + context.env.params.dRepActivity` in `buildInitialState` (`N/EmulatorBase.scala:754-789`), no new API. `[DR-4]` changes only DReps that are in `dreps` (`Map.adjust`); an unregistered voter is a GOV failure of phase 2. The order of `[DR-2]`, `[DR-4]` and the drain does not matter: they write disjoint fields (`C/Rules/Ledger.hs:419-421` runs bump, vote, drain). The `numDormantEpochs` that `[DR-4]` reads is the value after `[DR-2]`, so a tx with proposals and votes uses 0.

*Implementation status (phase 1, code):* `[DM-3]` is `VotingState.numDormantEpochs`, with a deprecated 1-argument constructor and `apply`, `VotingState.empty` and one MiMa filter for the 1-argument `copy` (D17). `[DR-1]`, `[DR-1a]` are in `CertsMutator.govCert`, which takes the counter of the state it runs on; `[DR-1a]` gates on `protocolVersion.major == 9`, as `hardforkConwayBootstrapPhase` does. `[DR-2]` to `[DR-4a]` are `CertsMutator.dormantDRepExpiries` and `votingDRepExpiries`, run in `transit` before the cert loop for every valid tx; the ledger runs them in the empty-certificates base case of CERTS at PV ≤ 10 (`C/Rules/Certs.hs:228-245`), which is also before any certificate, so one code path serves both. A counter of 0 leaves the state as it is, as `updateDormantDRepExpiry` does. The expiry subtraction is not clamped at 0 (the ledger's `binOpEpochNo (-)` on `Word64`); it needs `numDormantEpochs > currentEpoch + dRepActivity`, which no state reaches. `[DR-5]`, `[DR-6]` are in `EmulatorBase.buildInitialState`. `[CV-3]`: the `dreps.expiry` exclusion is gone except for two `UTXOS.PlutusV3 Initialization.Updating CostModels` vectors, named in full (`and setting the govPolicy afterwards succeeds`, `with alwaysFails govPolicy does not validate`), whose DRep votes come after the Imp test passed epochs; the third CostModels vector (`but no PlutusV3 CostModels fails`) votes before its `passNEpochs` (`UtxosSpec.hs:516-534`) and passes with no exclusion; the dump has no epoch, the harness runs every case at epoch 899 (`CardanoLedgerVectors.impEpoch`), and the `[DR-4]` expiry is 2 to 6 epochs short. That is a harness limit, not a phase 3 gap: the exclusion is scoped by vector name and goes when the harness knows each case's epoch (`[CV-8]`, phase 2). Tests: `CertsMutatorDRepExpiryTest` (the example table, the PV 9 rule, the bump boundary, a vote together with a proposal, a registration after the bump), `EmulatorGenesisDRepExpiryTest`.

*Example, illustrative,* `dRepActivity = 20`:

| Event | Epoch | `numDormantEpochs` before | DRep expiry after |
|---|---|---|---|
| register | 100 | 0 | 120 |
| 3 epochs with no active proposal | 103 | 3 | 120 (stored) |
| vote | 103 | 3 | 103 + 20 − 3 = 120 |
| tx with a proposal | 104 | 3 | 120 + 3 = 123; dormant := 0 |

## 11. Governance tx rules (phase 2)

### 11.1 GOV

- The tx rules MUST include a GOV validator and a GOV mutator that implement `C/Rules/Gov.hs:456-643`. `[GV-1]`
- GOV MUST see the cert state after CERTS. `[GV-2]`
- The GOV checks MUST run in the mutator chain, after `CertsMutator`, as the GOVCERT DRep checks do today (`R/CertsMutator.scala:153`). `[GV-2a]`

**Why** `[GV-2a]`: `STS.Mutator.transit` runs all validators before all mutators (`R/Entities.scala:213-223`). A validator sees only the pre-tx state, so it cannot see same-tx registrations, which GOV accepts (`C/Rules/Ledger.hs:439-451`).

- GOV MUST check each proposal in tx order, with the checks of `C/Rules/Gov.hs:494-578`. `[GV-3]`
- GOV MUST give each proposal the id `(txId, index in the tx)`. `[GV-4]`
- GOV MUST set `proposedIn = currentEpoch` and `expiresAfter = currentEpoch + govActionLifetime`. `[GV-5]`
- GOV MUST accept a `prevActionId` equal to the purpose root or to a pending proposal of the same purpose, including earlier proposals of the same tx. `[GV-6]`
- GOV MUST reject a `ParameterChange` that fails `ppuWellFormed`, including `nOpt = 0` at PV ≥ 11. `[GV-7]`
- GOV MUST reject a `ParameterChange` or `TreasuryWithdrawals` whose policy hash differs from the constitution's guardrails hash. `[GV-8]`
- GOV MUST check votes only after it adds the tx's proposals. `[GV-9]`
- GOV MUST check `GovActionsDoNotExist`, `VotingOnExpiredGovAction` and `DisallowedVoters` only over known voters. `[GV-10]`
- GOV MUST use the voter permission table of `C/Governance/Internal.hs:381-540`. `[GV-11]`
- GOV MUST reject at PV ≥ 11 a committee vote whose hot credential is not authorized by a cold credential in `committee.members`. `[GV-12]`
- GOV MUST NOT use the member term in `[GV-12]`. `[GV-13]`
- GOV MUST replace an earlier vote of the same voter on the same action. `[GV-14]`
- GOV MUST remove the votes of a DRep that the tx unregisters from all proposals. `[GV-15]`
- The GOV mutator MUST NOT run for `isValid = false`. `[GV-16]`

*Example, illustrative,* for `[GV-10]`: an unknown DRep votes on an unknown action. The ledger raises only `VotersDoNotExist`, not `GovActionsDoNotExist` (`C/Rules/Gov.hs:603-614`).

*Example, illustrative,* for `[GV-13]`: a member whose term ended in epoch 50 still votes in epoch 60 without a GOV failure. RATIFY ignores the vote (`[RT-7]`).

Rules added in the phase 2 design round (agent, 2026-11-03), each read from `C/Rules/Gov.hs:456-643` at the pin:

- The checks of `[GV-3]` for one proposal MUST run in this order, and the first failure is the one Scalus reports: `ProposalCantFollow` (`[GV-17]`), `MalformedProposal` (`[GV-7]`), `ProposalReturnAccountDoesNotExist`, `TreasuryWithdrawalReturnAccountsDoNotExist`, `ProposalDepositIncorrect`, `ProposalProcedureNetworkIdMismatch`, then by action: TreasuryWithdrawals `TreasuryWithdrawalsNetworkIdMismatch`, `InvalidGuardrailsScriptHash`, `ZeroTreasuryWithdrawals`; UpdateCommittee `ConflictingCommitteeUpdate`, `ExpirationEpochTooSmall` (`[GV-18]`); ParameterChange `InvalidGuardrailsScriptHash`; then `InvalidPrevGovActionId` (`[GV-6]`). `[GV-3a]`

  *Implementation status (phase 6):* the order holds among the `GovMutator` checks and in `transitAll`. `transit` reports `ProposalDepositIncorrect` first whatever fails with it, since `ProposalDepositValidator` is a validator and `transit` runs every validator before any mutator (D30). `GovMutatorTest` "spec [GV-3a]: transit reports a bad deposit first, transitAll in the ledger order" pins both.
- The return-account checks of `[GV-3a]` MUST test registration against the accounts after CERTS, for the proposal's return account and for every withdrawal account of a TreasuryWithdrawals. `[GV-3b]`
- `ppuWellFormed` of `[GV-7]` MUST reject an update when any of these holds: `maxBlockBodySize`, `maxTxSize`, `maxBlockHeaderSize`, `maxValueSize`, `collateralPercentage`, `committeeTermLimit`, `governanceActionValidityPeriod`, `poolDeposit`, `governanceActionDeposit` or `drepDeposit` is given as 0; `adaPerUtxoByte` is given as 0 at PV ≥ 10; `nOpt` is given as 0 at PV ≥ 11; no field is given. `[GV-7a]`
- A voter MUST count as known for `[GV-10]` when: a committee hot credential is the `HotCredential` of any entry of `committeeState`, membership and term not required; a DRep credential is in `dreps` after CERTS; a pool id is in `stakePools` after CERTS. `[GV-10a]`
- GOV MUST check the votes in this order: at PV ≥ 11 `UnelectedCommitteeVoters` over every committee voter of the tx, before the proposals; then after the proposals `VotersDoNotExist`, `GovActionsDoNotExist`, `VotingOnExpiredGovAction` (`expiresAfter < currentEpoch`), `DisallowedVoters`. `[GV-10b]`
- A ParameterChange changes a security-group parameter for `[GV-11]` when the update gives any of: `minFeeA`, `minFeeB`, `maxBlockBodySize`, `maxTxSize`, `maxBlockHeaderSize`, `adaPerUtxoByte`, `maxBlockExUnits`, `maxValueSize`, `governanceActionDeposit`, `minFeeRefScriptCoinsPerByte`. `[GV-11a]`
- GOV MUST reject a HardForkInitiation whose version cannot follow its predecessor: when `prevActionId` equals the hard-fork root, or the proposed major is above the current major plus one, the predecessor is the current pparams version; otherwise it is the version of the pending HardForkInitiation that `prevActionId` names. `new` can follow `cur` when `new.major = cur.major + 1 ∧ new.minor = 0`, or `new.major = cur.major ∧ new.minor = cur.minor + 1`. `[GV-17]`
- `[GV-17]` MUST look the predecessor up in the proposals before the tx only; a HardForkInitiation earlier in the same tx is not consulted, and the proposal then passes to `[GV-6]`. `[GV-17a]`
- GOV MUST reject an UpdateCommittee that adds and removes one credential (`ConflictingCommitteeUpdate`), or adds a member whose term is at most `currentEpoch` (`ExpirationEpochTooSmall`). `[GV-18]`
- `TransactionException.ruleName` MUST return the ledger constructor name for every GOV failure: `GovActionsDoNotExist`, `MalformedProposal`, `ProposalProcedureNetworkIdMismatch`, `TreasuryWithdrawalsNetworkIdMismatch`, `DisallowedVoters`, `ConflictingCommitteeUpdate`, `ExpirationEpochTooSmall`, `InvalidPrevGovActionId`, `VotingOnExpiredGovAction`, `ProposalCantFollow`, `InvalidGuardrailsScriptHash`, `VotersDoNotExist`, `ZeroTreasuryWithdrawals`, `ProposalReturnAccountDoesNotExist`, `TreasuryWithdrawalReturnAccountsDoNotExist`, `UnelectedCommitteeVoters`. `ProposalDepositIncorrect` keeps its existing exception and name. `[GV-19]`
- GOV MUST read the committee, the constitution's guardrails hash and the roots from the gov state before the tx. `[GV-20]`

**Why** `[GV-17a]`: `preceedingHardFork` gets `st`, the state before the tx, not the accumulating proposals (`C/Rules/Gov.hs:500-510`). **Why** `[GV-10a]` ignores membership: GOV interns a committee voter against `authorizedHotCommitteeCredentials committeeState` (`C/Rules/Gov.hs:477, 607`), which `K/State/CertState.hs:334-340` builds from every non-resigned entry. **Why** `[GV-7a]`: `C/PParams.hs:934-957`; the bootstrap exemption of `adaPerUtxoByte` is PV 9 only. **Why** `[GV-11a]`: the `SecurityGroup` fields of `ConwayPParams` (`C/PParams.hs:643-708`). **Why** `[GV-20]`: LEDGER passes `govState ^. committeeGovStateL` and the constitution to `GovEnv`, and the proposals as the GOV state (`C/Rules/Ledger.hs:438-451`); only ENACT changes the committee and the constitution.

*Implementation status (phase 2, design):* `[GV-1]`, `[GV-2a]` GOV is one mutator, `GovMutator` in `R/`, the second element of `DefaultMutators.all` between `CertsMutator` and `PlutusScriptsTransactionMutator` (D30). It sees the cert state after CERTS, which `[GV-2]` needs, and the gov state before the tx, which `[GV-20]` needs: the mutator chain threads the `State`, and no earlier mutator writes `govState`. The validator part of `[GV-1]` is the existing `ProposalDepositValidator`; `GovMutator` does not repeat the deposit check, so a tx with a bad parent and a bad deposit reports the deposit first, which the ledger also reports (it collects all failures). `GovMutator` returns `success(state)` for `isValid = false` (`[GV-16]`). Its env is read from the arguments: `txId = event.id`, `currentEpoch = context.slotConfig.epochOf(context.env.slot)`, pparams and network from `context.env`, committee, guardrails hash and roots from `state.govState`, DReps, pools and accounts from `state.certState`. The hard-fork decoder limit `GovAction.MaxHardForkMajor` (`SC/GovAction.scala`) is a decoding bound, not `[GV-17]`: a PV10 vector proposes major 12 and the ledger rejects it with `ProposalCantFollow`, which only the rule gives. `[GV-19]` the failure is one `TransactionException.GovException(transactionId, failure: GovFailure)`, where `GovFailure` is an enum in `L/GovState.scala` whose cases carry the ledger constructor names and payloads (ids, voters with action ids, accounts, credentials, the two protocol versions, the two script hashes); `ruleName` returns the case name (D31). `ppuWellFormed` and the security-group test of `[GV-11a]` are private functions of `GovMutator`, not methods of `ProtocolParamUpdate` (D32). The committee votes map is keyed by the hot credential built from the `Voter`: `ConstitutionalCommitteeHotKey` to `Credential.KeyHash`, `ConstitutionalCommitteeHotScript` to `Credential.ScriptHash`; a `StakingPoolKey` becomes a `PoolKeyHash`.

*Implementation status (phase 2, code, gov-rule):* `[GV-1]` to `[GV-20]`, `[GV-2a]`, `[GV-3a]`, `[GV-3b]`, `[GV-7a]`, `[GV-10a]`, `[GV-10b]`, `[GV-11a]`, `[GV-17a]` are `R/GovMutator.scala`, the second element of `DefaultMutators.all`. `GovFailure` and `GovActionPurpose` are in `L/GovState.scala`, with the `[GV-6]`, `[GV-14]`, `[GV-15]` operations as `GovState.addProposal` (returns `None` for a bad parent), `withVote` and `withoutDRepVotes`; `GovRoots.root(purpose)` reads one root. `TransactionException.GovException(transactionId, failure)` carries the failure, and `ruleName` returns `failure.productPrefix`, the case name, which is a literal on every platform. The payloads are `List` for the ledger's `NonEmpty`, `Set` for `NonEmptySet` and `Map` for `NonEmptyMap`. The PV 9 exemptions of the ledger are kept: at PV 9 the return-account checks, `ZeroTreasuryWithdrawals` and the `adaPerUtxoByte` condition of `[GV-7a]` are skipped (D40); the bootstrap failures themselves stay N/A. `[GV-9]`, the same-tx parent of `[GV-6]` and `[GV-17a]` cannot be reached by a real tx: a proposal id holds the tx id, which is the hash of the body that names it. `GovMutatorTest` drives them through the `private[rules]` overload `GovMutator.transit(context, state, event, txId)`, which `transit` calls with `event.id`. Tests: `GovMutatorTest` (every failure, the order of `[GV-3a]` and `[GV-10b]`, the permission table of section 15, the examples above, `[GV-12]` at PV 10 and 11), `EmulatorGovTest` (phase 2 acceptance 4), `WithdrawalsTest` (the mutator order). A scratch run of the GOV, RATIFY, ENACT, EPOCH and LEDGER dirs at the fixed epoch 899 (not committed; running them is `[CV-7]`, `[CV-8]`) gave 1772 cases with 3 verdict mismatches: `GOV.Voting.expired gov-actions` case 5, an epoch case that `[CV-8]` fixes, and two `RATIFY.Voting.Active voting stake.*.Rewards contribute …` cases that fail a POOL retirement-epoch check at 899, not GOV. The two vectors with Word64 values above `Long.MaxValue` still throw in the decoder (gov-state-model report). Every negative case in those dirs fails with the failure its test name gives, for example `ProposalReturnAccountDoesNotExist` for "Fails with invalid network ID in proposal return address", which is the first of the two failures the ledger collects there (`GovSpec.hs:1044-1050`).

### 11.2 GOVCERT committee checks

- GOVCERT MUST reject `AuthCommitteeHotCert` and `ResignCommitteeColdCert` for a cold credential whose state is `Resigned`. `[GC-1]`
- GOVCERT MUST reject them for a cold credential that is not in `committee.members` and not added by a pending `UpdateCommittee`. `[GC-2]`
- GOVCERT MUST overwrite the cold credential's state with the new authorization. `[GC-3]`
- A pending `UpdateCommittee` for `[GC-2]` is one in the proposals before the tx; an `UpdateCommittee` proposed in the same tx does not count. `[GC-2a]`
- `[GC-1]` MUST be checked before `[GC-2]`, and `ruleName` MUST return `ConwayCommitteeHasPreviouslyResigned` and `ConwayCommitteeIsUnknown` for them. `[GC-4]`

**Why** `[GC-2a]`: LEDGER computes `committeeProposals` from the gov state before GOV runs (`C/Rules/Ledger.hs:398-400`) and passes it down CERTS and CERT to GOVCERT (`C/Rules/Certs.hs:222, 251`, `C/Rules/Cert.hs:232`). **Why** `[GC-4]`: `C/Rules/GovCert.hs:191-210`.

*Implementation status (phase 2, design):* the checks are in `CertsMutator.govCert`, which is `private[rules]`: it takes the whole `VotingState` instead of the `(dreps, accounts)` tuple, plus the pre-tx `GovState` for the committee and the committee-purpose proposals, and its error type widens to `TransactionException`. The failures are two new exceptions, `CommitteeHasPreviouslyResignedException(transactionId, coldCredential)` and `CommitteeIsUnknownException(transactionId, coldCredential)`, not fields of `DRepException` (D19, D31). `[CV-1]`: the four GOVCERT expected failures of phase 0 come off the list.

*Implementation status (phase 2, code, govcert-committee):* the checks are a separate step, `CertsMutator.committeeCert(txId, govState)(committeeState, cert)`, `private[rules]`, run in the CERT step after `govCert`, not a widened `govCert` (D41). `govState` is `State.govState` as CERTS sees it, which is the pre-tx gov state because `CertsMutator` runs before `GovMutator` (D30), so `[GC-2a]` needs no extra code; the pending additions are the `addedMembers` of every `UpdateCommittee` proposal in `govState.proposals`, as `proposalsWithPurpose grCommitteeL` with the `UpdateCommittee` match of `C/Rules/GovCert.hs:201-206`. The exceptions are `TransactionException.CommitteeHasPreviouslyResignedException` and `CommitteeIsUnknownException`, each with `transactionId` and `coldCredential`. The four GOVCERT expected failures are off `CardanoLedgerConformanceTest`, and the harness now compares `committeeState` (`LedgerStateComparison`), so the GOVCERT and other dirs check `[GC-3]` against the vectors. The deprecated `VotingCertificatesMutator` still ignores committee certificates.

## 12. Epoch boundary (phase 3, completed by phases 4 and 5)

### 12.1 Tick

- The Emulator MUST run one epoch transition for each boundary that a slot change crosses, in order. `[EB-1]`
- The Emulator MUST NOT run an epoch transition for a slot change that crosses no boundary. `[EB-2]`
- The ticked tip MUST stay computed, not stored, as in chain spec `[NL-2]`. `[EB-3]`

*Example, illustrative,* for `[EB-1]`: `setSlot` from epoch 10 to epoch 13 runs the transitions for boundaries 11, 12 and 13. A pool with `retiring = 12` is removed at boundary 12. A proposal reward from boundary 11 is paid at boundary 12.

Rules added in the phase 3 design round (agent, 2026-11-03):

- `[EB-1]` stands where the ledger differs: NEWEPOCH runs the boundary only when the new epoch is `lastEpoch + 1` and otherwise only pulses (`C/Rules/NewEpoch.hs:165-170`), so a node whose ledger jumps two epochs processes no boundary. The Emulator MUST run every boundary of the jump, in order. `[EB-1a]`
- The ticked tip of `[EB-3]` MUST be computed at most once per `EmulatorState` value: `EmulatorState.tipSnapshot` is a `lazy val`, and `forge(entries, slot)` MUST reuse it when `slot` is the current slot. `[EB-3a]`
- A direct edit (chain spec `[DE-1]`) MUST land on the stored tip snapshot, before any boundary the current slot has crossed; the ticked view is derived from the edited snapshot. `[EB-3b]`

**Why** `[EB-1a]`: a node ticks at every block, so on a live chain every epoch has a boundary tick; the skip in NEWEPOCH is the failure mode of an epoch with no block. `setSlot` stands in for the ticks a node makes (D45). **Why** `[EB-3a]`: every reader (`ledger`, `context`, `utxos`, submit, revalidation) goes through `tipSnapshot` (`N/EmulatorState.scala:96`). With the full transition in the tick, a `setSlot` over 1000 epochs would rerun 1000 transitions on every read. A `lazy val` on the immutable state is a memo whose key is the state itself: every transition is a `copy`, which drops it, so no key of (tip block id, slot) is needed, and a direct edit, which replaces the stored tip without changing the tip id, cannot serve a stale tick (D48). The memo bounds the cost per state value, not per jump. In `Instant`, the first submit after a 1000-epoch jump passes through one or two intermediate `copy` calls (`withMempoolOf`, `accepted`), each with its own first read; then it forges, the stored tip is at the current slot and every later tick is a no-op. *Corrected (agent, phase 3 review):* in `Manual`, and after a direct edit (`withTipSnapshot`) in either preset, the stored tip stays before the jump until the next forge, so the first read of every new state value runs the crossed boundaries again from the stored tip (about 3.5 ms per 1000 epochs, section 12.1), and `revalidated` with a non-empty mempool runs them twice. The results stay correct; only the cost repeats until `produceBlock`. A memo outside the state value is not worth its key and invalidation for this cost. **Why** `[EB-3b]`: this is what `withTipSnapshot` does today (`N/EmulatorState.scala:529-534`). Its comment says an edit and the tick commute because the tick changes only the treasury and the donations; after phase 3 that is false (POOLREAP reads the accounts that `addRewards` edits; phase 4 SNAP reads the UTxOs that `addUtxo` edits), and `[EB-3b]` is the statement that stays true.

*Implementation status (phase 3, code, epoch-state-and-tick):* `[EB-1]`, `[EB-1a]`, `[EB-2]` are the loop of `LedgerSnapshot.atSlot`, for `n` from `epochOf(env.slot) + 1` to `epochOf(slot)` (*superseded by D66 (agent, phase 3 review):* the loop runs from `epoch.lastEpoch + 1`, and the genesis takes `lastEpoch = epochOf(context.env.slot)`); a move backwards runs no transition and keeps the account state, as the donation move did (`EmulatorParityTest` "a move to an earlier epoch ..."). `[EB-3a]`: `EmulatorState.tipSnapshot` is a `private[node] lazy val`, and `forge` takes it when the forge slot is the current slot. `[EB-3b]`: `withTipSnapshot` edits the stored tip; its comment states `[EB-3b]` and no longer claims that edit and tick commute. Measured (2026-11-03, JVM, `EmulatorEpochTest` "a jump over 1000 epochs, then reads and a submit"): `setSlot` over 1000 epochs plus the first read takes 194 µs with `[EB-17]` the only step; the items that add steps re-measure it. Tests: `EpochTransitionTest` (the `[EB-17]` step, `[EB-24]` identity, the `[ES-7]` constants) and `EmulatorEpochTest` (`[DM-13]` at genesis and after each of three boundaries, a jump over three boundaries moves the donations once, the memo, the 1000-epoch jump, phase 3 acceptance 5 for n = 1 and n = 1000; added (agent, phase 3 review): `[EB-1]` and `[EB-1a]` "a jump over five boundaries runs each of them", `[EB-3b]` "a direct edit after a boundary stays when the slot moves back before it", D66 "a block made after a move back to an earlier epoch does not run its boundary again", D67 "a retirement for a boundary the tip ran, accepted after a move back, is never reaped").

### 12.2 Order of one epoch transition

For boundary n, the epoch transition MUST run these steps in this order. Each step has its own ID. `[EB-4]`

| ID | Step | Ledger | Phase |
|---|---|---|---|
| `[EB-5]` | Apply `pendingRewards` (`[RW-10]`). | `C/Rules/NewEpoch.hs:172-178` | 4 |
| `[EB-6]` | SNAP: `go := set`, `set := mark`, `mark := snapshot of now`, `fee := State.fees`. | `S/Rules/Snap.hs:95-103` | 4 |
| `[EB-7]` | POOLREAP part 1: replace the params of each pool in `futureStakePoolParams`; empty that map. | `S/Rules/PoolReap.hs:151-168` | 3 |
| `[EB-8]` | POOLREAP part 2: for each pool with `retiring = n`, refund its deposit to its reward account if registered, else to the treasury. | `S/Rules/PoolReap.hs:172-222` | 3 |
| `[EB-9]` | POOLREAP part 3: clear the pool delegation of its delegators; remove the pool from `stakePools`, `retiring`, `deposits` and `vrfKeyHashes`. | `S/Rules/PoolReap.hs:221-228` | 3 |
| `[EB-10]` | Take `pendingRatify` (`[RT-1]`). | `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:301-304` | 5 |
| `[EB-11]` | Pay enacted treasury withdrawals to registered accounts; subtract only the paid sum from the treasury. | `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:216-249` | 5 |
| `[EB-12]` | Remove expired proposals and their descendants; for each enacted proposal, make it the root and remove its siblings and their descendants. | `C/Governance/Proposals.hs:492-556` | 3 (expiry), 5 (enactment) |
| `[EB-13]` | Set committee and constitution from the enact state; `prevParams := params`; `params :=` the enacted pparams. | `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:325-332` | 5 |
| `[EB-14]` | Refund the deposit of each removed proposal to its return account if registered, else to the treasury. | `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:186-200, 334-336` | 3 |
| `[EB-15]` | `numDormantEpochs += 1` when no remaining proposal has `expiresAfter ≥ n`. | `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:204-208` | 3 |
| `[EB-16]` | Keep in `committeeState` only the cold credentials of current members. | `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:426-430` | 3 |
| `[EB-17]` | Treasury += donation + unclaimed deposits of `[EB-8]` and `[EB-14]`; donation := 0. | `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:354-357` | 3 |
| `[EB-18]` | `deposited :=` the sum of all stake, pool, DRep and proposal deposits. | `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:358-363` | 3 |
| `[EB-19]` | Run HARDFORK when the major PV changed. | `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:374-378` | 5 |
| `[EB-20]` | Compute `pendingRatify` from the state after `[EB-19]` (`[RT-2]`). | `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:379` | 5 |
| `[EB-21]` | `blocksMadePrev := blocksMadeCur`; `blocksMadeCur := ∅`; `lastEpoch := n`. | `C/Rules/NewEpoch.hs:182-192` | 4 |
| `[EB-22]` | Compute `pendingRewards` from the state after `[EB-21]` (`[RW-1]`). | `S/Rules/Rupd.hs`, `S/LedgerState/PulsingReward.hs:284-299` | 4 |

- `[EB-8]` and `[EB-14]` MUST check registration against the accounts at that step. `[EB-23]`
- A phase that has not landed MUST leave its steps as identity. `[EB-24]`

Rules added in the phase 3 design round (agent, 2026-11-03), each read from `S/Rules/PoolReap.hs:132-240`, `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:186-208, 276-379, 426-430`, `C/Rules/Ratify.hs:353-359` and `C/Governance/Proposals.hs:470-556` at the pin:

- `[EB-7]` MUST also remove from `vrfKeyHashes` the whole entry of the VRF key of the current params of every pool whose future params carry a different VRF key, whatever the count (`danglingVRFKeyHashes`, `Map.withoutKeys`, `S/Rules/PoolReap.hs:139-149, 227`). `[EB-7a]`
- `[EB-8]` and `[EB-9]` MUST read the retiring pools from `stakePools` after `[EB-7]`: the deposit goes to the reward account of the new params, and the VRF key removed is that of the new params (`retiringPools`, `S/Rules/PoolReap.hs:175-176`). `[EB-8a]`
- `[EB-9]` MUST subtract one from the count of each retired pool's VRF key, and delete the entry at zero (`removeVRFKeyHashOccurrence`, `S/Rules/PoolReap.hs:226, 235-238`); it runs after `[EB-7a]`. `[EB-9a]`
- `[EB-9]` MUST clear `stakePoolDelegation` of every account whose pool is retired, found by a scan of the accounts, since Scalus has no delegator set (D22, D51). `[EB-9b]`
- Until phase 5 lands, `[EB-12]` MUST remove at boundary n every proposal with `expiresAfter < n − 1` (D50). `[EB-12a]`
- `[EB-12]` MUST remove with each removed proposal its descendants: every proposal whose `prevActionId` names a removed proposal, transitively. One forward pass over the insertion-ordered proposals finds them, since a child always follows its parent. `[EB-12b]`
- `[EB-15]` MUST read the proposals after `[EB-12]`. `[EB-15a]`

**Why** `[EB-12a]`: the ledger ratifies a snapshot of the proposals. The pulser made at boundary n − 1 holds the proposals after the removals of n − 1 with `reCurrentEpoch = n − 1` (`setFreshDRepPulsingState`, `C/Governance.hs:460-515`, `dpCurrentEpoch = epochNo` at `:507`); RATIFY marks an action expired when `expiresAfter < reCurrentEpoch` and it is not accepted (`C/Rules/Ratify.hs:353-359`); EPOCH at boundary n removes `rsExpired` with `proposalsApplyEnactment` (`C/Rules/Epoch.hs:321-336`, `C/Governance/Proposals.hs:465-507`). A proposal made in epoch n − 1 has `expiresAfter ≥ n − 1`, so it is never in that set, and nothing but enactment removes a proposal between boundaries. So for every proposal that is not enacted, the set is `{p : p.expiresAfter < n − 1}` over the proposals at boundary n. Checked on the 533 Dugite PV11 NEWEPOCH dumps (2026-11-03): the formula equals `rsExpired` of the dump's own pulser in 531 cases; the 2 others (`test_5591`, `test_6836`) differ by one action each, and that action is in `rsEnacted`: it was accepted at its last chance. Residual gap until phase 5: an action nobody votes on that the ledger would still enact, for example under a threshold of 0 (`[RT-11]`), stays until its expiry. Phase 5 replaces the formula by `pendingRatify.expired`. **Why** `[EB-15a]`: `updateNumDormantEpochs eNo newProposals` (`C/Rules/Epoch.hs:348`). The Imp timeline agrees: lifetime 2, a proposal in epoch 0 (`expiresAfter = 2`) is still stored when boundary 3 sets the counter to 1, and is removed at boundary 4 (`EpochSpec.hs:160-170`).

*Example, illustrative,* for `[EB-16]` under `[ES-13]`: with no committee, `committeeMembers` of `SNothing` is empty, so the first boundary removes every `committeeState` entry, including a hot key GOVCERT accepted for a pending `UpdateCommittee` (`[GC-2a]`). The entry comes back when a tx authorizes it again after the committee is enacted (phase 5). In 6 of the 533 NEWEPOCH dumps `committeeState` changes at the boundary.

*Implementation status (phase 3, design):* the steps are functions of `EpochTransition` in `scalus.cardano.ledger.epoch.internal` (D47), run in the order of the table; the steps of phases 4 and 5 are absent, which is `[EB-24]`. `[EB-7]`, `[EB-7a]`, `[EB-8]`, `[EB-8a]`, `[EB-9]`, `[EB-9a]`, `[EB-9b]` are one `poolReap(ledger, epoch, n)` step. Its refunds are summed per reward-account credential and added to the balance of a registered account, else to `accountState.treasury` (`S/Rules/PoolReap.hs:181-195, 218`); `deposited` is not touched there (`:217`), since `[EB-18]` recomputes it. `[EB-12a]`, `[EB-12b]` are `GovState.removeWithDescendants(ids: Set[GovActionId]): (GovState, Vector[GovActionState])`, the removal operation the phase 2 note promised, which phase 5 reuses for enacted siblings; the expiry step computes the ids. `[EB-14]` adds `procedure.deposit` to the balance of the account of `procedure.rewardAccount` when it is registered after `[EB-8]`, else to the treasury (`returnProposalDeposits`, `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:186-200`). `[EB-17]` adds the donation and the unclaimed proposal deposits (`C/Rules/Epoch.hs:354-357`); the unclaimed pool deposits were added inside `[EB-8]`, where the ledger adds them (`S/Rules/PoolReap.hs:218`, not `C/Rules/Epoch.hs`), so the table's citation for the pool part of `[EB-17]` is that line. `[EB-18]` is `EpochTransition.totalObligation(certState, govState)`: the deposits of the accounts, pools and DReps, as `EmulatorState.depositsOf` sums today (`conwayObligationCertState`, `C/State/CertState.hs:112-117`), plus the deposits of the proposals (`C/Governance.hs:416-422`); `depositsOf` is replaced by it, so the D26 genesis and the boundary use one sum (D52). `[EB-16]` keeps the `committeeState` entries whose cold credential is in `committee.members`, the committee of the gov state after `[EB-13]`, which in phase 3 is the unchanged one. `LedgerSnapshot.atSlot` runs the loop of `[EB-1]` over `EpochTransition.transition` and then sets `env.slot` and `env.treasury`; `EmulatorBase.atSlot` goes, with a MiMa filter if reported (D49).

*Implementation status (phase 3, code, poolreap):* `[EB-7]` to `[EB-9]`, `[EB-7a]`, `[EB-8a]`, `[EB-9a]`, `[EB-9b]` and `[EB-23]` are the private step `EpochTransition.poolReap(ledger, epoch, n)`, run before `[EB-17]`; it is identity when no pool has future params and none retires at `n`. It refines the design note above in one point: `deposited` drops by the refunded and the unclaimed deposits inside the step, as `S/Rules/PoolReap.hs:217` does, not only by `[EB-18]` (D60). *Superseded (agent, phase 3 review):* once `[EB-18]` landed, the decrement was dead code that no test could see, so it is gone and `[EB-18]` is the only writer of `deposited` at the boundary, as the design note said (D52, D62). A pool counts as retired when `retiring = n` exactly (`:173`); a future param of a pool not in `stakePools` is dropped (`Map.dropMissing`, `:156`); the step never adds or removes an account (the postcondition at `:123-130`). The unclaimed deposits go to `accountState.treasury` in the step, so `[EB-17]` adds only the donation until `[EB-14]` lands. Tests: `EpochTransitionTest` (activation, `[EB-7a]` at count 2, the refund to a registered account and to the treasury with `deposited`, `[EB-8a]` with a new reward account and a new VRF key retiring at the same boundary, `[EB-9a]` at count 2, `[EB-9b]`, two pools with one reward account, a later retiring epoch) and `EmulatorEpochTest` (the example of section 12.3 on mainnet epochs, with the tx retirement and the tx deregistration, the treasury delta of the boundary and the `deposited` drop; the refund to a registered reward account; a re-registration by `updatePool` that takes effect at the next boundary; added (agent, phase 3 review): `[EB-1a]` "a jump over several boundaries retires a pool at a boundary inside the jump"; the activation test of `EpochTransitionTest` also covers `[EB-7a]` when the new params keep the VRF key), which is acceptance 6 except `[EB-12b]`, which the proposal-expiry item tests. Measured again (2026-11-03, JVM, same test as in section 12.1): `setSlot` over 1000 epochs plus the first read takes 514 to 822 µs in three runs with `[EB-7]` to `[EB-9]` and `[EB-17]`; that emulator has no pools, so `poolReap` takes its early exit at every boundary, and the spread against the earlier 194 µs is run-to-run noise of a cold JVM, not the cost of the step.

*Implementation status (phase 3, code, gov-boundary):* `[EB-12]` (expiry), `[EB-12a]`, `[EB-12b]`, `[EB-14]`, `[EB-15]`, `[EB-15a]`, `[EB-16]`, `[EB-17]` and `[EB-18]` are in `EpochTransition.transition` after `poolReap`, as the design note above says: the private steps `expireProposals` (`[EB-12a]`, `[EB-12b]`, `[EB-14]`, `[EB-23]`), `updatedVotingState` (`[EB-15]`, `[EB-15a]`, `[EB-16]`) and `movedToTreasury` (`[EB-17]`: donation plus the unclaimed proposal deposits; the unclaimed pool deposits stay in `poolReap`, D60), then `deposited := totalObligation(certState, govState)` (`[EB-18]`, D52). `GovState.removeWithDescendants` is `private[ledger]` (D61) and returns the removed proposals in the order they were made; `EmulatorState.depositsOf` is gone, the two genesis builders call `totalObligation` (it was never released, so MiMa reports nothing). Refinements: `[EB-15]` has no early exit, so every boundary with no active proposal grows the counter, also in an emulator that never had a proposal (`C/Rules/Epoch.hs:204-208`); a DRep registered after a long proposal-free `setSlot` jump gets `epoch + dRepActivity − numDormantEpochs` as on a node (`[DR-3]`). `[EB-15a]` changes the result in phase 3 when `[EB-12b]` removes a descendant that is still active at `n`: a child made after its parent has a later `expiresAfter`, and the counter grows because `[EB-15]` reads the proposals after the removal (`C/Rules/Epoch.hs:348`); test: `EpochTransitionTest` "an expired proposal takes its descendants and refunds each of their deposits" (agent, phase 3 review). `[EB-16]` uses the committee of the ledger state, which phase 5 will set by `[EB-13]` first. `[EB-18]` scans accounts, pools, DReps and proposals at every boundary (D62); POOLREAP's own decrement of `deposited` (D60) was then overwritten by the same value, so it is gone (phase 3 review). Tests: `GovStateTest` (`[EB-12b]` with a three-deep chain, an unrelated sibling and an InfoAction; a removed child keeps its parent), `EpochTransitionTest` (`[EB-12a]` at `expiresAfter = n − 2` and `n − 1`, `[EB-14]` to a registered account and to the treasury with the donation, `[EB-12b]` with the refund of a descendant, `[EB-15]` with no proposal, with a kept but inactive one and with an active one, `[EB-16]` with a committee and with none, `[EB-18]` with stake, pool, DRep and proposal deposits; the two earlier identity tests now expect the counter bump), `EmulatorEpochTest` (phase 3 acceptance 1 to 4 on submitted InfoActions; acceptance 4 also checks the DRep expiry, `startEpoch + dRepActivity`, then `+ 3` after the second proposal). Each Emulator test fails with the gov steps disabled. Measured (2026-11-03, JVM, `EmulatorEpochTest` "a jump over 1000 epochs, then reads and a submit" run alone): `setSlot` over 1000 epochs plus the first read takes 3.5 to 3.8 ms in three runs, against 1.4 to 1.5 ms with the gov steps disabled in the same setup, so about 2 µs per boundary. The Dugite NEWEPOCH corpus check of acceptance 8 (`[CV-9]` to `[CV-12]`) is not part of this item.

**Why** `[EB-20]` and `[EB-22]` run at the boundary, not inside the epoch: both pulsers are pure functions of state that is fixed at the boundary at PV > 6 (`S/LedgerState/PulsingReward.hs:180-181`, `S/Era.hs:154-155`; `C/Governance.hs:471-515`). One computation gives the pulsed result. Decision D3.

**Why** `[EB-13]` takes the pparams from the enact state: the ledger reads them from `cgsFuturePParams`, which TICK makes definite only at the "point of no return" (`S/Rules/Tick.hs:150-157`; `C/Governance.hs:301-325`). A node always ticks there. The Emulator may not, because `setSlot` can jump. Applying the enact state gives what a node that ticked normally gives. Decision D3.

Rules added in the phase 5 design round (agent, 2026-11-03), read from `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:209-249, 276-379`, `C/Governance.hs:301-325, 460-516`, `C/Governance/Proposals.hs:465-579`, `C/Rules/HardFork.hs:68-125` and `C/Rules/NewEpoch.hs:160-192` at the pin:

- `[EB-10]` MUST read `pendingRatify` of the epoch state that the boundary finds: the result that `[EB-20]` computed at boundary n − 1. `[EB-10a]`
- `[EB-11]` MUST pay each entry of `pendingRatify.enactState.withdrawals` to the balance of the account of its credential when that account is registered after `[EB-9]`, MUST subtract only the paid sum from the treasury, and MUST leave the rest in the treasury (`applyEnactedWithdrawals`, `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:216-249`). `[EB-11a]`
- `[EB-12]` MUST first remove the ids of `pendingRatify.expired` with their descendants (`[EB-12b]`), then take each id of `pendingRatify.enacted` in order: for an action with a purpose, remove with their descendants the siblings, which are the proposals of the same purpose whose `prevActionId` equals the current root of that purpose, except the id itself; make the id the root of its purpose; remove the enacted proposal and keep its children. An action without a purpose (TreasuryWithdrawals) is only removed. An id that is not in the proposals MUST be identity (`proposalsApplyEnactment`, `C/Governance/Proposals.hs:492-556`). `[EB-12c]`
- `[EB-12a]` is withdrawn in phase 5: `[EB-12c]` takes the expired ids from `pendingRatify.expired` (`[RT-10]`). `[EB-12d]`
- `[EB-13]` MUST set the committee and the constitution of the gov state from `pendingRatify.enactState` in every case, MUST set `prevParams :=` the current pparams, and MUST set the current pparams `:= pendingRatify.enactState.params` only when `pendingRatify.enacted` holds a ParameterChange or a HardForkInitiation; else the current pparams stay (`C/Rules/Epoch.hs:325-332`; `predictFuturePParams`, `C/Governance.hs:301-325`; `nextEpochPParams`, `cardano-ledger-core State/Governance.hs:121-133`). `[EB-13a]`
- `[EB-14]` MUST refund the deposits of all proposals that `[EB-12c]` removed: expired, removed as siblings or descendants, and enacted. `[EB-14a]`
- Correction to `[EB-19]`: HARDFORK MUST run when the protocol version of the current pparams after `[EB-13]` differs from that of `prevParams`, major or minor (`curPv /= …`, `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:375-378`). For a new major 11 it MUST add one occurrence per VRF key of `stakePools` and `futureStakePoolParams` to the existing counts of `vrfKeyHashes` (`populateVRFKeyHashes`, `%~`, `C/Rules/HardFork.hs:77-78, 107-125`); for every other version it MUST be identity, major 10 included (D93). `[EB-19a]`
- Correction to `[BK-7]`: the genesis MUST fill `vrfKeyHashes` from the seeded pools only when the major protocol version of the genesis pparams is 11 or more; below it the map MUST be empty. `[BK-7a]`
- `[EB-20]` MUST read, after `[EB-19]`: `reCurrentEpoch = n`; the accounts, the UTxO, the DReps, the committee state, the pools and the proposals of the ledger state; `snapshots.mark` of `[EB-6]`; and an enact state with the committee, the constitution and the roots of the gov state, the current pparams, no withdrawals, and the treasury of `accountState` after `[EB-17]` (`setFreshDRepPulsingState`, `C/Governance.hs:460-516`; `mkEnactState`, `:327-337`; `ensTreasuryL .~ epochState ^. treasuryL`, `:511`). `[EB-20a]`
- `transition` MUST take the current pparams as `env.params` and MUST return the current pparams after the boundary with the ledger state and the epoch state. `LedgerSnapshot.atSlot` MUST pass the result of boundary n to boundary n + 1 and MUST write the last one to `context.env.params` (D88). `[EB-25]`
- `[EB-22]` MUST read `epoch.prevParams` after `[EB-13]`, not `env.params` (D68). `[EB-22a]`

**Why** `[EB-13a]`: the ledger takes the new pparams from `cgsFuturePParams`, which `predictFuturePParams` fills only when an enacted action is a ParameterChange or a HardForkInitiation (`guard (any hasChangesToPParams …)`, `C/Governance.hs:318`). Without one, the current pparams stay, also when they changed after boundary n − 1, as an Imp `modifyPParams` does: 511 of the 533 NEWEPOCH inputs carry `NoPParamsUpdate`. The committee and the constitution are set with no condition (`C/Rules/Epoch.hs:328-329`). **Why** `[EB-19a]`: a minor bump (11.0 to 11.1) is a valid HardForkInitiation (`pvCanFollow`), and the ledger then adds the counts a second time; the Emulator keeps that. `[BK-7a]` keeps the map at PV 10 as the ledger has it, empty: no PV 10 rule writes it, and the removal of POOLREAP is a no-op on an empty map (`S/Rules/PoolReap.hs:226-238`). So a hard fork from 10 to 11 gives the ledger's counts, which the `[BK-7]` fill at every version would double. POOLREAP empties `futureStakePoolParams` before HARDFORK runs, so only `stakePools` adds counts. **Why** `[EB-25]`: the pparams stay in `Context.env.params` (`[DM-7]`), and a jump over several boundaries must ratify and reward each of them with the pparams the boundary before it set.

*Implementation status (phase 5, design):* D50 and `[EB-12a]` are replaced by `[EB-12c]` with `pendingRatify.expired`. For every proposal that no RATIFY run enacts, both give the same set (**Why** `[EB-12a]`). They differ in three cases: an action that RATIFY enacts with no vote, for example under a threshold of 0 (`[RT-11]`), which `[EB-12a]` kept until its expiry; the first boundary after a `withState` genesis with proposals, where `[DM-9c]` expires none (`[EB-12a]` expired those with `expiresAfter < n − 1`); and a HardForkInitiation to major 12 or more, which `[EN-4]` never enacts, so it expires as an unvoted action does. The steps are one private `enact(ledger, epoch)` step for `[EB-10]` to `[EB-14]`, which replaces `expireProposals`; `GovState` gains a `private[ledger]` `enact(id): (GovState, Vector[GovActionState])` beside `removeWithDescendants` (D61). `[EB-16]` already reads the committee after `[EB-13]`. `[EB-20]` reuses the instant stake of `[EB-6]`, since no step between them changes the UTxO. Tests for `[EB-19a]` and `[BK-7a]`: a fork from 10 to 11 fills the map from empty; a fork from 11.0 to 11.1 doubles each count; a PV 10 genesis has an empty map. Before the `[BK-7a]` change the implementer greps `vrfKeyHashes` in `EmulatorGenesisVrfKeyHashesTest`, `EmulatorEpochTest`, `EpochTransitionTest` and `CertsMutatorVrfKeyHashesTest` for a genesis at PV 10.

*Implementation status (phase 5, code, enact-and-pparams):* the pparams side of `[EB-13a]`, `[EB-19a]`, `[EB-22a]`, `[EB-25]` and `[BK-7a]` are in the code; the gov side of `[EB-10]` to `[EB-14]` (committee, constitution, withdrawals, `[EB-12c]`) and `[EB-20]` are not, so `expireProposals` of `[EB-12a]` still runs. `transition` returns `(State, EpochState, ProtocolParams)` (D88). The private step `enactedParams` reads the action of each id of `pendingRatify.enacted` from `govState.proposals` after POOLREAP and before `[EB-12]`, since `RatifyState` keeps ids, not actions (D86): the step that implements `[EB-12c]` MUST keep this read before it removes the enacted proposals. An id that is not a proposal is no pparam action (D98). `prevParams := env.params` is set with `[EB-21]`, and `[EB-22]` reads `env.params`, the same pparams. The private step `hardFork` runs after `[EB-18]` and is identity unless the version changed and the new major is 11; the VRF counts it adds are `Long` and do not saturate, since no test reaches `Long.MaxValue` pools. `LedgerSnapshot.atSlot` builds one `EpochEnv` per boundary with the pparams the boundary before it returned, and writes the last into `context.env.params`. `[BK-7a]` is a gate on `protocolVersion.major` in `EmulatorBase.buildInitialState`.
