Tests: `EpochTransitionTest` (an enacted ParameterChange gives the enact-state pparams and keeps the old ones as `prevParams`; the reward update reads the old `rho`; a NewConstitution keeps the current pparams; an unknown id; 10 to 11 fills the map, 11.0 to 11.1 doubles it, 9 to 10 changes nothing, a ParameterChange runs no fork), `EmulatorEpochParamsTest` (`[EB-25]`: the context takes the new pparams; a move inside an epoch keeps them; a jump over two boundaries equals two moves, with `prevParams` the new pparams after the second), `EmulatorGenesisVrfKeyHashesTest` (a PV 10 genesis has an empty map). The grep that the design note asks for found no PV 10 genesis that asserts a filled map: `Context.testMainnet()` is at PV 11.

*Implementation status (phase 5, code, ratify):* `[EB-20]` and `[EB-20a]` are in the code: the private step `ratify` of `EpochTransition` runs after `hardFork` on the ledger state of `[EB-19]`, with the enact state of its gov state and the pparams after `[EB-13]` (`Ratify.enactStateOf`), the treasury of the account state after `[EB-17]`, `snapshots.mark` of `[EB-6]` and `reCurrentEpoch = n`; its result becomes `pendingRatify` with `[EB-21]`. With no proposal it computes no distribution and returns the enact state with nothing enacted or expired (D95). Refinement: the DRep distribution sums the UTxO stake again (`instantStake`) instead of reusing the one of `[EB-6]`: the reuse needs a new parameter through `snap`, for a cost paid only at a boundary with a proposal. (Superseded by the epoch-enactment note, which applies the whole enact state; historical:) Until the gov-side item lands (`[EB-10]` to `[EB-14]`, `[EB-12c]`), only the pparams effect of an enactment applies (`enactedParams`, `[EB-13a]`): an enacted proposal stays in the proposals and the roots do not move, so each later boundary ratifies it again until `[EB-12a]` expires it. For a ParameterChange or a HardForkInitiation this changes nothing, since the update gives the same pparams again and `hardFork` runs only on a version change. The committee, the constitution and the withdrawals of the enact state are not applied. Tests: `EpochTransitionTest` ("a boundary ratifies the proposals and keeps the result for the next boundary", with `reCurrentEpoch = n` expiring an InfoAction at `n − 1`; "a boundary ratifies with the treasury after the donations moved in", `[EB-20a]` with `[RT-5d]`; "a delegator of a pool that retires at the boundary adds no deposit to its stake", `[RT-4a]` through `transition`; "with no proposal the ratify result enacts nothing"). Each failed before the step existed.

*Implementation status (phase 5, code, epoch-enactment):* `[EB-10]` to `[EB-14]`, `[EB-10a]`, `[EB-11a]`, `[EB-12c]`, `[EB-12d]`, the gov side of `[EB-13a]` and `[EB-14a]` are in the code. The private step `enact(ledger, epoch)` of `EpochTransition` replaces `expireProposals` and runs after `enactedParams`, which still reads the enacted actions before they are removed. It runs in the order of `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:301-336`: the withdrawals on the accounts after POOLREAP, then `GovState.removeWithDescendants(pendingRatify.expired)`, then `GovState.enact(id)` for each enacted id in order, then the committee and the constitution of the enact state, then the refunds of all removed proposals onto the accounts after the withdrawals. `GovState.enact` is `private[ledger]` (D61). It computes the siblings as the proposals of the same purpose whose parent is the current root, since Scalus keeps no graph (D28). It returns the removed proposals with the enacted one. `[EB-13a]` sets the committee and the constitution at every boundary, so a mid-epoch direct edit of either is replaced at the next boundary by the value of the enact state, as on a node. The roots move only by `GovState.enact`; the step does not copy `enactState.roots`, as the ledger does not (`cgsProposalsL .~ newProposals`, `:327`). `[EB-12a]` (D50) is gone from the code. The NEWEPOCH runner passes the decoded `rsExpired` of each case into the ratify result it builds, so the cases that enact nothing still compare `govState.proposals`; the full decode of D97 stays with the runner item. Tests: `GovStateTest` (`enact` with a sibling, its child and the enacted child; a parent and a child enacted in order; NoConfidence against an UpdateCommittee; a TreasuryWithdrawals; an unknown id); `EpochTransitionTest` (the expired set decides, not `expiresAfter`; two boundaries remove an unvoted proposal as `[EB-12a]` did; `[EB-11a]` with an unregistered account; an enacted TreasuryWithdrawals with the payment and the refund on one balance; `[EB-12c]` with a sibling, its child and the enacted child, and an unclaimed refund; `[EB-13a]` committee and constitution). The four phase 3 expiry tests now set `pendingRatify.expired`; the tests that seed a committee start from the genesis ratify result of their gov state (`[DM-9c]`). Measured (2026-11-03, JVM, `EmulatorEpochTest` "a jump over 1000 epochs, then reads and a submit" run alone, three runs each): 17.7 to 18.6 ms with the step, 14.4 to 17.1 ms on the parent commit `9c8c00a9f`. That emulator has no proposal, so `ratify` takes its early exit; `enact` has none and copies the ledger state at every boundary.

### 12.3 Worked example, illustrative

Epoch length 100 slots. Pool P registered at genesis epoch 0. A tx in epoch 2 retires P at epoch 4. Its reward account is unregistered in epoch 3.

| Boundary | Effect |
|---|---|
| 3 | nothing for P |
| 4 | `[EB-8]`: P's account is unregistered, so the 500 ADA deposit goes to the treasury by `[EB-17]`. `[EB-9]`: P is removed; its delegators have no pool. |

## 13. Rewards (phase 4)

### 13.1 Snapshot

- SNAP MUST compute the mark snapshot from the UTxO, the accounts and the pools at the boundary. `[SN-1]`
- The stake of a credential MUST be the coin of base-address outputs with that stake credential plus its reward balance. `[SN-2]`
- SNAP MUST include only registered credentials that delegate to a pool; it MUST NOT check that the pool is registered (D118). `[SN-3]`
- SNAP MUST drop credentials with zero stake. `[SN-4]`
- Pointer and enterprise addresses MUST add no stake. `[SN-5]`
- The total active stake MUST be 1 when the snapshot is empty. `[SN-6]`

**Why** `[SN-2]` to `[SN-6]`: `K/State/Stake.hs:220-246`, `C/State/Stake.hs:101-121`, `K/State/SnapShots.hs:276-278, 418-430`.

Rules added in the phase 4 design round (agent, 2026-11-03), read from `S/Rules/Snap.hs:76-103`, `K/State/SnapShots.hs:187-216, 398-430` and `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:291-297` at the pin:

- `pools` of the mark snapshot MUST hold every pool in `stakePools` at SNAP, also a pool with no stake and no delegator, with its current params, not its future params (`resetStakePoolsSnapShot` over all of `psStakePools`). `[SN-7]`
- For each pool, `PoolSnapshot` MUST take: `stake` = the sum of `activeStake` over the credentials delegated to it; `numDelegators` = the number of registered accounts delegated to it, whatever their stake; `selfDelegatedOwners` = its owners whose key-hash credential is such an account; `selfDelegatedOwnersStake` = the sum of `activeStake` of those owners; `vrfKeyHash`, `pledge`, `cost`, `margin` from its params; `rewardAccount` = the credential of its reward account (`mkStakePoolSnapShot`, `K/State/SnapShots.hs:187-216`). `[SN-8]`
- SNAP MUST read the ledger after `[EB-5]` and before `[EB-7]`: a reward paid at the boundary is in the new mark, and a pool that retires at the boundary is in it too. `fee` MUST be `State.fees` after `[EB-5]`. `[SN-9]`
- `[SN-8]` MUST find the delegators by a scan of the accounts, as `[EB-9b]` does (D22, D51). `[SN-10]`

**Why** `[SN-9]`: NEWEPOCH applies the reward update, then EPOCH runs SNAP on that state and POOLREAP after it (`C/Rules/NewEpoch.hs:172-179`, `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:293-296`). Checked on the 4 dumps that retire a pool at their boundary (`test_5057`, `test_5072`, `test_6834`, `test_7749`): `st_out.mark` holds as many pools as `stakePools` of `st`, one more than `st_out`.

*Implementation status (phase 4, snapshots-and-blocks):* `[EB-6]`, `[SN-1]` to `[SN-10]` are the private step `EpochTransition.snap` and the function `EpochTransition.stakeSnapshot(ledger: State): StakeSnapshot` of `epoch.internal`, which the genesis calls too (`[ES-8a]`). SNAP runs first in `transition`, on the ledger before POOLREAP (`[SN-9]`); until `[EB-5]` lands it reads the ledger as the boundary finds it. The base-address stake is a private `instantStake(utxos)` in `EpochTransition`, not `StakeDistribution.aggregateUtxoStake`, which `[DM-15]` deprecates; `EmulatorBase.stakeDistribution` still reads the old object until the `[DM-15]` item. Refinements: `[SN-3]` "delegated to a registered pool" is not checked, as `resolveActiveInstantStakeCredentials` does not check it (`K/State/Stake.hs:220-246`): DELEG accepts a delegation only to a registered pool and `[EB-9b]` clears the delegations to a retired one, so a credential delegated to an unregistered pool exists only after a direct edit or a seed that names such a pool, and its stake then counts in `totalActiveStake` as on a node with the same state. `[SN-4]` drops a credential whose UTxO coin plus balance is 0, which also covers zero-coin outputs that a direct edit can make (the ledger's instant stake never holds a 0, `C/State/Stake.hs:104-112`). `selfDelegatedOwners` and `numDelegators` come from one scan of the accounts (`[SN-10]`). A direct edit after genesis (`addUtxo`, `[EB-3b]`) does not change the seeded `mark`, `set` and `go`; the next boundary's `mark` holds it, as on a node. Tests: `EpochTransitionTest` (`[SN-2]` with two base outputs, an enterprise and a pointer output; `[SN-3]`; `[SN-4]` with an owner of zero stake, `[SN-8]`; `[SN-6]`; `[SN-7]` with a pool with no delegator and future params; `[EB-6]` with three different snapshots; `[SN-9]`; `[EB-21]`), `EmulatorEpochTest` (`[ES-8a]` with a seeded delegated credential and pool; `[EB-6]` across one Emulator boundary). Measured (2026-11-03, JVM, `EmulatorEpochTest` "a jump over 1000 epochs, then reads and a submit" run alone): 5.8 to 16.4 ms in three runs, against 3.5 to 3.8 ms before SNAP; that emulator has 3 UTxOs and no accounts, so the cost is the step itself, not the scan.

### 13.2 Calculation

- The reward calculation MUST compute `pendingRewards` from: `go`, `fee`, `blocksMadePrev`, `reserves`, `prevParams`. `[RW-1]`
- The reward calculation MUST use the formulas of the table below, with `floor` where shown. `[RW-2]`

| Quantity | Formula | Ledger |
|---|---|---|
| `expectedBlocks` | `floor(f · slotsPerEpoch)` | `S/LedgerState/PulsingReward.hs:127-129` |
| `eta` | `min(1, blocksMade / expectedBlocks)` (`Measured`); `1` (`Ideal`) | `:133-138` |
| `deltaR1` | `floor(eta · rho · reserves)` | `:121-125` |
| `rPot` | `fee + deltaR1` | `:139` |
| `deltaT1` | `floor(tau · rPot)` | `:140` |
| `R` | `rPot − deltaT1` | `:141` |
| `totalStake` | `maxLovelaceSupply − reserves` | `:144, 304-306` |
| `sigma`, `sigmaA` | `poolStake / totalStake`, `poolStake / totalActiveStake` | `S/Rewards.hs:392-393` |
| `p`, `s` | `pledge / totalStake`, `ownerStake / totalStake` | `S/Rewards.hs:390-391` |
| `maxP` | `floor(R/(1+a0) · (σ' + p'·a0·(σ' − p'·(z0−σ')/z0)/z0))`, `z0 = 1/nOpt`, `σ' = min(σ, z0)`, `p' = min(p, z0)`; 0 if `pledge > ownerStake` | `K/State/SnapShots.hs:119-138`; `S/Rewards.hs:359-362` |
| `appPerf` | `(blocksN / max(1, blocksTotal)) / sigmaA` (`Measured`); `1` (`Ideal`) | `S/Rewards.hs:86-97` |
| `poolR` | `floor(appPerf · maxP)` | `S/Rewards.hs:364` |
| leader | `poolR` if `poolR ≤ cost`, else `cost + floor((poolR − cost)·(m + (1−m)·s/σ))` | `S/Rewards.hs:110-127` |
| member | 0 if `poolR ≤ cost`, else `floor((poolR − cost)·(1−m)·t/σ)`, `t = memberStake / totalStake` | `S/Rewards.hs:140-157` |
| `deltaR2` | `R − Σ rewards` | `S/LedgerState/PulsingReward.hs:242-280` |
| `deltaT`, `deltaR`, `deltaF` | `deltaT1`, `deltaR2 − deltaR1`, `−fee` | same |

- Under `Measured`, a pool with no block in `blocksMadePrev` MUST get no reward. `[RW-3]`
- Under `Ideal`, every pool in `go` with stake MUST be treated as a pool with `appPerf = 1`. `[RW-4]`
- A self-delegated owner MUST get no member reward. `[RW-5]`
- The leader reward MUST go to the pool's reward account. `[RW-6]`
- The calculation MUST sum the rewards of one credential. `[RW-7]`
- The calculation MUST omit a zero reward. `[RW-8]`
- The calculation MUST hold `deltaT + deltaR + Σ rewards + deltaF = 0`. `[RW-9]`

Rules added in the phase 4 design round (agent, 2026-11-03), read from `S/LedgerState/PulsingReward.hs:99-212, 242-280`, `S/RewardUpdate.hs:250-273`, `S/Rewards.hs:86-157, 291-393` and `K/State/SnapShots.hs:119-151` at the pin:

- `[EB-22]` MUST read `snapshots.go`, `snapshots.fee`, `blocksMadePrev` and `accountState.reserves` after `[EB-21]` of the same boundary; the pparams of D68; `slotsPerEpoch = slotConfig.epochLength`; `poolPerformance` and `activeSlotsCoeff` of the `ChainConfig`; and `maxLovelaceSupply = 45 × 10^15`, a constant, the value of the mainnet, preprod and preview Shelley genesis. `[RW-13]`
- `expectedBlocks` MUST be `floor(f · slotsPerEpoch)`: the ledger's factor `1 − d` is 1, since Conway fixes `d = 0` (`C/PParams.hs:855`). `blocksTotal` MUST be the sum of all counts in `blocksMadePrev`. Under `Measured`, `eta` MUST be `min(1, blocksTotal / max(1, expectedBlocks))`. `[RW-14]`
- Under `Ideal`, `eta` MUST be 1, the pools that earn MUST be the pools of `go.pools` whose `stake` is above 0, their `appPerf` MUST be 1, and `blocksMadePrev` MUST NOT be read. Under `Measured`, the pools that earn MUST be the pools of `go.pools` with an entry in `blocksMadePrev`, and `appPerf` MUST be 0 when `sigmaA = 0`. `[RW-15]`
- `sigma`, `p`, `s` and `t` MUST be 0 when `totalStake ≤ 0`. `[RW-16]`
- An `nOpt` of 0 MUST count as 1. `[RW-17]`
- A member reward MUST be computed for each credential of `go.activeStake` whose pool in `go.delegations` earns, unless the credential is the key hash of one of that pool's `selfDelegatedOwners`, with `t = activeStake(cred) / totalStake`; no registration check applies (`rewardStakePoolMember`, `S/RewardUpdate.hs:250-273`; `rewardOnePoolMember`, `S/Rewards.hs:291-321`). `[RW-18]`
- `[RW-8]` MUST apply to member rewards only. The leader reward of every pool that earns MUST be an entry of `rewards`, also when it is 0, at `PoolSnapshot.rewardAccount` of `go`, not at the reward account of the current pool params (`collectLRs`, `S/LedgerState/PulsingReward.hs:177-182`). `[RW-8a]`
- The calculation MUST use exact rationals over `BigInt`, and MUST round only at the `floor`s of the table of `[RW-2]`, toward −∞; every other quantity, `eta`, `σ'` and `p'` included, MUST stay exact. `[RW-19]`
- The calculation and `[EB-5]` MUST use the rules of PV 7 and later whatever the major version of the pparams: no registration prefilter of rewards (`hardforkBabbageForgoRewardPrefilter`, `S/Era.hs:155`), and the rewards of one credential summed (`hardforkAllegraAggregatedRewards`, `:130`). `[RW-20]`

**Why** `[RW-14]`, `[RW-16]`, `[RW-17]`: each replaces a ledger crash or default that no live chain reaches. `blocksMade % expectedBlocks` divides by zero when `f · slotsPerEpoch < 1` (`S/LedgerState/PulsingReward.hs:133-138`), which an Emulator test with short epochs reaches; `max(1, …)` keeps `eta = 0` with no block and gives `eta = 1` with any block. `pledge % totalStake` fails at `totalStake = 0` (`S/Rewards.hs:390`) and so does the member share `c % totalStake` (`:320`), while `%?` gives 0 for the other ratios (`:391-392`); a caller can set `reserves` to the whole supply, and then no pool earns. `mkPoolRewardInfo` fails on `nOpt = 0` (`:384`); `maxPool` takes 1 (`K/State/SnapShots.hs:148-151`), and PV11 GOV rejects the value (`[GV-7a]`), so only a genesis can carry it. **Why** `[RW-15]`: `mkApparentPerformance` returns 0 for `sigmaA = 0` (`S/Rewards.hs:92-93`), so a pool with no stake earns nothing under either model, and `Ideal` needs no block count. **Why** `[RW-20]`: the Emulator runs PV11; 105 NEWEPOCH inputs carry previous pparams at PV 2.0, but none has a reward (`[CV-16]`), so no case can show the gates.

*Implementation status (phase 4, design):* corrections to the table of `[RW-2]`: the row `expectedBlocks` is `[RW-14]`; `eta` uses `blocksTotal`, the sum over all pools; `appPerf` is 0 when `sigmaA = 0` (`[RW-15]`); `p` and `s` are `[RW-16]`. `[RW-6]` is `[RW-8a]`. The example of section 13.4 was checked by hand against the formulas (2026-11-03): every value of its table follows. The calculation is `EpochTransition.rewardUpdate` in `epoch.internal`, a pure function of the inputs of `[RW-13]`, so the runner (`[CV-16]`) and the unit tests call it without an Emulator; the rational type is a small `Rational(numerator: BigInt, denominator: BigInt)` in `epoch.internal` (D74).

*Implementation status (phase 4, reward-calculation):* `[DM-11a]`, `[RW-1]` to `[RW-9]`, `[RW-13]` to `[RW-20]`, `[RW-8a]`, `[EB-22]` and the `[EB-22]` part of `[RW-21]` are in the code. `EpochTransition.rewardUpdate(go, fee, blocksMadePrev, reserves, env)` is the calculation; `transition` runs it last, on the state after `[EB-21]`, and stores `Some` of it in the new field `EpochState.pendingRewards`, which the genesis sets to `None` (`[ES-8a]`). `[EB-5]` (`[RW-10]` to `[RW-12]`, the apply part of `[RW-21]`) is not in this item: the update is computed at every boundary and replaced at the next, and no balance, treasury or reserve changes yet. `RewardUpdate` is public in `scalus.cardano.ledger.epoch`; `Rational` is `private[epoch]` in `epoch.internal` and does not reduce its terms. `blocksTotal` sums every entry of `blocksMadePrev`, also of a pool that is not in `go`, as `startStep` does. The divisions by `sigma` in the leader and member formulas run only when `poolR > cost`, which needs `maxP > 0` and so `sigma > 0`, since `pledge ≤ selfDelegatedOwnersStake ≤ stake`. The NEWEPOCH runner passes an `EpochEnv` with the mainnet pparams and does not compare the result until `[CV-16]`. Tests: `RewardCalculationTest` (the vector of section 13.4; `Ideal` ignores the blocks; pledge not met; owner exclusion, a script credential with an owner's hash bytes is not excluded; `poolR ≤ cost`; a leader and a member reward of one credential summed; `[RW-3]`, `[RW-8a]`, `[RW-14]`, `[RW-16]`, `[RW-17]`; a 300-case property test against a port of the reference `rewardOld` and `rewardOnePool` with `d = 0`, which also checks `deltaT`, `deltaR`, `deltaF` and `[RW-9]`), `EpochTransitionTest` (`[EB-22]`, `[RW-21]`) and `EmulatorEpochTest`.

### 13.3 Application

- At the next boundary `[EB-5]` MUST add each reward to its account when the account is registered then. `[RW-10]`
- `[EB-5]` MUST add the rewards of unregistered accounts to the treasury. `[RW-11]`
- `[EB-5]` MUST add `deltaT` to the treasury, `deltaR` to the reserves and `deltaF` to `fees`. `[RW-12]`
- `[EB-5]` MUST be identity when `pendingRewards` is `None`, and `[EB-22]` MUST replace `pendingRewards` at every boundary. `[RW-21]`

**Why** `[RW-21]`: NEWEPOCH applies `nesRu` only when it is `SJust` and clears it (`C/Rules/NewEpoch.hs:172-178, 190`); RUPD then makes the next one. At the first boundary after the genesis nothing is pending (`[ES-8a]`), so `deltaT` and the first rewards land at the second boundary.

*Implementation status (phase 4, reward-application):* `[EB-5]`, `[RW-10]` to `[RW-12]` and `[RW-21]` are the private step `EpochTransition.applyRewards(ledger, epoch)`, the first step of `transition`, before SNAP (`applyRUpdFiltered` at PV ≥ 7, `S/LedgerState/IncrementalStake.hs:95-128`). Registration is read from the accounts at the boundary, before POOLREAP, as NEWEPOCH applies the update to `es0` (`C/Rules/NewEpoch.hs:172-179`). So a reward to the reward account of a pool that retires at the same boundary is paid, and the deposit refund of `[EB-8]` adds to it. The step sets `pendingRewards := None`; `[EB-22]` then sets the next update. Two consequences of the order: SNAP (`[EB-6]`, `[SN-9]`) sees the paid rewards in the balances and the fees after `deltaF`, and `[EB-22]` reads the reserves after `deltaR`. So the fee snapshot of boundary n is the fees of epoch n − 1 alone, and `State.fees` keeps them until `deltaF` of boundary n + 1 takes them out; it no longer grows across boundaries. Tests: `EpochTransitionTest` (the registered and unregistered split with the three deltas and a check that the four pots keep their sum; `[SN-9]` with a paid reward; a reward to the account of a pool that retires at the same boundary; the `[EB-22]` test now expects the reserves and fees after the old update), `EmulatorEpochTest` and `EmulatorParityTest` (the treasury tests that cross two or more boundaries now add the `deltaT` of each). Measured again (2026-11-03, JVM, `EmulatorEpochTest` "a jump over 1000 epochs, then reads and a submit" run alone): `setSlot` over 1000 epochs plus the first read takes 14.1 to 14.5 ms in three runs, against 5.8 to 16.4 ms after SNAP alone; that emulator has no pool, so the reward update of each boundary is the no-stake case.

### 13.4 Example, normative test vector for `[RW-2]`

Inputs: `reserves = 35 × 10^15`, `fee = 0`, `Ideal`, `rho = 3/1000`, `tau = 1/5`, `a0 = 3/10`, `nOpt = 500`. One pool: stake `10^13`, pledge 0, no owner stake, cost `170 × 10^6`, margin `1/100`. One member holds all `10^13`.

| Quantity | Value |
|---|---|
| `deltaR1` | 105 000 000 000 000 |
| `deltaT1` | 21 000 000 000 000 |
| `R` | 84 000 000 000 000 |
| `totalStake` | 10 000 000 000 000 000 |
| `maxP` = `poolR` | 64 615 384 615 |
| leader | 814 453 846 |
| member | 63 800 930 768 |
| `deltaR2` | 83 935 384 615 386 |

### 13.5 Timing, illustrative

A tx in epoch e delegates credential c to pool P.

| Boundary | Effect for c |
|---|---|
| e+1 | c is in `mark` |
| e+2 | c is in `set` |
| e+3 | c is in `go`; `[EB-22]` computes c's reward for epoch e+2 |
| e+4 | `[EB-5]` pays it |

*Implementation status (phase 4, reward-application):* `EmulatorEpochTest` tests this table on the Emulator: a `registerAndDelegateToPoolAndDRep` tx in epoch e puts the credential in `mark` at e+1, `set` at e+2 and `go` at e+3, and e+4 pays the reward of an independent BigInt port of the formulas; with `[ES-8]`, seeded stake is paid at boundary 2. Both tests then withdraw the exact reward. A key-hash withdrawal needs a DRep delegation at PV 11 (`ConwayWdrlNotDelegatedToDRep`, `C/Rules/Ledger.hs:518`), so the seeded case first delegates the vote: `EmulatorStakeRegistration` seeds no DRep delegation.

## 14. Emulator surface

- `ChainConfig` MUST gain `poolPerformance: PoolPerformance`, with `Ideal` in `ChainConfig.instant` and `ChainConfig.manual`. `[ES-1]`
- `ChainConfig` MUST gain `activeSlotsCoeff: Rational`, with `1/20` in `ChainConfig.instant` and `ChainConfig.manual`. `[ES-2]`
- `ChainConfig` MUST gain `blockIssuer: BlockIssuer`, one of `Unattributed` or `Pool(poolId)`, with `Unattributed` in both presets. `[ES-3]`
- The forge MUST add one block to `blocksMadeCur` of the issuer pool. `[ES-4]`
- The forge MUST NOT count an `Unattributed` block. `[ES-5]`
- `EmulatorInitialState` MUST gain `reserves`, `committee`, `committeeHotKeys` and `constitution`. `[ES-6]`
- When `reserves` is not given, the Emulator MUST set it to 6 029 414 056 167 760 lovelace, mainnet at epoch 665 (Koios `/totals`). `[ES-7]`
- When the treasury is not given, the Emulator MUST set it to 1 392 896 975 262 771 lovelace, mainnet at epoch 665. `[ES-7a]`
- The Emulator MUST NOT create a UTxO for the ADA that is outside the Emulator. `[ES-7b]`

**Why** `[ES-7]` to `[ES-7b]`: owner decision on Q6 (2026-11-02). The formula takes `totalStake = maxLovelaceSupply − reserves` (`S/LedgerState/PulsingReward.hs:144, 304-306`). So with mainnet reserves, a test pool has the share of stake and the reward it would have on mainnet. A UTxO for the rest adds nothing when it is not delegated, and it outweighs every test voter when it is. Tests set other pools, stake and reserves through `EmulatorInitialState`.

*Implementation status (phase 3, design):* `[ES-7]` and `[ES-7a]` are one value, `AccountState.mainnet = AccountState(Coin(1_392_896_975_262_771L), Coin(6_029_414_056_167_760L))`, in `scalus.cardano.ledger.epoch` (D54). `[ES-7]` `EmulatorInitialState` gains `reserves: Coin = AccountState.mainnet.reserves` as its ninth field, with a deprecated 8-argument constructor and companion `apply` and a filter for the 8-argument `copy`, the D33 pattern; the `Builder` gains `reserves(Coin)`; `JsEmulatorOptions` gains `reserves?: bigint` beside its `treasury` option, with the `ReversedMissingMethodProblem` filters the gov options have, and `scalus.d.ts` is regenerated. The public `EmulatorState.initial` overloads and the `Emulator` constructors that take no `EmulatorInitialState` use the default; one new `private[node]` `EmulatorState.initial` overload takes the reserves, and `EmulatorBase.fromInitialState` passes `initState.reserves`. `[ES-7a]` "not given" is the env default (D53): `UtxoEnvDefaults.testMainnet` and the JS `treasury` option (`JEmulator.scala:1686`, "Defaults to 0") take `AccountState.mainnet.treasury`; `UtxoEnv.default` (Testnet, "TODO: remove") stays at zero. The conformance harness sets its env treasury to zero itself (`CardanoLedgerVectors.scala:107-116`), so D37 is unaffected. Touched tests: `WithdrawalsTest.scala:85-86` and `ValueNotConservedUTxOValidatorTest.scala:613-614` state `Some(Coin.zero)` against a default env and must state `context.env.treasury`. The CHANGELOG MUST list in bold that `Context.testMainnet()` and the JS default now carry the mainnet treasury, so a tx that states `currentTreasuryValue = 0` against them fails with `TreasuryValueMismatch`. `[ES-7c]` The touched-test list above covers the shared and JVM test dirs of `scalus-cardano-ledger`, `scalus-testkit` and `scalus-examples` only; the implementer greps `scalus-cardano-ledger/js/src/test`, `scalus-examples/jvm`, `scalus-site/content`, `scalus-skills` and the ts-exporter fixtures for a zero-treasury assumption (`getTreasury() === 0n`, `currentTreasuryValue` of 0) before the change.

*Implementation status (phase 3, code, epoch-state-and-tick):* `[ES-7]`, `[ES-7a]`, `[ES-7b]`, `[ES-7c]` and the account-state part of `[ES-9]` (D55) are in the code, with one change to D54, D59: `EmulatorInitialState` gains `reserves` as its ninth field without a deprecated 8-argument constructor and `apply` and without an 8-argument `copy` filter, since the 8-field shape never shipped (it came in `b85d9ebf2`, after `v1.4.1`; MiMa compares against 1.4.0); the deprecated 5-argument forms pass `AccountState.mainnet.reserves`. JS: `Emulator.create` rejects negative `reserves` with a `TypeError`, as it does a negative `treasury`; the two `ReversedMissingMethodProblem` filters of `JsEmulatorOptions.reserves` are in `build.sbt`; `scalus.d.ts` is regenerated. `[ES-7a]`: correction to the touched-test list. `WithdrawalsTest.scala:85-86` states a zero withdrawal, not a treasury, and needs no change; `ValueNotConservedUTxOValidatorTest.scala:614` validates against `Context()`, whose `UtxoEnv.default` keeps 0, and now states `defaultContext.env.treasury` only for clarity. The one test that failed is `ByronAddressInScriptContextTest.scala:105` (an Emulator tx with `currentTreasuryValue = Some(Coin.zero)`), which now states `emulator.treasury`. The `[ES-7c]` grep found `EmulatorJsTest` "getTreasury is 0 ...", the npm test `__tests__/emulator.test.ts` "defaults the treasury to 0" and the npm README comment "defaults to 0", all updated; `scalus-examples/jvm`, `scalus-site/content`, `scalus-skills` and the ts-exporter fixtures state no zero treasury. `[ES-11]`: no factory has a flag; `[ES-12]` and `[ES-7c]` are bold CHANGELOG lines; the `[ES-12]` line names the rules generally, and the items that land POOLREAP and expiry keep it true.

*Example, illustrative,* mainnet pparams, `Ideal`, pool cost 170 ADA, margin 1 %:

| Delegated to the pool | Member reward per epoch |
|---|---|
| 10 000 ADA | 0 (pool reward 2.86 ADA, below the cost) |
| 1 000 000 ADA | 115 ADA |
| 10 000 000 ADA | 2 666 ADA |

*Implementation status (phase 4, reward-application):* correction to the table, checked against `maxPool`, `memberRew` and `leaderRew` (`S/Rewards.hs:86-157`) with pledge 0, the `[ES-7]` reserves, fee 0 and the mainnet pparams: the member reward is 114.475 ADA for 1 000 000 ADA (not 115) and 2 659.45 ADA for 10 000 000 ADA (not 2 666); the 10 000 ADA row is right (pool reward 2.856 ADA, all of it the leader's). Test: `EmulatorEpochTest` "mainnet-like pool: …" checks the first two rows to the lovelace against an independent BigInt port of the formulas.
- The Emulator MUST fill `mark`, `set` and `go` from the initial state at genesis. `[ES-8]`
- `EmulatorBase` MUST provide reads for: reserves, current epoch, current pparams, proposals, one gov action state, committee, constitution, one DRep state, the three snapshots, the pending reward update. `[ES-9]`
- Every new surface MUST follow the interop rules: no default parameters, `with<Field>` copy methods, Java and JS forms. `[ES-10]`
- Every Emulator factory MUST run the epoch rules. There MUST NOT be a flag that turns them off. `[ES-11]`
- The CHANGELOG MUST list `[ES-11]` in bold as a migration hazard: retiring pools are reaped, and delegated accounts earn rewards. `[ES-12]`
- When `EmulatorInitialState` gives no committee, the gov state MUST have no committee. `[ES-13]`
- When `EmulatorInitialState` gives no constitution, the constitution MUST have no guardrails script. `[ES-14]`

**Why** `[ES-11]`: owner decision on Q4 (2026-11-02). The Emulator is to behave like a node, and a node has no switch for epoch rules.

**Why** `[ES-13]`: owner decision on Q7 (2026-11-02). A test that needs ratification seeds a committee (`[ES-6]`). Without one, only NoConfidence and UpdateCommittee can pass (`[RT-6]`).

**Why** `[ES-1]`: in `Instant` each tx makes one block, at any slot, so `blocksMade / expectedBlocks` is close to 0. Under `Measured`, rewards would be close to 0. `Ideal` gives the rewards of a node where every pool is perfect, which is what a dApp test expects. `Measured` exists for tests of pool performance. Owner decision on Q1 (2026-11-02).

**Why** `[ES-8]`: without it, seeded stake earns its first reward at boundary 4 (section 13.5). With it, at boundary 2. Owner decision on Q2 (2026-11-02): the Emulator never starts from a real genesis, so it seeds the snapshots.

*Example, illustrative,* for `[ES-6]`: a test that wants a ParameterChange to pass seeds one committee member with a hot key and threshold `1/1`, and sets `dRepVotingThresholds` low. Without a committee the gov state is in no-confidence, and ParameterChange can never pass (`[RT-6]`).

- The Emulator MUST seed `committeeState` with a `HotCredential` entry per seeded hot key, and MUST reject a hot key whose cold credential is not a seeded committee member. `[ES-6a]`

**Why** `[ES-6a]`: GOVCERT rejects the same authorization with `ConwayCommitteeIsUnknown` (`[GC-2]`); a genesis must not reach a state a tx cannot reach.

*Implementation status (phase 2, design):* `[ES-6]` `EmulatorInitialState` gains `committee: Option[Committee]`, `committeeHotKeys: Map[Credential, Credential]` (cold to hot) and `constitution: Option[Constitution]` as fields 6 to 8, with the defaults `None`, `Map.empty`, `None` as the other five fields have; a deprecated 5-argument constructor and companion `apply` without defaults keep the 1.4 shape, and the 5-argument `copy` gets a MiMa filter (D33). A call that names only the old five fields resolves to the deprecated overload, since resolution prefers the alternative that needs no default, so `Builder.build()` and `JEmulator` pass all eight. The `Builder` is the Java form of `EmulatorInitialState` and gains `committee(Committee)`, `addCommitteeHotKey(cold: Credential, hot: Credential)` and `constitution(Constitution)`; the case class gets no `with<Field>` methods, as it has none today; `JsEmulatorOptions` gains `committee`, `committeeHotKeys` and `constitution`, credentials given as `credentialType` and `credentialHash` like `JDRepRegistration`, with the `ReversedMissingMethodProblem` filters the earlier options have; `scalus.d.ts` is regenerated. `[ES-13]`, `[ES-14]` `GovState.empty` holds `committee = None` and `Constitution(Anchor("", <32 zero bytes>), None)`; a seeded constitution replaces it whole. The genesis `State` is built in `EmulatorBase` with the gov state and `deposited`; `Emulator.withState` starts from it through `EmulatorState.initial(ledger: State, …)` and `EmulatorBase.fromState`, `buildInitialState` keeps its public tuple signature, and `EmulatorState.depositsOf` becomes `private[node]` (D34). `[ES-9]`, gov part: `EmulatorBase.govState: GovState`, `getGovState` in `EmulatorJavaApi`, and `getGovState()` in `JEmulator` as a JS object with the proposals in order (id, votes, action kind, `proposedIn`, `expiresAfter`), the committee and the constitution; a single proposal is `govState.proposal(id)`, a DRep state is `certState.vstate.dreps` (D35).

*Implementation status (phase 2, code, emulator-gov-surface):* `[ES-6]`, `[ES-6a]`, `[ES-13]`, `[ES-14]` and the gov part of `[ES-9]` are in the code as the design note above says, with these refinements. `reserves` of `[ES-6]` waits for phase 3 (`[ES-7]`). `EmulatorBase.buildInitialState` seeds `committeeState` with one `HotCredential` per hot key and throws `IllegalArgumentException` for a hot key whose cold credential is not a seeded member, also when no committee is seeded; JS `Emulator.create` turns it into a `TypeError`. `EmulatorBase.genesisLedger` builds the genesis `State`, and `EmulatorBase.fromInitialState` is the one body of `Emulator.withState` on both platforms (D34). The deprecated 5-argument forms carry the version `1.4.1`, the last tag. The Java form is `getGovState` returning the `GovState` itself, as `getProtocolParams` returns `ProtocolParams`. The JS shape is D42. `TxBuilder` does not collect the cold-key witness of `AuthCommitteeHotCert` and `ResignCommitteeColdCert` (`TransactionStepsProcessor.scala`, "not supported"), so `sign` drops the cold key's signature; an Emulator test adds the cold key with `requireSignature`. Rollback and mempool revalidation need no gov code: the snapshots carry `State.govState`, so a rollback removes a proposal and revalidation drops a vote on it with `GovActionsDoNotExist` (`EmulatorGovTest`).

*Implementation status (phase 3, design):* the account-state part of `[ES-9]` (D55): `EmulatorState.accountState: AccountState` is public, the one read of the account state, as `ledger` and `context` are of the ledger. On it: `EmulatorBase.reserves: Coin` and `EmulatorBase.currentEpoch: EpochNo` (`slotConfig.epochOf(currentContext.env.slot)`); `getReserves` and `getCurrentEpoch` in `EmulatorJavaApi`; `getReserves(): bigint` and `getEpoch(): number` in `JEmulator`, beside `getTreasury()` and `getSlot()`; `ImmutableEmulator.reserves`, since its `state: State` does not reach the account state. `treasury` keeps reading `context.env.treasury`, which `[DM-13]` keeps equal to `accountState.treasury`. No `with<Field>` methods on `AccountState` and `EpochState`: no caller builds or edits them, they are read results (`[ES-10]` covers the surface a caller gives: `EmulatorInitialState.reserves`, the Builder and the JS option).

Rules added in the phase 4 design round (agent, 2026-11-03):

- Correction to `[ES-2]`: Scalus has no off-chain `Rational` (the on-chain `prelude.Rational` is not for this), so `activeSlotsCoeff` MUST have type `UnitInterval`, default `UnitInterval(1, 20)`, and `ChainConfig` MUST reject a value of 0. `[ES-2a]`
- `PoolPerformance` MUST be an enum `Ideal, Measured` in `scalus.cardano.ledger.epoch`; `BlockIssuer` MUST be an enum `Unattributed, Pool(poolId: PoolKeyHash)` in `scalus.cardano.node.emulator`, with the factory `BlockIssuer.pool(poolId)` for Java. `ChainConfig` MUST gain `withPoolPerformance`, `withActiveSlotsCoeff` and `withBlockIssuer`. `[ES-1a]`
- The forge MUST count every block it forges, an empty block and a block of `produceBlock(on, …)` included, in `blocksMadeCur` of the block's snapshot, under the `poolId` of the `blockIssuer` of the `ChainConfig`, whether or not that pool is registered. The `epoch` of the block snapshot MUST be the `epoch` of the ticked base snapshot of the forge plus that count, never the `epoch` of the last mempool entry's snapshot (chain spec `[FG-6]`). The header MUST keep the fixed issuer bytes of chain spec `[BH-8]`. `[ES-4a]`
- At genesis, `mark`, `set` and `go` MUST each be the snapshot of `[SN-1]` of the genesis ledger state, `fee` MUST be 0, `blocksMadePrev` and `blocksMadeCur` MUST be empty, and `pendingRewards` MUST be `None`. `[ES-8a]`
- The stake reads of `[ES-9]` MUST be `snapshots: SnapShots` and `pendingRewards: Option[RewardUpdate]` on `EmulatorState`, `EmulatorBase` and `ImmutableEmulator`; Java `getSnapshots` and `getPendingRewardsOrNull`; JS `getStakeSnapshots()` and `getPendingRewards()`. `[ES-9a]`
- The JS options MUST gain `poolPerformance?: "ideal" | "measured"`, `activeSlotsCoeff?: Fraction` (the shape of D42) and `blockIssuer?: string`, a hex pool id; an absent `blockIssuer` is `Unattributed`, and a bad value is a `TypeError`. `[ES-10a]`

**Why** `[ES-4a]`: BBODY counts each block for the hash of its issuer key (`incrBlocks`, `A/Rules/Bbody.hs:250-257`, run by Conway BBODY at `C/Rules/Bbody.hs:288`; with `d = 0` no slot is an overlay slot) and RUPD reads only the pools of `go`. The Emulator has no pool cold key, only the pool id, so it counts the id it is given; a header whose issuer key hashes to the pool id would need the cold verification key, for no rule that reads it. The base `epoch` matters because an entry snapshot is `base.after(newLedger, tx)`, which keeps the `EpochState` of the base at submit time: when a forge leaves entries in the mempool (`forgePrefix`), the next block's last entry still carries the `EpochState` from before the first block, and taking it would drop the first block's count. The rule is sound since no tx rule writes `EpochState`. `branchBlock` builds its entries on its own base, so `produceBlock(on, …)` follows the same rule. **Why** `[ES-8a]`: owner decision on Q2: seeded stake earns from the second boundary on (section 13.5 with `[ES-8]`); a reward update at genesis would pay at the first boundary, which no node does.

*Implementation status (phase 4, design):* `[ES-1]` to `[ES-5]`: `ChainConfig` gains `poolPerformance`, `activeSlotsCoeff` and `blockIssuer` as fields 5 to 7 (D75); its constructor, `apply` and `copy` are private, but MiMa may still report the constructor and `copy`, and the implementer takes per-symbol filters with a comment from `mimaReportBinaryIssues`. `BlockNode.of` takes the issuer and builds the block snapshot as the last entry's snapshot with `epoch = before.epoch` plus the count, so a rollback removes the count with the block (D76). `EpochTransition.transition(ledger, epoch, boundary, env)` gains an `EpochEnv(params, epochLength, poolPerformance, activeSlotsCoeff)` of `epoch.internal`, and `LedgerSnapshot.atSlot` takes the `ChainConfig` to build it with `context.env.params` and `context.slotConfig.epochLength` (D84). `[ES-8a]` is in the one genesis builder, the `private[node]` `EmulatorState.initial` overload with the reserves (`N/EmulatorState.scala:829-859`), so `Emulator.withState` and `EmulatorBase.fromInitialState` seed it too. `[ES-9a]` (D82): `getPendingRewardsOrNull` follows `getTransactionOrNull`; `getSnapshots` returns the Scala `SnapShots`, as `getGovState` returns `GovState`. The JS shapes: `getStakeSnapshots(): { mark, set, go: StakeSnapshotEntry[]; fee: bigint }` with `StakeSnapshotEntry { credential, rewardAddress, pool, stake }`, the rows of `activeStake`, and no pool rows (as D42 left out the proposal procedure); `getPendingRewards(): RewardUpdate | undefined` with `{ deltaT, deltaR, deltaF: bigint; rewards: { credential, rewardAddress, amount: bigint }[] }`. The new `JsEmulatorOptions` members take the `ReversedMissingMethodProblem` filters of the earlier options; `scalus.d.ts` is regenerated. The CHANGELOG line of `[ES-12]` gains: from the second boundary on, every boundary moves `deltaT` from the reserves to the treasury, also with no stake (about 3.6 M ADA with the defaults of `[ES-7]`).

*Implementation status (phase 4, snapshots-and-blocks):* `[ES-3]` to `[ES-5]`, `[ES-4a]`, the `blockIssuer` part of `[ES-1a]` and `[ES-10a]`, and `[ES-8]`, `[ES-8a]` are in the code. `BlockIssuer` (`Unattributed`, `Pool(poolId)`) is in `N/emulator/ChainConfig.scala`, with the Java forwarders `BlockIssuer.unattributed()` and `BlockIssuer.pool(poolId)` as `ChainView` has; Java sees the pool id as a `ByteString`, since `PoolKeyHash` is an opaque type. `ChainConfig` gains `blockIssuer` as its fifth field, not its seventh: `poolPerformance` and `activeSlotsCoeff` come with the reward calculation, whose item MAY put them before it, since the constructor, `apply` and `copy` are private. `BlockNode.of` takes the issuer, in both forge paths (`forge` and `branchBlock`). The JS option `blockIssuer` is the hex of a 28-byte pool id; any other string is a `TypeError` that names the option. MiMa reports no problem for `ChainConfig` (it is new since 1.4.0); the two `ReversedMissingMethodProblem` filters of `JsEmulatorOptions.blockIssuer` are in `build.sbt`, and `scalus.d.ts` is regenerated. The blocks made are not readable from JS until the stake reads of `[ES-9a]` land; the JS test checks only that the option is taken and that a bad value is rejected. Since `[ES-9a]` landed, the JS tests read the effect of `poolPerformance`, `activeSlotsCoeff` and `blockIssuer` through the `deltaT` of `getPendingRewards()` after one block and one boundary (`JEmulatorChainTest`). Tests: `EmulatorEpochTest` (`[ES-5]` with both presets; `[ES-4]` with empty and non-empty blocks of an unregistered pool, rotated by `[EB-21]` at the next boundary; a rollback removes the count; `produceBlock(on, txs)` counts), `BlockRefScriptsSizeTest` (`[ES-4a]`: six 200 KiB txs forged as two blocks after `rollback(6)` in `Instant` give a count of 2; a mutation that takes the last entry's epoch state gives 1), `JavaEmulatorChainInteropTest`, `JEmulatorChainTest`.

*Implementation status (phase 4, reward-calculation):* `[ES-1]`, `[ES-2a]`, the rest of `[ES-1a]` and of `[ES-10a]` are in the code. `ChainConfig` has `poolPerformance`, `activeSlotsCoeff` and `blockIssuer` as fields 5 to 7 (D75), with `withPoolPerformance` and `withActiveSlotsCoeff`; its `require` rejects an `activeSlotsCoeff` of 0. `PoolPerformance` has the Java forwarders `PoolPerformance.ideal()` and `measured()`, as `BlockIssuer` has. `LedgerSnapshot.atSlot(slot, config)` builds the `EpochEnv` of D84 from `context.env.params`, `context.slotConfig.epochLength` and the `ChainConfig`. JS: `poolPerformance` is `"ideal"` or `"measured"`; `activeSlotsCoeff` is a `Fraction` of two safe integers with `0 < numerator ≤ denominator`; any other value is a `TypeError` that names the option. The four `ReversedMissingMethodProblem` filters of the two options are in `build.sbt`, and `scalus.d.ts` is regenerated. Tests: `EmulatorEpochTest` (the presets; the first boundary computes the update of the seeded stake under `Ideal`; under `Measured` with `f = 1/432 000`, `Unattributed` blocks pay nothing and one block of `Pool(P)` pays what `Ideal` pays), `JavaEmulatorChainInteropTest`, `JEmulatorChainTest`.

*Implementation status (phase 4, reward-application):* `[ES-9a]` is in the code as D82 says. `EmulatorState.snapshots` and `pendingRewards` read the epoch state of the ticked tip snapshot; `EmulatorBase` reads them from one state read each, and `ImmutableEmulator` from its `emulatorState`. Java: `getSnapshots` and `getPendingRewardsOrNull` in `EmulatorJavaApi`. JS: `getStakeSnapshots(): StakeSnapshots` (`mark`, `set`, `go` as `StakeSnapshotEntry[]`, `fee: bigint`) and `getPendingRewards(): RewardUpdate | undefined` (`deltaT`, `deltaR`, `deltaF: bigint`, `rewards: RewardEntry[]` with `credential`, `rewardAddress`, `amount`); `pool` of a `StakeSnapshotEntry` is a `string`, not optional, since every `activeStake` credential has a delegation (`[DM-10a]`). The JS shape carries no blocks made and no pool rows (D82). No new option, so no new MiMa filter; `scalus.d.ts` is regenerated. Tests: `EmulatorEpochTest` "the stake reads give …", `ImmutableEmulatorTest`, `JavaEmulatorChainInteropTest`, `JEmulatorChainTest`.

Rules added in the phase 5 design round (agent, 2026-11-03):

- The ratify read of `[ES-9]` MUST be `pendingRatify: RatifyState` on `EmulatorState`, `EmulatorBase` and `ImmutableEmulator`, read from the ticked tip snapshot; Java `getPendingRatify`; JS `getPendingRatify(): PendingRatify` with `enacted` and `expired` as arrays of the gov action id shape of `getGovState()` (D42), `enacted` in order, and `delayed: boolean`. There MUST be no read of the last ratify result (D94). `[ES-9b]`
- The current pparams MUST change at a boundary by `[EB-13a]`: `fetchLatestParams`, `cardanoInfo`, `getProtocolParams`, the `subscribeProtocolParams` stream of `StreamingEmulator` and the validation of every later tx MUST read them, and mempool revalidation after a boundary MUST use them. The CHANGELOG MUST say that the pparams of an Emulator change when a ParameterChange or a HardForkInitiation is enacted. `[ES-15]`

*Implementation status (phase 5, design):* `[ES-9b]`: the JS shape carries no enact state, as D42 left out the proposal procedure; a test reads the result of an enactment after the boundary through `getGovState()`, `getProtocolParams()` and the balances. *Updated (agent, final review):* no JS test reads an enacted pparam through `getProtocolParameters()`; the JVM `[ES-15]` test reads it through `currentParams`, and the JS method reads the same `cardanoInfo`. The new `EmulatorJavaApi` member takes the MiMa filters that `mimaReportBinaryIssues` reports, and `scalus.d.ts` is regenerated. `[ES-15]` needs no new code beyond `[EB-25]`: every reader goes through `currentContext.env.params` of the ticked tip (`N/EmulatorBase.scala:281, 296`, `N/EmulatorState.scala:163, 235, 306`). The CHANGELOG line of `[ES-15]` is not bold: before phase 5 no gov action could be enacted, so no existing test sees a change.

*Implementation status (phase 5, code, epoch-enactment):* `[ES-9b]` is in the code. `EmulatorState.pendingRatify` reads the epoch state of the ticked tip snapshot; `EmulatorBase.pendingRatify` and `ImmutableEmulator.pendingRatify` read it. Java: `getPendingRatify` in `EmulatorJavaApi`; MiMa reports nothing for it. JS: `getPendingRatify(): PendingRatify` with `enacted` and `expired` as arrays of `GovActionRef { txHash, index }`, the id fields of `GovActionState`, and `delayed`. `expired` is a set, so the JS array is sorted by `txHash`, then `index`. `scalus.d.ts` is regenerated. `[ES-15]` holds with no new code, except for the stream (superseded by the next note). Tests: `EmulatorEnactmentTest` (acceptance 9, the ratify then enact shape of the lifecycle example of section 15 with lifetime 2, with a committee member, a DRep and the SPO security-group threshold at 0; acceptance 14, the mempool drop under `Manual` and one move against two), `JavaEmulatorChainInteropTest`, `ImmutableEmulatorTest`, `JEmulatorGovTest` (an InfoAction in `expired` after its last epoch). A mutation that never takes the enacted pparams fails the three tests of `EmulatorEnactmentTest`.

*Implementation status (phase 5, code, review round 1):* `[ES-15]`: the hub of `StreamingEmulator` was seeded with the pparams of construction, and nothing updated them, so `subscribeProtocolParams` never emitted enacted pparams. Now `StreamingEmulator` gives the hub the pparams of the emulator each time it syncs (`SubscriptionHub.updateParams`), also after a rollback that removes the blocks that ratified them. *Review round 2:* `subscribeProtocolParams` syncs before it registers, so a subscriber that comes after an enactment made on the wrapped emulator starts at the enacted pparams with no stream operation between. Test: `EmulatorEnactmentTest` "a protocol-params subscriber of a StreamingEmulator sees the enacted pparams" (the late subscriber, and a rollback of the committee vote that restores the old pparams).

## 15. Ratify and enact (phase 5)

- `[EB-20]` MUST run RATIFY over the proposals in insertion order, sorted stably by priority: NoConfidence, UpdateCommittee, NewConstitution, HardForkInitiation, ParameterChange, TreasuryWithdrawals, InfoAction. `[RT-1]`
- The ratify environment MUST use `reCurrentEpoch = n`, the new mark pool distribution, and the DRep distribution of `[RT-3]`. `[RT-2]`
- The DRep distribution MUST add, per account delegated to a DRep: its UTxO stake, its reward balance, and the deposits of proposals whose return account it is. `[RT-3]`
- The SPO distribution MUST add the proposal deposits of each delegator to its pool's stake. `[RT-4]`
- `[RT-5]` is split into `[RT-5a]` to `[RT-5e]`. RATIFY MUST enact an action only when all five hold.
- The action's parent MUST equal the root of its purpose; TreasuryWithdrawals and InfoAction have no parent. `[RT-5a]`
- For UpdateCommittee, every new member term MUST be at most `reCurrentEpoch + committeeMaxTermLength`. `[RT-5b]`
- No delaying action MUST have been enacted earlier in this run. `[RT-5c]`
- For TreasuryWithdrawals, the sum MUST be at most the treasury of the enact state. `[RT-5d]`
- The committee, the SPOs and the DReps MUST each accept by the threshold table below. `[RT-5e]`
- NoConfidence, UpdateCommittee, NewConstitution and HardForkInitiation MUST count as delaying actions. `[RT-5f]`
- RATIFY MUST treat the committee threshold as "never" when there is no committee or when the active committee is smaller than `committeeMinSize`. `[RT-6]`
- The committee ratio MUST exclude expired, resigned and unauthorized members, and abstain votes. `[RT-7]`
- The SPO ratio MUST count a non-voting pool as No for HardForkInitiation, and otherwise by its reward account's DRep delegation. `[RT-8]`
- The DRep ratio MUST ignore DReps with `reCurrentEpoch > expiry`. `[RT-9]`
- RATIFY MUST mark an action expired when `expiresAfter < reCurrentEpoch` and it is not enacted. `[RT-10]`
- ENACT MUST implement the table of `C/Rules/Enact.hs:83-134`. `[EN-1]`
- ENACT MUST apply a `ParameterChange` with `ProtocolParamUpdate.applyTo`. `[EN-2]`
- HARDFORK into PV11 MUST fill `vrfKeyHashes`. `[EN-3]`
- The Emulator MUST NOT enact a HardForkInitiation to major 12 or higher; RATIFY MUST treat it as not accepted, and the Emulator MUST log a warning. `[EN-4]`

**Why** `[EN-4]`: PV12 (Dijkstra) rules are out of scope. Running Conway rules under a PV12 label would give wrong results silently. Owner decision on Q8 (2026-11-02).

Thresholds, normative for `[RT-5e]` (`C/Governance/Internal.hs:337-540`). "never" = `NoVotingThreshold`, the action cannot pass. "auto" = `NoVotingAllowed`, the body counts as accepting.

| Action | Committee | SPO | DRep |
|---|---|---|---|
| NoConfidence | auto | `pvtMotionNoConfidence` | `dvtMotionNoConfidence` |
| UpdateCommittee | auto | `pvtCommitteeNormal`, or `pvtCommitteeNoConfidence` when there is no committee | `dvtCommitteeNormal`, or `dvtCommitteeNoConfidence` when there is no committee |
| NewConstitution | committee threshold | auto | `dvtUpdateToConstitution` |
| HardForkInitiation | committee threshold | `pvtHardForkInitiation` | `dvtHardForkInitiation` |
| ParameterChange | committee threshold | `pvtPPSecurityGroup` if a security-group param changes, else auto | maximum over the changed groups of network, economic, technical and gov thresholds (`:319-335`) |
| TreasuryWithdrawals | committee threshold | auto | `dvtTreasuryWithdrawal` |
| InfoAction | never | never | never |

- A threshold of 0 MUST count as accepted without a ratio (`C/Rules/Ratify.hs:130, 175, 234`). `[RT-11]`
- A ratio with a zero denominator MUST be 0 (`C/Rules/Ratify.hs:144, 208, 259`). `[RT-12]`
- The SPO ratio MUST be yes / (total active − abstain), weighted by pool stake with proposal deposits. `[RT-13]`
- For the SPO default vote of `[RT-8]`: AlwaysNoConfidence MUST count as Yes on NoConfidence and No otherwise; AlwaysAbstain MUST count as Abstain; any other delegation MUST count as No. `[RT-14]`
- The DRep ratio MUST count a non-voting active DRep as No, AlwaysNoConfidence as Yes on NoConfidence and No otherwise, and MUST exclude AlwaysAbstain. `[RT-15]`

Voter permission, normative for `[GV-11]` (`C/Governance/Internal.hs:381-540`):

| Action | CC | DRep | SPO |
|---|---|---|---|
| NoConfidence | no | yes | yes |
| UpdateCommittee | no | yes | yes |
| NewConstitution | yes | yes | no |
| HardForkInitiation | yes | yes | yes |
| ParameterChange | yes | yes | only if a security-group param changes |
| TreasuryWithdrawals | yes | yes | no |
| InfoAction | yes | yes | yes |

**Why** `[RT-1]` to `[RT-15]`: `C/Rules/Ratify.hs:136-360`, `C/Governance/Internal.hs:337-552`, `C/Governance/DRepPulser.hs:208-249`. Port these functions one by one; the order of checks matters.

*Example, illustrative,* lifecycle with `govActionLifetime = 6`:

| Epoch | Event |
|---|---|
| 10 | proposal submitted (`expiresAfter = 16`); votes cast |
| boundary 11 | `[EB-20]` ratifies it with `reCurrentEpoch = 11`, using the votes cast in epoch 10 |
| boundary 12 | `[EB-11]` to `[EB-14]` enact it and refund the deposit |

A vote cast in epoch 11 counts only from boundary 12. The last ratify attempt is at boundary 17; removal and refund happen at boundary 18.

Rules added in the phase 5 design round (agent, 2026-11-03), read from `C/Rules/Ratify.hs:118-379`, `C/Rules/Enact.hs:83-134`, `C/Governance/Internal.hs:319-552`, `C/Governance/DRepPulser.hs:208-249, 389-425`, `C/Governance.hs:567-576`, `cardano-ledger-core State/SnapShots.hs:446-465`, `C/PParams.hs:643-708, 1182-1229` and `C/Era.hs:175` at the pin:

- The DRep distribution of `[RT-3]` MUST be computed over every account with a DRep delegation, from the state of `[EB-20a]`: its coin in `EpochTransition.instantStake` of the UTxO, plus its balance, plus the deposits of the proposals left after `[EB-12]` whose return account has its credential (`proposalsDeposits`, `C/Governance/Proposals.hs:566-579`). The entry MUST be added also when it is 0. A credential DRep MUST count only when it is registered; AlwaysAbstain and AlwaysNoConfidence always count (`computeDRepDistr`, `C/Governance/DRepPulser.hs:208-249`). `[RT-3a]`
- The SPO distribution of `[RT-4]` MUST hold the pools of `snapshots.mark.pools` with `numDelegators > 0`, each with its `stake`, and its total MUST start at `snapshots.mark.totalActiveStake` (`calculatePoolDistr'`, `State/SnapShots.hs:449-465`). Then, for each account with a pool delegation to a pool of the distribution and a proposal deposit (the sum per credential of `[RT-3a]`), whether or not it delegates to a DRep, the deposit MUST be added to that pool and to the total (`addToPoolDistr`, `C/Governance/DRepPulser.hs:228-236`). The accounts are those after POOLREAP, so a delegator of a pool that retired at the boundary adds nothing, while the pool keeps its mark stake. `[RT-4a]`
- The committee is active when its active members, the members with a `HotCredential` entry in `committeeState` and `reCurrentEpoch ≤` their expiry, number at least `committeeMinSize` (`votingCommitteeThresholdInternal`, `C/Governance/Internal.hs:460-495`). `[RT-6a]`
- In the committee ratio of `[RT-7]`, an active member with no vote MUST count as No (`committeeAcceptedRatio`, `C/Rules/Ratify.hs:143-168`). `[RT-7a]`
- `[RT-9]` MUST read the stored `expiry` of the DRep state after `[EB-15]`, not the expiry with the dormant epochs added; an unregistered credential DRep MUST count neither in the numerator nor in the denominator (`dRepAcceptedRatio`, `C/Rules/Ratify.hs:258-281`). `[RT-9a]`
- The SPO default vote of `[RT-8]` and `[RT-14]` MUST look up the pool in `stakePools` and its reward account in the accounts, both after POOLREAP; a pool or an account that is missing, or an account with no DRep delegation, MUST count as No (`defaultStakePoolVote`, `C/Governance.hs:567-576`). `[RT-14a]`
- At major protocol version 9 (`hardforkConwayBootstrapPhase`, `C/Era.hs:175`), read from the pparams of the enact state, RATIFY MUST take every DRep threshold but that of InfoAction as 0 (`C/Governance/Internal.hs:525-527`), MUST NOT apply the `committeeMinSize` check of `[RT-6]` (`:475`), and MUST count a pool with no vote as Abstain, except on HardForkInitiation (`spoAcceptedRatio`, `C/Rules/Ratify.hs:213-216`). `[RT-16]`
- Every value RATIFY reads from the enact state MUST be read from the enact state at that point of the run, with the changes of the actions enacted before it in the same run: the roots of `[RT-5a]`, the `committeeMaxTermLength` of `[RT-5b]`, the treasury of `[RT-5d]`, the committee and every threshold and `committeeMinSize` of `[RT-5e]` (`ratifyTransition`, `C/Rules/Ratify.hs:318-360`). `[RT-17]`
- The ratio comparisons of `[RT-5e]` MUST be exact: `ratio ≥ threshold` over rationals of `BigInt` (D74). `[RT-18]`
- UpdateCommittee MUST enact as follows: with no committee, the new committee is the added members and the new threshold, and the removed members are ignored; with a committee, its members are the added members and the old members not removed, an added member's term replacing an old one, with the new threshold (`updatedCommittee`, `C/Rules/Enact.hs:118-134`). `[EN-5]`
- TreasuryWithdrawals MUST enact by adding each amount to `withdrawals` under the credential of its reward account, summed per credential, and by subtracting the sum from the treasury of the run (`C/Rules/Enact.hs:97-103`). `[EN-6]`
- `ProtocolParamUpdate.applyTo(params: ProtocolParams): ProtocolParams` MUST set each field of the table below that the update holds and MUST keep every other field, `protocolVersion` included (`conwayApplyPPUpdates`, `C/PParams.hs:1182-1229`). `[EN-2a]`
- `[EN-4]` MUST make RATIFY treat a HardForkInitiation to major 12 or more as not accepted, after the checks of `[RT-5a]` to `[RT-5d]`, so it delays nothing and expires by `[RT-10]`. The warning of `[EN-4]` is withdrawn (D91). `[EN-4a]`

`[EN-2a]` field table, normative. The key is the CBOR key of the update; the group is that of `C/PParams.hs:643-708` (N network, E economic, T technical, G gov; S marks the security group).

| Key | `ProtocolParamUpdate` | `ProtocolParams` | Group |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `minFeeA` | `txFeePerByte` | E, S |
| 1 | `minFeeB` | `txFeeFixed` | E, S |
| 2 | `maxBlockBodySize` | `maxBlockBodySize` | N, S |
| 3 | `maxTxSize` | `maxTxSize` | N, S |
| 4 | `maxBlockHeaderSize` | `maxBlockHeaderSize` | N, S |
| 5 | `keyDeposit` | `stakeAddressDeposit` | E |
| 6 | `poolDeposit` | `stakePoolDeposit` | E |
| 7 | `maxEpoch` | `poolRetireMaxEpoch` | T |
| 8 | `nOpt` | `stakePoolTargetNum` | T |
| 9 | `poolPledgeInfluence` | `poolPledgeInfluence` | T |
| 10 | `expansionRate` | `monetaryExpansion` | E |
| 11 | `treasuryGrowthRate` | `treasuryCut` | E |
| 16 | `minPoolCost` | `minPoolCost` | E |
| 17 | `adaPerUtxoByte` | `utxoCostPerByte` | E, S |
| 18 | `costModels` | `costModels`: `CostModels(old.models ++ update.models)`, so a language in the update replaces the old model of that language and the others stay (`updateCostModels`, `cardano-ledger-core Plutus/CostModels.hs:401-411`) | T |
| 19 | `executionCosts` | `executionUnitPrices` | E |
| 20 | `maxTxExUnits` | `maxTxExecutionUnits` | N |
| 21 | `maxBlockExUnits` | `maxBlockExecutionUnits` | N, S |
| 22 | `maxValueSize` | `maxValueSize` | N, S |
| 23 | `collateralPercentage` | `collateralPercentage` | T |
| 24 | `maxCollateralInputs` | `maxCollateralInputs` | N |
| 25 | `poolVotingThresholds` | `poolVotingThresholds` | G |
| 26 | `drepVotingThresholds` | `dRepVotingThresholds` | G |
| 27 | `minCommitteeSize` | `committeeMinSize` | G |
| 28 | `committeeTermLimit` | `committeeMaxTermLength` | G |
| 29 | `governanceActionValidityPeriod` | `govActionLifetime` | G |
| 30 | `governanceActionDeposit` | `govActionDeposit` | G, S |
| 31 | `drepDeposit` | `dRepDeposit` | G |
| 32 | `drepInactivityPeriod` | `dRepActivity` | G |
| 33 | `minFeeRefScriptCoinsPerByte` | `minFeeRefScriptCostPerByte`: the `floor` of the interval, as `ConwayProtocolParams.toProtocolParams` takes it (D89) | E, S |

The DRep threshold of a ParameterChange is the largest of `dvtPPNetworkGroup`, `dvtPPEconomicGroup`, `dvtPPTechnicalGroup` and `dvtPPGovGroup` over the groups of its fields, 0 for no field (`pparamsUpdateThreshold`, `C/Governance/Internal.hs:319-335`); the SPO threshold is `pvtPPSecurityGroup` when a field is in S, else "auto" (`:396-400`).

*Implementation status (review round 1):* key 33 is a known divergence of D89. The ledger keeps `minFeeRefScriptCostPerByte` as a `NonNegativeInterval` and uses the rational in the reference-script fee. After an enacted non-integer value, for example `25/2`, the Emulator uses 12 where the ledger uses 12.5. So the Emulator can accept a tx whose fee the ledger rejects as too small. A fix keeps the interval in `ProtocolParams` for the fee computation; no network carries such a value.

**Why** `[RT-4a]`: the ledger seeds the pulser with the pool distribution of the new mark (`ssStakeMarkPoolDistr snapshots1`, `C/Rules/Epoch.hs:300`) and the accounts of the state after EPOCH (`dpAccounts`, `C/Governance.hs:501`), and adds a deposit only to a pool it finds in that distribution. **Why** `[RT-17]`: a ParameterChange is not a delaying action, so a parent and its child can both enact in one run, and a `committeeMinSize` raised by a ParameterChange applies to a TreasuryWithdrawals later in the same run (`votingCommitteeThreshold` reads `rsEnactStateL . ensCurPParamsL` of the running state, `C/Governance/Internal.hs:441-450`). **Why** `[RT-16]`: GOV keeps the PV 9 exemptions (D40); RATIFY keeps them too, at the cost of three conditions. **Why** `[EN-2a]` at key 33: the ledger type is `NonNegativeInterval`, the Scalus field is a `Long`; mainnet, preprod and preview carry 15, so the floor is exact on every network.

*Implementation status (phase 5, design):* RATIFY and ENACT are one object `Ratify` in `scalus.cardano.ledger.epoch.internal`: `ratify(…)`, a port of `ratifyTransition` with the check order of `C/Rules/Ratify.hs:337-341` (`[RT-5a]`, `[RT-5b]`, `[RT-5c]`, `[RT-5d]`, then the three bodies), `enact(…)`, and the two distributions of `[RT-3a]` and `[RT-4a]` as public functions, so the NEWEPOCH runner compares them (`[CV-19]`). `RatifyState` stores neither distribution (D95); with no proposal, `[EB-20]` computes neither and returns the enact state of `[EB-20a]` with nothing enacted or expired. The field groups of `[EN-2a]` live once, in `Ratify`; `GovMutator.changesSecurityGroup` (D32) calls it (D90). `ProtocolParamUpdate.applyTo` is public in `scalus-core` (`SC/ProtocolParamUpdate.scala`), an addition, so MiMa needs no filter; it does not reduce the rationals of the update, and the dumps hold reduced values, so the runner compares by `==`. HARDFORK of `[EB-19a]` is a private step `hardFork` of `EpochTransition`.

*Implementation status (phase 5, code, enact-and-pparams):* `[EN-1]`, `[EN-2]`, `[EN-2a]`, `[EN-5]` and `[EN-6]` are in the code. `Ratify.enact(state: EnactState, treasury: Coin, id: GovActionId, action: GovAction): (EnactState, Coin)` returns the treasury of the run beside the enact state (D86); it does not check `[RT-5d]`, so a caller that skips that check can get a negative treasury. A HardForkInitiation sets `params.protocolVersion` (`ensProtVerL`, `C/Rules/Enact.hs:93-96`). `ProtocolParamUpdate.applyTo` is in `SC/ProtocolParamUpdate.scala` as D89 says. `[EN-4a]` is the predicate `Ratify.isConwayAction(action)`, false for a HardForkInitiation to major 12 or more; RATIFY MUST call it after `[RT-5a]` to `[RT-5d]` and treat a false result as not accepted. No code calls it yet (superseded: `Ratify.ratify` calls it, see the ratify note). `[EN-3]` is `[EB-19a]`. Tests: `ProtocolParamUpdateApplyTest` (shared `scalus-core`, acceptance 12: one case per key, each changing exactly its field, `2/10` kept unreduced, a cost model merge, `15/2` gives 7), `EnactTest` (each action; UpdateCommittee with no committee, with a member both removed and added, and with a new term for a kept member; the `[EN-4a]` predicate).

*Implementation status (phase 5, code, ratify):* `[RT-1]` to `[RT-18]` and `[EN-4a]` are in `Ratify` (`L/epoch/internal/Ratify.scala`). `Ratify.ratify(env: RatifyEnv, enactState: EnactState, treasury: Coin, proposals: Vector[GovActionState]): RatifyState` ports `ratifyTransition` with the check order of `C/Rules/Ratify.hs:337-341`, and `[EN-4a]` (`isConwayAction`) after `[RT-5d]` and before the three bodies. `RatifyEnv` is the ledger's `RatifyEnv` (`C/Governance/Internal.hs:562-571`) without `reInstantStake`, which RATIFY does not read. The distributions are public functions (D95): `Ratify.dRepDistribution(ledger: State): Map[DRep, Coin]` (`[RT-3a]`) and `Ratify.poolDistribution(ledger: State, mark: StakeSnapshot): PoolDistribution` (`[RT-4a]`, `PoolDistribution(stake, total)`); `proposalDeposits` is private. D90 is done: the `[EN-2a]` group table is `Ratify.modifiedGroups` and `Ratify.changesSecurityGroup` (with the enum `PParamGroup`), and `GovMutator` calls the latter. Refinements: the ledger recurses and inserts an expired id after the rest of the run; expiry reads nothing the run changes, so `ratify` is one fold over the sorted proposals, with the same sets. `delayed` is `delayed || isDelaying(action)`: the ledger overwrites it (`rsDelayedL .~`), but nothing enacts once it is true, so both give the same value. A threshold is an `Option[UnitInterval]`: `None` is `NoVotingThreshold` and 0 is `NoVotingAllowed` (`toRatifyVotingThreshold`, `C/Governance/Internal.hs:346-350`); the short circuit `r == minBound` tests the numerator, so `0/5` passes as `0/1` does. The running protocol version of `[RT-16]` changes only by a HardForkInitiation, which delays the rest of the run, so no test can see a bootstrap branch change inside one run. Tests: `RatifyTest` (the `[EN-2a]` group of each key; `[RT-3a]` and `[RT-4a]`; `[RT-1]` priority and order of submission; `[RT-5a]` to `[RT-5d]`, `[RT-10]`, `[EN-4a]`; `[RT-17]` with a parent and a child ParameterChange, the max term length and a `committeeMinSize` raised in the run; each threshold row of the committee, SPO and DRep columns, `[RT-6]`, `[RT-6a]`, `[RT-7]`, `[RT-7a]`, `[RT-8]`, `[RT-9]`, `[RT-9a]`, `[RT-11]` to `[RT-15]`, `[RT-14a]`; `[RT-16]` in three tests). Acceptance items 1 (both withdrawal cases), 2, 4, 5, 6, 7 and 8 have unit tests of `ratify` or of the distributions; the Emulator items 9, 10 and 14 wait for the gov-side item, since only that item applies an enactment to the ledger state (superseded: the gov-side item landed, and `EmulatorGovLifecycleTest` and `EmulatorEnactmentTest` cover items 9, 10 and 14). The two zero-denominator tests of `[RT-12]` assert a rejection, so they passed against the stub too. *Review round 1:* `RatifyTest` adds an `[RT-18]` case that a `Double` comparison fails (`3333333333333333 / 10^16` against `1/3`, with a one-lovelace control) and an `[RT-6a]` case of a member with no `committeeState` entry.

## 16. Phases

Ordered by testing impact. Each phase lands as its own branch and keeps CI green.

### Phase 0 – tx parity fixes

- Scope: `[TX-1]`, `[TX-2]`, `[TX-4]`, `[BB-1]` to `[BB-4]`, `[CV-1]`.
- No state change.
- Acceptance:
  1. A failing test per fix on its parent commit (memory rule "fix needs a failing test"). For `[TX-1]`: a PlutusV3 spend with the same UTxO in inputs and reference inputs at PV11 fails; the same tx with a PlutusV2 script passes.
  2. The 36 DELEG, CERTS and GOVCERT dirs pass. Note: GOVCERT dirs with committee certs may need phase 2; list them as expected failures with a pointer to phase 2.
  3. A Manual-preset test with three 400 KiB ref-script txs forges two blocks.
- Risks: the TxBuilder may produce overlapping txs for PlutusV3 at PV11 by default (commit `123babebd`). Check `SpendAndReferenceSameUtxoTest` and change the builder if it does.

### Phase 1 – ledger bookkeeping

- Scope: `[DM-3]`, `[DM-4]`, `[BK-1]` to `[BK-8]`, `[DR-1]` to `[DR-6]`, `[DR-1a]`, `[DR-4a]`, `[CV-4]`, `[CV-5]`.
- Acceptance:
  1. `[CV-3]`: remove the `deposited`, `dreps.delegates` and `dreps.expiry` exclusions; all run dirs pass.
  2. Unit tests of the example in section 10.1.
  3. A PV11 test of a duplicate VRF key on a new pool and on a re-registration, and of two new pools with one VRF key in one tx (`[BK-8]`).
- Risks: `VotingState` and `PoolsState` are public case classes. New fields break `copy` and `apply` for MiMa. Precedent: `UtxoEnv` gained `treasury` with a deprecated old constructor, and `copy` broke (`2026-10-07-scalus-emulator-provider-design.md:345`). Decided in D17: the new primary constructors take no defaults; a deprecated constructor and a deprecated companion `apply` keep the 1.4 arity and defaults; `VotingState.empty` and `PoolsState.empty` replace the zero-argument calls; per-symbol MiMa filters with a comment for `copy` and for the synthetic default-argument methods that vanish, taken from `mimaReportBinaryIssues` output (baseline `scalusStableVersion = 1.4.0`). `committeeState` stays in phase 2 and costs a second forwarder round (D24).

### Phase 2 – governance tx rules

- Scope: `[DM-1]`, `[DM-2]`, `[DM-5]`, `[DM-5a]`, `[DM-6]`, `[GV-1]` to `[GV-20]`, `[GV-2a]`, `[GV-3a]`, `[GV-3b]`, `[GV-7a]`, `[GV-10a]`, `[GV-10b]`, `[GV-11a]`, `[GV-17a]`, `[GC-1]` to `[GC-4]`, `[GC-2a]`, `[ES-6]`, `[ES-6a]` (committee, hot keys, constitution), the gov parts of `[ES-9]`, `[ES-13]`, `[ES-14]`, `[CV-2]`, `[CV-6]`, `[CV-7]`, `[CV-8]`.
- Acceptance:
  1. The 56 GOV dirs and the tx steps of the EPOCH, RATIFY, ENACT and LEDGER dirs pass, with the harness epoch of `[CV-8]`, and with any `expectedFailures` or `Exclusion` entry scoped and explained (D37).
  2. `[CV-3]`: remove the `govState` exclusion, and the four GOVCERT expected failures of phase 0. The test decoder decodes the gov state by `[CV-6]` (`scalus-cardano-ledger/jvm/src/test/scala/scalus/testing/conformance/LedgerState.scala:10`).
  3. Unit tests for each PV11-only failure: `UnelectedCommitteeVoters`, `nOpt = 0`; and for `[GV-17a]`, `[GC-2a]` and `[GV-15]`, which the vectors cover thinly or not at all.
  4. An Emulator test: a vote by a seeded DRep on a missing action fails with `GovActionsDoNotExist`; the same vote by an unregistered DRep fails with `VotersDoNotExist` (`[GV-10b]`); a proposal and a vote on it by that DRep pass, and `govState.proposal(id)` shows the vote.
  5. `ImmutableEmulatorTest` "keep every field of the ledger state" round-trips a non-empty gov state (one proposal and a committee), since an empty one cannot fail the assertion.
- Risks: replacing `GovState = Array[Element]` changes a public type, with the filters of D27 and the CHANGELOG line of `[DM-5a]`. The vectors are PV10, so PV11-only checks have unit tests only. The harness replays every case with a treasury of 0 and, before `[CV-8]`, at epoch 899; `[CV-8]` lands first, and what it cannot derive takes D37 entries.

### Phase 3 – epoch boundary core

- Scope: `[DM-8]`, `[DM-9]` (account state and `lastEpoch`, D66), `[DM-13]`, `[DM-14]`, `[EB-1]` to `[EB-4]`, `[EB-1a]`, `[EB-3a]`, `[EB-3b]`, `[EB-7]` to `[EB-9]`, `[EB-7a]`, `[EB-8a]`, `[EB-9a]`, `[EB-9b]`, `[EB-12]` (expiry only), `[EB-12a]`, `[EB-12b]`, `[EB-14]`, `[EB-15]`, `[EB-15a]`, `[EB-16]`, `[EB-17]`, `[EB-18]`, `[EB-23]`, `[EB-24]`, `[ES-7]`, `[ES-7a]`, `[ES-7c]`, the account-state part of `[ES-9]`, `[CV-9]` to `[CV-12]`, chain-spec amendments of section 18.
- Acceptance (ported Imp scenarios, `eras/conway/impl/testlib/Test/Cardano/Ledger/Conway/Imp/EpochSpec.hs`), as Emulator tests in a new `EmulatorEpochTest` (shared test dir), with `setSlot(firstSlotOfEpoch(n))` for `passEpoch`, and unit tests of the step functions in `EpochTransitionTest` (D58):
  1. "Expired proposal deposit refunded" (`:84`): lifetime 1, deposit 999; the proposal is present after 2 boundaries, gone after 3, and the return account holds the deposit.
  2. "Proposals are expired and removed as expected" (`:107`), restated on observable state, since Scalus has no pulser: lifetime 1; after 2 boundaries the proposal is still in `govState.proposals`; after 3 it is gone.
  3. "deposit is moved to treasury when the reward address is not registered" (Treasury block, `:415`, body `:451-486`): lifetime 8, deposit 100; 5 boundaries, the account is deregistered, 5 more boundaries; the treasury grew by exactly the deposit at the boundary of removal. Assert the delta of that boundary, not an absolute value: phase 4 adds `deltaT` and rewards at every boundary.
  4. "expiry is updated based on the number of dormant epochs" (DRep block, `:150`): lifetime 2, `dRepActivity` offsets as in the Imp timeline, the counter 0, 0, 1, 2, 3 at boundaries 1 to 5, reset by a second proposal with the expiry bumped by 3.
  5. Shelley Imp `EpochSpec` "Crosses epoch boundaries" (`eras/shelley/impl/testlib/Test/Cardano/Ledger/Shelley/Imp/EpochSpec.hs:39`): `setSlot` over n epochs gives `currentEpoch == start + n`, for n = 1 and n = 1000.
  6. The example of section 12.3, and a pool re-registration that takes effect at the next boundary; unit tests pin `[EB-7a]`, `[EB-8a]` (a re-registration with a new reward account and a new VRF key, retiring at the same boundary) and `[EB-9a]`, `[EB-9b]`, `[EB-12b]`.
  7. The existing donation tests stay green (`EmulatorParityTest.scala:372-466`, `EmulatorForkTest.scala:427-462`). *Amended (agent, phase 3 review):* one of them changed with D66, `EmulatorParityTest` "a donation made after a move back moves into the treasury once, at the next boundary" (it was "... going forward again"): a genesis at epoch `E` has run boundary `E`, so a donation made after `setSlot` to epoch `E − 1` moves at boundary `E + 1`, as on a node.
  8. `NewEpochConformanceTest` runs the 533 cases of `[CV-9]` by `[CV-10]` to `[CV-12]` with no `expectedFailures`; a case that still differs takes a D37 entry by case name with its cause.
- Risks: the tick memo of `[EB-3a]` replaces the (tip block id, slot) cache the risk note first proposed, which a direct edit would have served stale (D48). No repo test retires a pool and then crosses its epoch (checked 2026-11-03: `EmulatorTest.scala:623-665, 898-920` stay inside the epoch), so the CHANGELOG hazard of `[ES-12]` is for user tests only. `[ES-7c]` changes the default treasury, with its own bold line. `LedgerSnapshot`, `EmulatorBase.atSlot` and `EmulatorInitialState` need the MiMa filters of D46, D49 and D54.

*Implementation status (phase 3, tests, epoch-scenarios):* `EmulatorEpochTest` ports every Imp scenario of the list with the numbers and epochs of the Haskell test, and three more from the DRep block. Acceptance 1 now proposes a `txFeePerByte` ParameterChange, as Imp's `mkMinFeeUpdateGovAction` does, and also asserts that `txFeePerByte` is unchanged after the expiry. Acceptance 4 sets `dRepActivity = 100`, the Imp default behind `offDRepActivity 100`. Added: "Proposals survive multiple epochs without any activity" (`:62`): lifetime 4, a tree of 5 ParameterChange proposals (a root, two children, two grandchildren under the first child; policy `None`, since the Emulator constitution has no guardrail script), the proposals are equal after 5 boundaries and empty after the 6th. "expiry is not updated for inactive DReps" (`:189`): `dRepActivity = 2`, lifetime 2; at boundaries 3 to 5 the counter is 1, 2, 3, the stored expiry stays at `+2` and the actual expiry (`vsActualDRepExpiry`, `C/State/VState.hs:154`) is `+3`, `+4`, `+5`; the second proposal at `+5` sets the expiry to `+5`. The Imp title says "not updated", but its assertions show the bump: the actual expiry `+5` is not below the current epoch `+5` (`C/Rules/Certs.hs:330-333`), so the test name in Scalus states that. "expiry updates are correct for a mixture of cases" (`:240`): three DReps, `dRepActivity = 4`, with an `UpdateDRepCert` at `+4` (expiry `4 + 4 − 2`), an `UnregDRepCert` at `+4`, the dormant bump at `+5` and `+8`, and a DRep vote at `+10` on a proposal whose `expiresAfter` is `+10`; the counter and the stored and actual expiries match the Imp timeline at each of the 12 boundaries. Shelley "Crosses epoch boundaries" (`Shelley/Imp/EpochSpec.hs:39`) has, besides n = 1 and n = 1000, ten draws of n from 1 to 100 by a seeded generator for Imp's `Positive n <- arbitrary` (D63). The scenarios found no divergence. They exercise code of the earlier items, so each was shown discriminating by a one-line mutation of the step it checks: the `[EB-15]` activity test at `n − 1` reddens acceptance 4 and both new DRep tests; `[DR-2]` with `<=` for `<` reddens both new DRep tests; the `VotingOnExpiredGovAction` check with `<=` and `[DR-4]` (no vote update) and `[DR-1]` without the dormant subtraction each redden the mixture test; `[EB-12a]` at `expiresAfter < n` reddens acceptance 1 to 4, the tree test and the mixture test. The donation tests of acceptance 7 are unchanged by this item.

### Phase 4 – snapshots and rewards

- Scope: `[DM-9]` (snapshots, blocks, rewards; `prevParams` moved to phase 5, D68), `[DM-9a]`, `[DM-10]`, `[DM-10a]`, `[DM-10b]`, `[DM-11]`, `[DM-11a]`, `[DM-14a]`, `[DM-15]`, `[DM-16]`, `[DM-18]` to `[DM-21]`, `[DM-20a]`, `[DM-20b]`, `[DM-21a]`, `[EB-5]`, `[EB-6]`, `[EB-21]` (the blocks-made part only: `lastEpoch := n` landed in phase 3, D66), `[EB-22]`, `[SN-1]` to `[SN-10]`, `[RW-1]` to `[RW-21]`, `[RW-8a]`, `[ES-1]` to `[ES-5]`, `[ES-1a]`, `[ES-2a]`, `[ES-4a]`, `[ES-8]`, `[ES-8a]`, the stake parts of `[ES-9]` (`[ES-9a]`), `[ES-10a]`, the phase 4 part of `[CV-13]` (`[CV-14]` to `[CV-16]`), `[CS-5]`.
- Acceptance:
  1. The normative vector of section 13.4.
  2. A property test against a port of the reference `rewardOnePool` (`eras/shelley/test-suite/src/Test/Cardano/Ledger/Shelley/Rewards.hs:368-430`) with `d = 0`. The Shelley golden examples (`TwoPools.hs`) use `d = 0.5` (`Examples/Init.hs:51`), so they are not direct vectors.
  3. An Emulator test of section 13.5: delegate, cross four boundaries, withdraw the exact reward.
  4. A `Measured` test: an `Unattributed` chain pays no reward; a `Pool(P)` chain pays P.
  5. Optional IT test: one mainnet epoch with the it-data stake state (`StakeStateResolver`) against Koios account rewards.
  6. *Added (agent, phase 4 design):* `NewEpochConformanceTest` runs `[CV-14]` to `[CV-16]` on the 531 decoded cases with no `expectedFailures` entry; a case that still differs takes a D37 entry by case name with its cause.
  7. *Added (agent, phase 4 design):* the shared `ProtocolParams` test of the example for `[DM-20a]`, and a `fromBlockfrostJson` of a `rho` given as the string `"0.003"` and as the number `3e-3`, both `UnitInterval(3, 1000)`.
  8. *Added (agent, phase 4 design):* `EpochTransitionTest` unit tests for `[SN-7]` (a pool with no delegator), `[SN-8]` (an owner with zero stake counts in `selfDelegatedOwners` and `numDelegators`), `[SN-9]` (a reward paid at the boundary is in the new mark; a pool retiring at it is in the mark), `[RW-8a]` (a leader entry of 0 at the go reward account), `[RW-14]` (`expectedBlocks = 0`), `[RW-16]`, `[RW-17]` and `[RW-21]`; `EmulatorEpochTest` tests for `[ES-4a]` (a rollback removes the count; one mempool forged as two blocks gives `blocksMadeCur = 2`, as in the phase 0 test of six 200 KiB txs) and `[ES-8a]` (the first reward lands at the second boundary).
