- Risks: `Ideal` rewards change account balances in tests that cross epochs with delegated accounts; those that withdraw an exact seeded amount break (`[ES-11]`, `[ES-12]`). The rational pparam types break `scalus-core` binary compatibility (`[DM-21]`). Small test pools earn nothing for their members under mainnet reserves (`[ES-7]`); document the 600 000 ADA rule of thumb. *Added (agent, phase 4 design):* a seeded pool with `pledge > 0` whose owners do not delegate to it earns nothing (`maxP = 0`, `S/Rewards.hs:359-362`), so the rule of thumb also says "pledge 0, or delegate the owners"; the leader reward needs its reward account registered at the paying boundary, else it goes to the treasury (`[RW-11]`). `deltaT` lands at every boundary from the second one after the genesis (`[RW-21]`), so a test that crosses two or more boundaries and asserts an absolute treasury or reserves breaks; before the change the implementer greps `treasury` and `reserves` in `EmulatorParityTest`, `EmulatorForkTest`, `EmulatorEpochTest`, `EpochTransitionTest`, `EmulatorJsTest`, `ImmutableEmulatorTest`, the npm `__tests__` and `scalus-examples`, as `[ES-7c]` did. SNAP is O(UTxO + accounts) at every boundary, so the 1000-epoch test of section 12.1 is measured again. `ChainConfig` and `ProtocolParams` need the MiMa filters of D75 and D79.

### Phase 5 – ratify and enact

- Scope: `[DM-12]`, `prevParams` of `[DM-9]` (D68), `[EB-10]`, `[EB-11]`, `[EB-12]` (enactment; `[EB-12a]` is replaced by `pendingRatify.expired`), `[EB-13]`, `[EB-19]`, `[EB-20]`, `[RT-1]` to `[RT-15]`, `[EN-1]` to `[EN-4]`, mutable pparams, the phase 5 part of `[CV-13]`. *Added (agent, phase 5 design):* `[DM-9b]`, `[DM-9c]`, `[DM-12a]`, `[BK-7a]`, `[EB-10a]`, `[EB-11a]`, `[EB-12c]`, `[EB-12d]`, `[EB-13a]`, `[EB-14a]`, `[EB-19a]`, `[EB-20a]`, `[EB-22a]`, `[EB-25]`, `[RT-3a]`, `[RT-4a]`, `[RT-6a]`, `[RT-7a]`, `[RT-9a]`, `[RT-14a]`, `[RT-16]` to `[RT-18]`, `[EN-2a]`, `[EN-4a]`, `[EN-5]`, `[EN-6]`, `[ES-9b]`, `[ES-15]`, `[CV-17]` to `[CV-20]`.
- Acceptance (ported from `RatifySpec.hs` and `EnactSpec.hs`, names as in the vector dirs):
  1. ENACT "Treasury withdrawals.Modify EnactState as expected", "Withdrawals exceeding treasury submitted in several proposals within the same epoch".
  2. ENACT "Competing proposals.higher action priority wins", "proposals of same priority are enacted in order of submission".
  3. ENACT "Committee enactment.CC re-election", "NoConfidence", "Constitution", "futurePParams".
  4. RATIFY "Delaying actions.A delaying action delays all other actions even when all of them may be ratified in the same epoch".
  5. RATIFY "Expired and resigned committee members are discounted from quorum.Expired" and ".Resigned".
  6. RATIFY "Voting.Active voting stake.DRep.Proposal deposits contribute to active voting stake.Directly", "Rewards contribute to active voting stake".
  7. RATIFY "SPO default votes.After bootstrap phase.Reward account delegated to AlwaysNoConfidence".
  8. RATIFY "CommitteeMinSize affects in-flight proposals.TreasuryWithdrawal fails to ratify due to an increase in CommitteeMinSize".
  9. EPOCH "DRep.proposal is accepted after two epochs".
  10. The lifecycle example of section 15 as an Emulator test.
  11. *Added (agent, phase 5 design):* `NewEpochConformanceTest` runs `[CV-17]` to `[CV-20]` on the 531 decoded cases; the 7 entries of `[CV-20]` are its only `expectedFailures`, and `test_5591` and `test_6836` pass in full.
  12. *Added (agent, phase 5 design):* a shared `scalus-core` test of `ProtocolParamUpdate.applyTo` with one case per row of the `[EN-2a]` table, a cost model merge that keeps a language the update does not name, and a key 33 value of `15/2` that gives 7.
  13. *Added (agent, phase 5 design):* `EpochTransitionTest` unit tests for `[EB-11a]` (a withdrawal to an unregistered account stays in the treasury), `[EB-12c]` (a sibling and its child go with an enactment; the enacted proposal's child stays), `[EB-13a]` (no pparam action keeps edited pparams), `[EB-19a]` and `[BK-7a]` (section 12.2 note), `[RT-4a]` (a delegator of a pool that retires at the boundary adds no deposit), `[RT-16]` (PV 9), `[RT-17]` (a parent and a child ParameterChange enact in one run; a `committeeMinSize` raised in the run blocks a later TreasuryWithdrawals) and `[EN-4a]`. *Implementation status (review round 1):* `[EB-11a]`, `[EB-12c]`, `[EB-13a]`, `[EB-19a]` and `[RT-4a]` are in `EpochTransitionTest`; `[RT-16]` and `[RT-17]` are in `RatifyTest`, and `[RT-17]` also in `GovStateTest`, `EmulatorEnactSpecTest` and `EmulatorRatifySpecTest`; `[EN-4a]` is in `RatifyTest` and `EnactTest` (`EmulatorEnactSpecTest` "futurePParams" names it only to explain why it starts at PV 10); `[BK-7a]` is in `EmulatorGenesisVrfKeyHashesTest`. *Updated (agent, final review):* `EmulatorEnactSpecTest` "futurePParams" runs a PV 10 genesis through the fork to 11 and counts the VRF keys after it: none before, one per pool after.
  14. *Added (agent, phase 5 design):* an Emulator test of `[ES-15]` and `[EB-25]`: a ParameterChange of `txFeePerByte` is enacted at the second boundary after its votes; `getProtocolParams` shows it; a tx built with the old fee before the boundary and still in the `Manual` mempool is dropped by revalidation; a `setSlot` over both boundaries in one move gives the same pparams, `prevParams` and reward update as two moves.
- Risks: the ratify rules are long and order-sensitive; port the Haskell function by function. `ProtocolParamUpdate.applyTo` covers about 30 fields; test each one. *Added (agent, phase 5 design):* `[BK-7a]` changes the genesis VRF map of a PV 10 Emulator (an empty map), which no PV 10 rule reads; the 1000-epoch test of section 12.1 is measured again, and `[EB-20]` with no proposal computes no distribution (D95). `EpochState` gains two fields under the wildcard filter of `epoch.internal.*`; `RatifyState`, `EnactState` and `ProtocolParamUpdate.applyTo` are additions.

*Implementation status (phase 5, tests, ratify-enact-scenarios):* acceptance 1 to 10 are Emulator tests: `EmulatorEnactSpecTest` (items 1 to 3), `EmulatorRatifySpecTest` (items 4 to 8) and `EmulatorGovLifecycleTest` (items 9 and 10), on the shared fixture `GovImp`. The fixture is the Imp genesis of `initGenesis` (Conway `ImpTest.hs:259-306`): its voting thresholds, `committeeMinSize` 1, `committeeMaxTermLength` 20, `govActionLifetime` 30, `govActionDeposit` 123, `dRepDeposit` 70 ADA, `dRepActivity` 100, and a committee of two members with threshold 1/1 that expires 15 epochs after the start. The scenarios found no divergence. Refinements:

- The DReps and pools of `setupSingleDRep`, `setupDRepWithoutStake` and `setupPoolWithStake` are seeded at genesis; each DRep delegator delegates its vote by a tx in the first epoch. No assertion reads the epoch of the setup.
- Imp's `isCommitteeAccepted` and `isDRepAccepted` have no Emulator read (D94). The fixture ports them: `Ratify.ratify` on the one proposal, with Imp's `reCurrentEpoch = current epoch − 1` (`getRatifyEnv`, `ImpTest.hs:1029-1049`) and with the thresholds of the other bodies set to 0. "Expired" and "Resigned" assert only these reads, as Imp does; on "Expired", the constitution is proposed in the epoch where the committee is enacted, so no boundary can ratify it while the second member is active.
- Imp's `calculatePoolAcceptedRatio` (0, then 1) in "Staking address delegated to AlwaysNoConfidence" is read as whether the NoConfidence is enacted.
- "TreasuryWithdrawal fails to ratify due to an increase in CommitteeMinSize" sets `committeeMinSize` 2 at genesis, not after the first `passEpoch`: no proposal exists before that point, and Imp's `modifyPParams` patches the ratify state too (`ImpTest.hs:250-256`).
- Imp draws some values at random; the tests fix them inside Imp's ranges: the withdrawals 3, 2.5, 1.5 and 0.6 ADA against a treasury of 5 ADA (500 000 lovelace left); the fees 100, 400 and 800 lovelace per byte; a withdrawal of 50 ADA.
- "Modify EnactState as expected" runs ENACT twice in Imp. Through the Emulator both proposals enact in one RATIFY run, so only the end of the run is observable: withdrawals of 777 and 222, and a treasury of 1.
- "futurePParams" forks from PV 10 to 11: at PV 11 Imp's next major is 12, which `[EN-4a]` keeps out.
- In "Rewards contribute to active voting stake", Imp's pool delegator has no stake beyond the min-UTxO fixup of its output; here that output holds 1 ADA.
- The lifecycle example of section 15 maps the spec's epoch `10 + k` to the start epoch plus `k`. It has four tests: votes of epoch 10, a vote cast in epoch 11, no vote (expired at boundary 17, removed with the refund at 18), and a vote cast in epoch 16 (ratified at boundary 17).

Each test fails under at least one one-line mutation of the step it checks: no `[RT-5c]` check, no `committeeMinSize` check of `[RT-6]`, no `[EB-16]` committee-state filter, an inexact `[RT-18]` comparison, no deposits in `[RT-3a]`, AlwaysNoConfidence as No in `[RT-14]`, `[RT-10]` with `<=`, no `[RT-5d]` check, no `[EB-11a]` payment, a NoConfidence that keeps the committee, no `[EB-13a]` constitution, no balance in `[RT-3a]`, no `[RT-1]` sort, no expiry in `[RT-6a]`, and the order of submission reversed.

### Phase 6 – faithful, low value

- Scope: `[FL-1]` to `[FL-11]`, `[FL-7a]`, `[CV-21]` to `[CV-25a]` with `[CV-22a]`, `[CV-23a]`, `[CV-24a]` (sections 9.2, 9.3). *Decided (agent, phase 6 design):* the PV-dependent value size is a comment only (section 5.3 note, D101); the header size check moves to section 17 (D102); `nonMyopic` is not kept (D103).
- Acceptance. *Corrected (agent, phase 6 design):* the criterion "the conformance test compares the failure constructors" cannot be met: the PV10 vectors and the Dugite LEDGER dumps record no failure, only the verdict and the new state (section 9.2, D99). The constructors come from the Imp lists instead:
  1. Emulator tests (`LedgerFailuresImpTest`, shared test dir) of the five Imp lists of section 9.3, at PV 11, through `ledgerFailures` and the names of `[FL-6]`: each list is equal, in order. A mutation that reverses group (6) of `[FL-7]` fails the Conway CERTS row; one that puts group (2) before group (1) fails the Alonzo rows.
  2. Emulator tests of the single-failure Imp lists of the new exceptions: `ValidationTagMismatch (IsValid False) PassedUnexpectedly` (`eras/alonzo/impl/testlib/Test/Cardano/Ledger/Alonzo/Imp/UtxosSpec.hs:176`), `CollectErrors [NoCostModel]` (`:208`, and Conway `UtxosSpec.hs:534, 548` with PlutusV3), `CollectErrors [BadTranslation (ReferenceInputsNotDisjointFromInputs …)]` of `[TX-1]` (Conway `UtxosSpec.hs:129`). Each is red on its parent commit, where the rule name is `IllegalArgument`.
  3. A test of `[FL-5]`: a tx with a phase-1 failure and a PlutusV3 script that fails gives the phase-1 failure and no `ValidationTagMismatch`; a collect error after a phase-1 failure is still in the list. A mutation that evaluates anyway fails it.
  4. A test of `[FL-3]`: for a tx with a spent input and an expired validity interval, `submit` and mempool revalidation report what they reported before phase 6 (`UtxoNotAvailable`, `InputsSpent`), and `ledgerFailures` gives both names, `BadInputsUTxO` first, by `[FL-7]`.
  5. A unit test pins `ledgerNames` of every `TransactionException` case (`[FL-6]`); a test of the shelley unit-test vector of the `[FL-4]` note asserts `[BadInputsUTxO]` and names the `[FL-11]` (a) difference.
  6. `DugiteLedgerConformanceTest` passes on the sample of `[CV-25]` in CI, and on the full local archive, with only D37 entries that name a cause (`[CV-21]` to `[CV-25a]`). *Done (agent, phase 6):* 7 entries, all decode limits of the harness (section 9.2 note, D116).
  7. Java and JS tests of `getLedgerFailureNames` and `ledgerFailures` on one tx with two failures.
- Risks: the order of `[FL-7]` is derived from the interpreter. The Imp lists confirm groups (1), (2), (4) and (6), and the shelley unit test confirms (3); no Imp list has two failing certificates (`[FL-11]` (b)). `[FL-9]` and `[FL-10]` change `SubmitError.rule` of two kinds of rejection from `IllegalArgument` to `PassedUnexpectedly` and `CollectErrors`; the CHANGELOG lists it, not in bold. `TxBuilder.complete` must still fail with the `BalancingException` that names the input of `[TX-1]` (section 9 note), so the implementer checks what it catches. The full runner decodes about 6.8 GB of CBOR and evaluates the scripts of 7 446 txs, so it runs locally only. New public surface: the two exception cases and `CollectError` are additions; `EmulatorBase.ledgerFailures` and `EmulatorJavaApi.getLedgerFailureNames` take the filters that `mimaReportBinaryIssues` reports; `scalus.d.ts` is regenerated.
- Do this only on demand.

## 17. Consensus-only rules, skipped

| Rule | Ledger | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| OCERT | `libs/cardano-protocol-tpraos/src/Cardano/Protocol/TPraos/Rules/OCert.hs:31-50` | KES and op-cert counters; the forge writes fixed bytes |
| OVERLAY | `.../Rules/Overlay.hs:97-129` | VRF leader checks |
| PRTCL, TICKN, UPDN | `.../Rules/Prtcl.hs:112-218`, `Tickn.hs:65`, `Updn.hs:32` | nonces and header sequence; the chain spec keeps slot and block number invariants |
| TICKF | `C/Rules/Tickf.hs:60-92` | forecast for the leader schedule |
| `ObsoleteNodeCHAIN` | `Chain.hs:69` | node config |
| `HeaderSizeTooLargeCHAIN` | `Chain.hs:70-72` | `chainChecks` runs in the consensus header validation, not in a ledger STS; the forge makes the only headers, with real field sizes (chain spec `[BH-8]`), so the check fails only when the pparams reject every real header, which halts a real chain (D102) |
| `adoptGenesisDelegs` | `S/Rules/Tick.hs:112-140` | no genesis delegation certs in Conway |

## 18. Chain-spec and provider-spec amendments

- `[EB-1]` supersedes chain spec `[NL-2]`: `tick` runs the epoch transitions, not only the donation move. `[CS-1]`
- `[EB-17]` supersedes provider spec `[SC-7a]` and `[SC-7f]`: the donation moves at each boundary; with several boundaries the later ones see 0, so the result is the same. `[CS-2]`
- `[DM-13]` keeps provider spec `[SC-6]`, with the account state as the source. `[CS-3]`
- Chain spec section 18 item 1 and item 2 are replaced by phases 3 to 5. `[CS-4]`
- `[ES-4a]` amends the chain spec forge (section 4, `[BH-8]`): the forge counts each block for the `blockIssuer` of the `ChainConfig` in the ledger snapshot, whose `epoch` comes from the ticked base, not from the last entry of chain spec `[FG-6]`, and the header keeps its fixed issuer bytes. The phase 4 implementation commit adds an *Implementation status (ledger rules, phase 4)* note under chain spec `[BH-8]`, and one under chain spec `[CF-1]` for the three new `ChainConfig` fields of `[ES-1]` to `[ES-3]`. `[CS-5]`

*Implementation status (phase 3, design):* the phase 3 implementation commit edits the two other specs as follows, since this design round touches only this spec. `[CS-1]`: under chain spec `[NL-2]` an *Implementation status (ledger rules, phase 3)* note says that `tick` runs the epoch transitions of this spec's `[EB-1]` for every boundary the slot change crosses, that the tip view stays computed and is memoized per state (`[EB-3a]`), and that a direct edit lands on the stored tip before any pending boundary (`[EB-3b]`); the M3 note's sentence "it commutes with the tick, which changes only the treasury and the donations" is superseded by that note, and so is the comment at `N/EmulatorState.scala:526-527`. `[CS-2]`: under provider spec `[SC-7a]` and `[SC-7f]` a note says the move is step `[EB-17]` of this spec, run at every boundary. `[CS-3]`: under provider spec `[SC-6]` a note names `AccountState.treasury` as the source and `[DM-13]` as the invariant. `[CS-4]`: chain spec section 18 items 1 and 2 get "superseded by `2026-11-02-emulator-ledger-rules-design.md`".

*Implementation status (phase 3, code, epoch-state-and-tick):* `[CS-1]` and `[CS-4]` are notes in `2026-10-21-emulator-chain-design.md` (under `[NL-2]`, and on items 1 and 2 of its section 18); `[CS-2]` and `[CS-3]` are one note in `2026-10-07-scalus-emulator-provider-design.md` under section 13.3. The comment at `N/EmulatorState.scala:526-527` now states `[EB-3b]`.

## 19. Decisions

| # | Decision | Rejected |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | One spec for all gaps; phases by testing impact | one spec per rule family, as chain spec section 18 planned |
| D2 | Pparams stay in `Context.env` (`[DM-7]`) | pparams in the gov state as in Haskell |
| D3 | Rewards and ratify computed at the boundary; no pulsing, no point of no return | port the pulsers and the TICK schedule |
| D4 | `Ideal` performance by default (owner, Q1) | `Measured` by default; a random block schedule |
| D5 | Stake computed from the UTxO at the boundary; no incremental instant stake | maintain `utxosInstantStake` per tx |
| D6 | New epoch code in `scalus.cardano.ledger.epoch`; old objects deprecated | fix `RewardsCalculation` in place |
| D7 | GOV before rewards | rewards first: `addRewards` is a workaround, GOV has none |
| D8 | Vectors test tx rules only; boundaries use ported Imp scenarios | extend the Haskell dumper now (Q5) |
| D9 | Rational pparam types (owner, Q3) | decimal-string conversion of `Double` |
| D10 | Epoch rules always on (owner, Q4) | an opt-in flag for one release |
| D11 | No default committee (owner, Q7) | a test committee at genesis |
| D12 | Reject a hard fork to PV12 (owner, Q8) | enact it under Conway rules |
| D13 | A repeated Byron attribute key keeps its last value, as the node's `Map` does (agent, phase 0) | sum every occurrence |
| D14 | A Byron address a node cannot decode (attribute 1 not one whole bytestring, attribute 2 not a Word32) gets a size: all bytes of attribute 1, none of attribute 2 (agent, phase 0) | throw from `attributesSize`. A node rejects such a tx at decoding, so no choice has node parity; the residual gap is that the Emulator accepts such an output when its size is 64 or less |
| D15 | `ByronAddress.derivationPath` keeps returning the raw attribute-1 value (agent, phase 0) | return the decoded HD payload, which changes a public accessor for no rule |
| D16 | `CertsMutator` writes `State.deposited`, since it is the one mutator that holds the pre-CERTS cert state (`[BK-1]`) (agent, phase1) | the UTXO mutator, which runs after CERTS and would miss the refunds of unregistered accounts and DReps; reordering `DefaultMutators.all`, against provider spec `[SC-3a]`; threading the pre-CERTS state through `Context` |
| D17 | `VotingState` and `PoolsState` gain fields with no defaults; a deprecated constructor and companion `apply` keep the 1.4 arity and defaults; `.empty` vals; per-symbol MiMa filters for `copy` and the vanished default-argument methods (agent, phase1) | a wildcard filter, which AGENTS.md forbids for `scalus.cardano.ledger`; defaults on the new primary constructor, against the interop rule |
| D18 | The VRF map is `Map[VrfKeyHash, Long]` with the existing `VrfKeyHash` of `SC/Hashes.scala:126` (agent, phase1) | a `NonZero Word64` wrapper type as in Haskell; the saturation at `maxBound` is unreachable |
| D19 | `VRFKeyHashAlreadyRegistered` is a new `VrfKeyHashAlreadyRegisteredException` (agent, phase1) | a new field of `StakePoolException`, which breaks its `apply`, `copy` and constructor |
| D20 | The PV9 reverse-delegation bug (ledger issue 4772, `processDelegationInternal (pvMajor pv < 10)`) is not emulated (agent, phase1) | port the `preserveIncorrectDelegation` branch; the Emulator runs PV11 and the vectors PV10 |

The same epoch also sets `expiresAfter` of proposals and DRep expiries of such txs. A node cannot reach this, since its slot never goes below its tip; a node at `e + 1` rejects that retirement. A user who rewinds should not schedule retirements for an epoch at or below `lastEpoch` (test: `EmulatorEpochTest` "a retirement for a boundary the tip ran, accepted after a move back, is never reaped") (agent, phase 3 review) | validating at `max(epochOf(slot), lastEpoch)`: the validators see only `Context`, which has no `lastEpoch`, and a new `Context` field is a MiMa break in a mixed package; rejecting a `setSlot` below `lastEpoch`: breaks the move below a genesis tip that D66 keeps; retiring at `retiring <= n`: diverges from POOLREAP and still accepts a tx a node rejects |

Its GOV rule then rejects such a proposal with `ProposalCantFollow` (shelley `PParams.hs:304-306`), so both reject the tx. The Emulator takes a decoded tx, so its behaviour does not change, and the next node release has the bound 12. | D121 | `EmulatorInitialState` is `final case class … private (…)` with `empty` and one `with<Field>` method per field, the `ChainConfig` pattern, so a snapshot loader later adds fields and factories only; the 1.4 constructor, `apply` and `copy` stay deprecated with their 1.4 defaults (source compat for `EmulatorInitialState(utxos = u)`, binary compat for `copy$default$N` and `$lessinit$greater$default$N`); the class writes out a private 9-argument `copy`, since the synthetic one's defaults would forbid the 1.4 `copy`'s; the branch's 9-argument public `apply` and constructor go, as they never shipped; one `FinalClassProblem` filter (agent, release review) | keep the public 9-field case class, where every new field breaks the constructor, `apply` and `copy`; a 1.4 `copy` without defaults, which drops `copy$default$N` and so the 1.4 callers by field name |
| D122 | `EmulatorBase.ledgerState` (and `EmulatorState.ledgerState`) returns the whole ledger state of the ticked tip in the node's shape: `epoch.NewEpochState`, `epoch.EpochState` and `ledger.LedgerState`, each `final case class … private[scalus] (…)` with no defaults, so a snapshot loader can later add factories and a new field adds an accessor only; the internal `epoch.internal.EpochState` is renamed `EpochBookkeeping` to free the name; `params`, `prevParams`, `futureParams` and `pendingRatify` sit in `EpochState` beside `ledgerState`, since the Scalus `GovState` holds only the tx rules' part (D27, `[DM-7]`); `accountState` is the bookkeeping's, which `[DM-13]` keeps equal to `treasury`, and a test asserts it; the existing getters stay; the conformance test type `LedgerState` is renamed `VectorLedgerState`, as its shape (the vector's CBOR) differs (agent, release review) | the internal `LedgerSnapshot` made public, which exposes the context and the datum store; a flat Scalus-shaped record, which a node snapshot loader cannot fill field for field |
| D123 | `poolDistr` is `calculatePoolDistr` of `snapshots.set`: the node takes `ssStakeMarkPoolDistr` of the snapshots before EPOCH (`C/Rules/NewEpoch.hs:182`), whose mark SNAP then moves to `set`; a pool with no delegator is left out (`SnapShots.hs:449-463`); the share is a `UnitInterval` in lowest terms, since stake over total stake is at most 1 and fits a `Long` (agent, release review) | `mark`, which already holds the rewards of the last boundary; a public `Rational` type, which Scalus has only on-chain |
| D124 | `NonMyopic` is typed (`likelihoods: Map[PoolKeyHash, IndexedSeq[Float]]`, `rewardPot`), as `PoolRank.hs:88-101, 231-235`, and always `NonMyopic.empty` from the Emulator, D103 (agent, release review) | leave the field out, which a snapshot loader would then add as a breaking change |
| D125 | `futureParams` is an enum of the node's two solid cases, `NoPParamsUpdate` and `DefinitePParamsUpdate(params)`: definite when `pendingRatify` enacts a ParameterChange or HardForkInitiation that the tip's gov state still holds (`predictFuturePParams`, `C/Governance.hs:301-325`); never `PotentialPParamsUpdate`, since the ratify result is known whole at the boundary (D3); the companion forwards each case as a static method, `noPParamsUpdate` and `definitePParamsUpdate(params)`, as interop rule 9 requires (D127); Java reads it with `instanceof FuturePParams.DefinitePParamsUpdate` (agent, release review) | `Option[ProtocolParams]`; a third case the Emulator never produces |
| D126 | JS `getLedgerState(): LedgerState` is one flat readonly object from one state read, built from the shapes the single getters return (`Utxo[]`, `GovState`, `StakeSnapshots`, `RewardUpdate`, `PendingRatify`, `ProtocolParams`), plus `PoolBlocks` rows, `PoolDistr` with `PoolStake` rows (share numerator and denominator as `bigint`, since a lovelace fraction can pass 2^53; rows sorted by pool) and `futureProtocolParams` (`undefined` for `NoPParamsUpdate`); it leaves out the cert state and `NonMyopic`, which have no JS shape and which the account reads cover (agent, release review) | the nested Scala shape, which JS callers would walk three levels down for the UTxOs; a JS cert-state shape for this read alone |
| D127 | Interop rule 8 binds new public enums only, and rule 9 is a MUST: `BlockProduction`, `AwaitTxPolicy` and `OrphanTxPolicy`, not in 1.4.1, extend `java.lang.Enum`; the released parameterless enums keep their superclass until a major version, since MiMa rejects a new superclass; `FuturePParams` forwards both cases, as a forwarder is additive; the enums that break either rule are listed in the interop spec's status notes (release review) | retrofit the released enums, a binary break; keep rule 9 optional, which left Java no plain call to build a case |
| D128 | Java reads the ledger state through `Emulator.getLedgerState` and the getters of per-platform traits, interop Tier 1: `NewEpochStatePlatform` (`getBlocksMadePrev`, `getBlocksMadeCur` as `java.util.Map[PoolKeyHash, java.lang.Long]`, `getPendingRewardsOrNull`), `PoolDistrPlatform` (`getPools`) and `NonMyopicPlatform` (`getLikelihoods`, lists of `java.lang.Float`); the JS copies are empty, as JS reads `getLedgerState` of the JS Emulator (D126); the maps of `SnapShots`, `RewardUpdate` and `RatifyState` stay Scala-only for now, as before this branch (release review) | Java getters in the shared types, which add Java names to the Scala API; `scala.jdk` conversions in each Java caller |

**Why** D5: an incremental instant stake touches every tx mutator. The boundary computation is O(UTxO) once per epoch, which is cheap for an Emulator.

## 20. Open questions for the owner

Resolved 2026-11-02: Q1 `Ideal` (D4), Q2 seed the snapshots (`[ES-8]`), Q3 change the types (`[DM-18]` to `[DM-21]`), Q4 on by default (`[ES-11]`), Q6 mainnet-like reserves and treasury (`[ES-7]` to `[ES-7b]`), Q7 no committee (`[ES-13]`), Q8 reject (`[EN-4]`), Q9 keep the `Long` types (D44).



5. **Q5.** PV11 vectors. Findings (2026-11-02, research note `pv11-vectors-research.md` in the session notes):
   - Scalus's `vectors.tar.gz` is byte-identical to Cardano Blueprint `main` (`cardano-scaling/cardano-blueprint`, `src/ledger/conformance-test-vectors/`). Dingo (`blinklabs-io/ouroboros-mock`), gerolamo and the old Amaru harness use the same PV10 per-tx corpus. Amaru now vendors the scenario corpus of Blueprint PR #71 (still PV10) and checks only tx verdicts (`crates/amaru-ledger/tests/evaluate_ledger_states.rs:257, 327`).
   - No PV10-format corpus exists at PV11. Upstream cardano-ledger has a dump hook (`CONFORMANCE_CBOR_DUMP_PATH`, `libs/cardano-ledger-conformance/src/.../ExecSpecRule/Core.hs:262`) for LEDGER and NEWEPOCH, but it dumps only Haskell/Agda divergences. Its conformance Imp suite moved to PV11 in `4bc98a1c1` (2026-07-01), after the 1.22.1.0 tag.
   - The only PV11 corpus: Dugite (`michaeljfazio/dugite`) patches that hook to dump every case and publishes `ledger-rules.tar.gz` weekly (1.6 GB: 7 446 LEDGER, 533 NEWEPOCH transitions). Its pin is cardano-ledger master `a88b60bd` (conway 1.23.0.0), not the production pin, and the test names are lost.
   - No Imp-derived data tests the reward formula: Imp tests make no blocks, so eta = 0. Amaru tests rewards and pots against per-epoch stake-distribution fixtures that a Haskell extractor pinned to node 11.0.1 produces from mainnet, preprod and preview snapshots (`crates/amaru/tests/summary.rs`). Dingo compares with Koios (`internal/koiosparity/`).
   - Recommendation: (a) keep the PV10 tarball as a regression set; (b) prototype a `NewEpochState` decoder and a NEWEPOCH runner on a NEWEPOCH-only slice of Dugite's corpus (owner approved; downloaded 2026-11-02: release `conformance-corpus-v20261101-084724`, `ledger-rules.tar.gz` sha256 `018c480caad1abf311b7203998d0e905b743d17ee26213edc4575d027faab996`, 10.2 GB unpacked, of which NEWEPOCH is 533 cases in 20 MB, each with `ctx`, `env`, `sig`, `st`, `st_out` CBOR files); (c) then generate our own corpus with a small Scalus-owned Haskell dumper on the public Imp hooks `modifyImpInitPostSubmitTxHook` and `modifyImpInitPostEpochBoundaryHook` (`eras/shelley/impl/testlib/Test/Cardano/Ledger/Shelley/ImpTest.hs:127-130`), at the production tag 1.22.1.0. It runs the full Conway Imp suite at PV11 (`withEachEraVersion`), needs no Agda, keeps test names, and gives LEDGER and NEWEPOCH before and after states; (d) for rewards, use Amaru's PV11 mainnet fixtures (epochs 644 to 648) or Koios, through the it-data stake cache.
   - LEDGER part, checked 2026-11-03 (agent, phase 6 design): 7 446 cases, 6 918 distinct, 93 rejected (no `st_out`), every sampled case at PV 11.0. `env` is `[slot, [epoch], txIx, pparams, [treasury, reserves]]`, so a runner has the real slot, epoch, pparams and treasury. No file records the failure, so the cases give verdicts and states only (D99). Runner and sample: `[CV-21]` to `[CV-25a]`, D108.
